His Ashcroft opinion (1983) distinguishes genuine health regulation without significant impact from unsupported burdens. An independently forbidden obstacle and scrutiny of lesser burdens can provide minimum protection, but adequate fit under a less demanding formula cannot replace those requirements. He therefore does not join the operative abandonment of the trimester timetable.

Blackmun would invalidate the fixed waiting period and unnecessary exclusive physician delivery. Thornburgh (1986) protects voluntary clinical advice while rejecting compulsory ancillary persuasion. He accordingly would invalidate the three ancillary notices and their incorporation into parental consent. Section 3205(a)(2)(iii) says, “In the case of rape, this information may be omitted.” That permission carries no police-reporting condition, but does not resolve his objection to the mandatory notice. He accepts medically relevant information, including gestational age, lawful receipt certification, State publication and elective receipt of materials. District findings 181–184 identify directory defects; they do not establish that every published item is false. He opposes the proposed focused materials vacatur and interim distribution injunction.

He joins narrow First Amendment permission for actual clinical disclosures, reserving ancillary speech after due-process invalidation. He sustains the parental provision against a facial challenge premised on an assumed attendance command, subject to severance of the invalid ancillary content. The district's operational finding and the Third Circuit's unresolved construction in footnote 23 do not establish that the statute commands a clinic visit. He therefore opposes a special attendance remand and interim injunction, while reserving a properly presented actual enforced-visit challenge.

Blackmun joins invalidation of spousal notice and dependent notice-reporting fields, retaining confidential marital-status data. Actual coercion and interference with protected choice supply the ground; he does not add an independently sufficient equality, general-privacy or spousal compelled-speech holding. He would invalidate the referral-identity field and automatic public facility disclosures because the record shows burdens inadequately connected to their asserted benefits. Adequate confidentiality does not erase the two physicians' recorded refusal to refer.

He nevertheless sustains confidential medical-necessity, emergency and diagnostic-basis reports. Ashcroft recognizes the usefulness of recorded medical reasons; Thornburgh distinguishes useful confidential reporting from exposure and interference. These reports do not authorize advance approval, useless tests or punishment solely for later disagreement with reasonable clinical judgment. His public positions in Foucha and Riggins (May 18, 1992) reinforce attention to actual bodily interference and justification without supplying a universal proof rule. He accepts the protective emergency construction, reserving standalone vagueness and its disputed preservation. His remedial position remains provision-specific severance and ordinary implementation, without wholesale invalidation of the Act.

### Stevens — Separate opinion concurring in part and dissenting in part

**Joins and scope.** No complete joins; agreement with a particular result elsewhere is not a join in this complete separate writing.

Justice Stevens joins the constitutional foundation and the stated reasons for retaining it. His Thornburgh concurrence (1986) protects childbearing decisions against restriction supported only by majority disapproval; his Webster opinion (1989) requires attention to actual medical usefulness and burden. Hodgson (1990) places the justification for imposed obstacles on the State and distinguishes legitimate parental assistance from needless interference. Those sources support Stone's first five steps: identify the real obligation and affected population, examine purpose and cumulative effects, prohibit substantial obstacles to protected care, and require adequate justification and fit for meaningful lesser burdens.

This is Stevens's substantive proposed alternative, not agreement with every remedy or a generalized facial formula. He does not join Stone's sixth step as a comprehensive large-fraction rule. His Ohio v. Akron Center concurrence (1990), joining Parts I–IV but not Part V, supports careful facial analysis grounded in actual safeguards and demonstrated operation. Nor does he join bare elimination of staged protection when his meaningful-burden alternative has not become controlling law. Constitutional protection cannot depend on a replacement standard that appears only in a minority writing.

Stevens would invalidate the fixed adult waiting period and exclusive physician delivery. Hodgson's treatment of time for a minor's parental consultation does not establish a corresponding adult waiting rule. He nevertheless sustains accurate clinical information, including gestational age, and the three limited ancillary notices. The paternal-support provision permits omission in rape cases without requiring police reporting (§3205(a)(2)(iii)); optional materials need not be accepted, read or endorsed (§3205(a)(3)). Those actual limits distinguish useful information from compelled ideological agreement. Lawful receipt certification may survive severance of invalid requirements.

He joins narrow First Amendment permission for these factual disclosures; Riley (1988) prevents treating compensation as dispositive. He sustains publication and elective receipt while preserving concrete challenges to misinformation. District findings 181–184 concern actual directory defects; they do not warrant an automatic suspension of every challenged item. He rejects the proposed focused materials vacatur and interim distribution injunction. He also sustains the present informed-parent provision, including lawful medical and ancillary content, without inventing an enacted attendance demand. The district's operational finding and Third Circuit footnote 23 leave an actual visit construction unresolved. He therefore rejects the special attendance vacatur, remand and interim injunction.

Stevens joins spousal-notice invalidation on protected-choice grounds, removal of notice-compliance reporting, and preservation of confidential marital-status information. He would invalidate the referring-identity field because the recorded deterrence lacks sufficient justification. He sustains the three confidential medical-basis reports and the limited public facility reports; clinical documentation and fiscal accountability must be evaluated under their distinct record and statutory conditions. He adds no independently sufficient equality, general-privacy or spousal-speech holding, and reserves standalone emergency vagueness. His Riggins position (May 18, 1992) supports justified protection of bodily decisionmaking; his Quill opinion (May 26) supports examining separable precedents without treating every revision as abandonment of their foundation.

### Souter — Separate opinion concurring in part and dissenting in part

**Joins and scope.** No complete joins; agreement with a particular result elsewhere is not a join in this complete separate writing.

Justice Souter joins retention of protected choice, viability and necessary life-and-health care. His Payne concurrence (June 27, 1991) explains that disagreement with a constitutional precedent alone does not establish the special justification needed to overrule it. Concrete unworkability or internal tension can support a limited correction. The present grounds do not displace Roe's constitutional foundation. The Court's decisions in Hilton (December 16, 1991) and Quill (May 26, 1992) likewise require attention to the particular proposition and setting; their statutory and commercial considerations do not dictate the abortion result.

Souter accepts prohibition of an obstacle that gives another person effective control over protected care and rejects automatic immunity for every lesser burden. He does not adopt a complete replacement formula. Stone's proposal carefully distinguishes relevant populations, real causal burdens, clinical evidence and actual relief, but its specificity does not itself justify a universal new balancing rule, burden transfer or facial denominator. Souter's Riggins join (May 18, 1992) concerns forced medication; his Medina concurrence (June 22) resists automatic transfer of procedural methods across different constitutional questions. He therefore applies the relevant abortion precedents and the demonstrated facts rather than declaring a new comprehensive test. He also declines operative abandonment of staged protection without identifying the justification for each displaced rule.

Souter would invalidate the fixed waiting period and exclusive physician-delivery requirement because the grounds offered do not sufficiently justify departing from Akron's directly relevant protections. He sustains accurate clinical information, gestational age and the three concise ancillary notices. Section 3205(a)(2)(iii) permits paternal-support information to be omitted in rape cases; it does not require omission or police reporting. Materials remain optional under §3205(a)(3), and receipt certification cannot enforce an invalid prerequisite.

He joins narrow First Amendment permission for these factual disclosures, without universal professional-speech immunity or treating compensation as dispositive. He sustains lawful publication and elective receipt while preserving concrete challenges to misleading execution. The district's directory findings do not establish that every material is unlawful. He opposes the focused materials vacatur and interim distribution injunction. He also sustains the present informed-parent provision, including lawful medical and ancillary content, without assuming an enacted clinic-visit requirement. The operational finding and the unresolved construction identified in Third Circuit footnote 23 warrant reservation of an actual enforced-visit case, not the proposed special attendance vacatur and interim injunction.

Souter joins invalidation of spousal notice and notice-compliance fields while preserving confidential marital-status data. Protected choice and actual coercion provide complete relief, so independent equality, general-privacy and spousal-speech grounds remain undecided. He sustains confidential referral identification, acknowledging the two physicians' predicted withdrawal but finding insufficient demonstrated service obstruction for facial relief. He also sustains the three medical-basis reports and limited public facility disclosures under their distinct statutory conditions. The reports preserve actual clinical standards and do not create prior permission or hindsight control. He accepts the protective emergency construction while reserving standalone vagueness and its contested preservation.

### O'Connor — Separate concurrence

**Joins and scope.** No complete joins; agreement with a particular result elsewhere is not a join in this complete separate writing.

Justice O’Connor concurs separately to explain the regulatory standard she would retain and her additional grounds concerning spousal notice. She preserves the constitutional protection for previability choice while rejecting Roe’s rigid trimester machinery. Her Akron and Thornburgh dissents supply a two-stage inquiry: first determine whether the regulation creates an absolute obstacle or severely limits access, without weighing the State’s interests at that threshold; then, if that burden exists, require compelling justification. Ordinary burdens receive rational review. A severe burden is not automatically unconstitutional under every conceivable regulation, although an independently forbidden transfer of the patient’s decision to another person cannot be justified by invoking family consultation. Her Riggins opinion does not transplant its medication-specific justification requirements into every abortion regulation.

She therefore rejects a general rule placing an evidentiary burden on the State whenever any meaningful interference is shown. Professional evidence and workable alternatives matter, but the Constitution does not invariably require the least costly policy. She accepts accurate medical information, physician responsibility for its delivery and the fixed deliberation interval on this record, acknowledging the proven additional visits, expense and delay. The separate ancillary notices communicate facts rather than require ideological allegiance. Section 3205(a)(2)(iii) permits paternal-support information to be omitted in rape cases, without a reporting condition; legal liability does not guarantee payment. Chosen materials and receipt certification do not require reading or assent, and her rejection of present materials relief does not certify every pamphlet or listed agency as accurate.

Hodgson supports one-parent informed consent with an effective independent bypass based on maturity and actual informed consent or, alternatively, best interests. She joins the limited rejection of the attendance challenge and would also sustain the district’s assumed operation with that adequate alternative. She does not invent an express visit command or a parent-specific waiting clock. She sustains confidential referrer identification, the distinct medical-judgment reports and these public facility reports on their record-specific justifications. Reporting does not itself authorize punishment merely because an official later prefers a different lawful clinical judgment.

Spousal notice fails independently under the protected-choice rule. O’Connor also relies on Hogan’s requirement that a sex classification substantially serve an important governmental objective without resting on stereotyped roles. Biological difference does not justify giving a husband supervisory power over his wife’s medical decision. Her joined Hodgson reasoning additionally protects the narrow sphere of adult reproductive communication from this imposed control. Those are additional grounds, not a general constitutional right to secrecy or a claim that every pregnancy classification is invalid. She reaches no independent spousal compelled-speech or emergency-vagueness holding, and resolves no disputed waiver through silence. Her ordinary stare-decisis analysis protects the constitutional foundation while allowing a demonstrated defect in a particular regulatory rule to be corrected.

### White — Concurrence in part and dissent in part

**Joins and scope.** O’Connor joins only White’s isolated selection of intermediate scrutiny. Scalia and Thomas join except that tier selection; they assume the tier without deciding and join its application.

White concurs in part and dissents in part. Scalia and Thomas join except for White's conclusion that intermediate scrutiny governs the spousal-notice sex classification; they assume that standard without deciding its applicability and join the analysis under it. O’Connor joins only the isolated selection of that tier and rejects White’s sustaining application. The writing accepts the Court's precise permissions for physician delivery, the waiting interval, lawful information, specified clinical reports and public-funding disclosures at their stated scopes. It also joins removal of Roe's trimester timetable as a barrier to pursuing health and potential-life interests throughout pregnancy. These direct joins adopt the limited reasons given for those permissions, without recognizing a fundamental abortion right or supplying a comprehensive regulatory test.

The three Justices reject the retained constitutional foundation and the Court's reasons for preserving it. White's Roe and Thornburgh dissents and Scalia's Webster and Ohio v. Akron Center writings identify their principal objection: the Constitution does not establish the claimed priority of elective abortion over the State's protection of prenatal life. Thomas joins the source and precedent analysis expressed here; no earlier abortion opinion is attributed to him. They accept that precedent deserves reasoned consideration, but regard the source defect and continuing doctrinal difficulties as sufficient to reconsider Roe. Their position does not deny every substantive liberty or dispense with independent speech, equality and privacy guarantees.

They would sustain spousal notice because the father's prospective parental interest and marital participation support this notice requirement, while acknowledging the record's coercion and retaliation findings and the actual statutory limits. Under intermediate scrutiny, applied by White and assumed by Scalia and Thomas, they find the information requirement substantially related to an important actual objective. Caban supplies the standard, and White's Michael M. position reinforces attention to that relationship; biological differences alone do not justify legal subordination. Their privacy analysis addresses the particular disclosure to the spouse, not a general denial of intimate privacy. Riley likewise prevents treating all compensated medical expression as commercial or all truthful compelled speech as permissible.

They would also sustain the notice-compliance and nonnotice-reason reports on their primary premise that notice is valid. They cast no conditional vote for removing those fields after the Court invalidates notice. Their broader reporting position preserves confidentiality, distinct data fields and the different public-funding triggers; parental informed consent, assumed attendance and judicial bypass remain separate questions. The writing adopts no universal Salerno rule. It leaves the distinct spousal First Amendment merits and preservation question open for ordinary handling of any genuinely preserved claim, and does not turn the emergency health construction into a standalone vagueness holding.

## Chronology and later dependencies

**Chronological dependency result.** The correction is effective June 29, 1992 in case-list order: Casey, Lucas, Espinosa. Lucas and Espinosa share the independent same-day entering-law baseline; neither committed record relies on Casey. Their property/compensation and capital-sentencing holdings, remedies and published positions are unaffected. Benten v. Kessler follows on July 17. Its controlling denial rests on the missing entitlement to mandatory drug return despite an assumed procedural challenge, and expressly reserves a constitutional claim not presented by the applicants. Casey does not present that claim, displace Benten’s agency-law questions, or establish admission of RU-486. Stevens’s individual Benten justification-and-method dissent remains consistent with his published Casey position. No later committed event is materially affected and no separate-correction blocker is identified. The later records and Render Inputs remain operative and unmodified. The chronology cursor stays July 17, 1992, after Benten.

## Procedure after action

Supreme Court merits review concludes in both dockets. In No. 91-902, the invalidation of §3209 is affirmed, 6–3. In No. 91-744, the judgment is affirmed in part and reversed in part, and the cause is remanded for proceedings consistent with the component holdings. The appellate approval of notice-given and nonnotice-reason reporting is reversed; those questions and their dependent enforcement must be removed, while the independently operating confidential marital-status question is retained under Act 64’s severability clause. The remaining challenged requirements are sustained at their stated scopes. No focused materials or attendance vacatur, separate remand, or interim injunction is ordered. The Court does not enjoin the sustained fixed waiting or physician-delivery requirements. The lower courts must conform relief and forms to the invalid notice obligations, preserve lawful independently operating requirements and respect the stated statutory defenses and culpability terms. No wholesale invalidation, new prosecution, damages award, expedited mandate, rehearing outcome or invented deadline is ordered. A later actual unlawful application remains challengeable through the appropriate proceedings; that reservation is not a newly retained Supreme Court merits matter.

## Sources, provenance and research cutoff

**Research cutoff: June 29, 1992, immediately before the merits action.** Historical primary materials retrieved in September 2026 are evidence of date-eligible text and facts; their retrieval dates are not in-world events. The historical Supreme Court Casey decision is comparator evidence only. Lucas, Espinosa and Benten are read solely in the later dependency review, never to supply entering Casey law. Present-day abortion law and subsequent scholarly explanations do not enter this adjudication.

### Individual writings and governing pre-divergence law

The adaptive annex identifies the exact complete authored or joined writing read, and the proposition supporting every Justice/component row. Principal full-text source files include [Roe, 410 U.S. 113](https://static.case.law/us/410/cases/0113-01.json), [Doe, 410 U.S. 179](https://static.case.law/us/410/cases/0179-01.json), [Danforth, 428 U.S. 52](https://static.case.law/us/428/cases/0052-01.json), [Bellotti, 443 U.S. 622](https://static.case.law/us/443/cases/0622-01.json), [Akron, 462 U.S. 416](https://static.case.law/us/462/cases/0416-01.json), [Ashcroft, 462 U.S. 476](https://static.case.law/us/462/cases/0476-01.json), [Thornburgh, 476 U.S. 747](https://static.case.law/us/476/cases/0747-01.json), [Webster, 492 U.S. 490](https://static.case.law/us/492/cases/0490-01.json), [Hodgson, 497 U.S. 417](https://static.case.law/us/497/cases/0417-01.json), [Ohio v. Akron Center, 497 U.S. 502](https://static.case.law/us/497/cases/0502-01.json), and Souter's concurrence joined by Kennedy in [Payne, 501 U.S. 808](https://static.case.law/us/501/cases/0808-01.json). These case links are not a blanket certification that every separate writing in every linked multi-opinion case was read. The annex's writing-specific checks control, and reasons are not attributed to all Justices who agreed with a case's judgment.

The source checks separately included [Marks, 430 U.S. 188](https://static.case.law/us/430/cases/0188-01.json), for the date-eligible narrowest-grounds inquiry; [Riley, 487 U.S. 781](https://static.case.law/us/487/cases/0781-01.json), including the Court's opinion, which Scalia joined except footnote 11, and his concurrence; and the other speech, equality, privacy and severability authorities pinpointed in the annex. Additional complete own writings read include O'Connor's [Hogan opinion, 458 U.S. 718](https://static.case.law/us/458/cases/0718-01.json), her concurrence joined Kennedy in [Michael H., 491 U.S. 110, 132](https://static.case.law/us/491/cases/0110-01.json), and Kennedy's concurrence in [Texas v. Johnson, 491 U.S. 397, 420–421](https://static.case.law/us/491/cases/0397-01.json). No abortion writing is invented for Souter or Thomas. Their row-specific uncertainty concerns the extension of their general methods and actual earlier simulated positions to this record.

### Simulated public law and separate comparator

Earlier OT1991 public outputs supply only the holdings and joins actually published in this timeline. The attached entering-law map identifies the source file, writing, Justices and exact limitations for Foucha, Riggins, Lee, Medina, Collins, Hilton, Quill, McCollum, Hudson and Wright. Historical versions of those post-divergence decisions do not displace the simulated versions.

The runtime Casey comparator and [complete historical Casey opinions, 505 U.S. 833](https://static.case.law/us/505/cases/0833-01.json), are opened only after the new eight-Justice commitments freeze. Their authorship and joinder facts are confined to the historical reconciliation. Simulated authorship, assignments, votes and law are Engine inferences independently recorded in this decision; they are not historical facts.


## Adaptive audit annex — staged handoffs and final compatibility

True blindness was unavailable. The neutral packet and all eight provisional matrices were frozen anew before the full historical comparator was opened. Each of the five historical opinions was read in full by its assigned reader; all three reconciliation reports were reviewed before the full approved Stone version 1.2 was opened. Version 1 was opened only after that stage, for correction comparison. Version 1.1 was not used as a judicial position. The complete frozen packet, matrices, reconciliation and final join tables follow so the record does not depend on scratch files for their legal content. Historical comparator facts below are audit material, never entering law or public opinion assignments.

**Section 8 conclusion.** No substantive non-Stone commitment changed after reconciliation. The accepted refinements state narrower common grounds already supported by the frozen rows: particular permissions without an unadopted general rights premise; actual reporting predicates rather than a new constitutional immunity; the supplied bypass as a safeguard without attributing its constitutional necessity to dissenters; and an isolated equality-tier join distinct from its disputed application. The sanitized audit remained unexposed to the full Stone supplement. No universal framework, conditional notice-report remedial vote, new finding, historical authorship or fictional draft exchange was imported. The detailed tests below record every Justice’s joins/refusals, objections and minimum revisions; they are final compatibility determinations, not invented conversations.

### Stone — every final opinion part

| Actual part | Join/refusal and exact ground, objection or minimum revision |
|---|---|
| The framework retaining protected choice | Direct join / author. Section II A retains Roe’s constitutional foundation, protected previability decision, and viability/life-and-health boundaries; Section II C replaces the machinery without abandoning that foundation. Joins this common ground, which does not adopt his entire replacement. No objection or revision. |
| Stare decisis and the constitutional foundation | Direct join / author. Section II A preserves the constitutional foundation, protects reliance on reproductive decisions and lawful care, and explains the distinct reason for revising regulatory implementation. The common opinion’s ordinary precedent analysis is compatible at that scope; this does not attribute a separately enumerated workability test or special legitimacy rule to Stone’s text. No objection or revision. |
| Medical emergency and timely clinical judgment | Direct join / author. Section II D.1 accepts the prospective protective construction: no wait for catastrophe or loss of sound clinical judgment. Joins construction and relief, while the standalone vagueness ground remains unaddressed under the separately named fallback. No revision. |
| Accurate material medical information, including gestational age | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 preserves accurate information material to informed clinical consent, including gestational age, independently of the physician-exclusive delivery and fixed-delay commands he rejects. No objection to this isolated content permission; no revision. |
| Notice that free state information is available | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 permits concise accurate notice that lawful free materials are available, separated from compulsory receipt or approval of a particular disputed publication. No objection or revision at this scope. |
| Notice of potentially available medical assistance | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 permits accurate notice that medical assistance may be available; it makes no guarantee of eligibility, benefits or payment. No objection or revision. |
| Paternal-support information and the rape omission permission | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 permits accurate legal-support information, with the statutory permission to omit it in rape and no police-report condition. This is not a guarantee of collection. No objection or revision. |
| Lawful publication and the patient’s choice to receive materials | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 permits lawful State publication and the patient’s voluntary choice to receive it; his separate focused-remedy request concerns the actual disputed materials. No objection or revision to this limited permission. |
| The requested focused proceedings concerning particular materials | No complete join. Refuses under Section II D.3–4 and E. He expressly seeks focused vacatur/remand and interim protection. Minimum revision would grant those particular proceedings and protection, not merely reserve a future application. |
| Accurate information supporting informed parental consent | Direct join / author. Section II D.4 permits accurate information supporting lawful parental involvement at the lower construction’s actual scope; this does not import every adult timing or delivery demand or approve an additional visit. No objection or revision. |
| One-parent informed consent and independent judicial authorization | Direct join / author. Section II D.4 preserves ordinary informed parental consent only with a prompt, confidential, independent and practically usable judicial alternative, including authorization on maturity/informed consent or best interests. No objection or revision to this safeguarded architecture. |
| The separate parental-attendance objection | No complete join. Refuses under Section II D.3–4 and E. He expressly seeks focused vacatur/remand and interim protection. Minimum revision would grant those particular proceedings and protection, not merely reserve a future application. |
| Spousal notice and State-enabled control of protected choice | Direct join / author. Section II D.5 independently rejects State-enabled spousal control, taking the recorded risks and limited substitutes in their actual terms. He joins the common liberty ground without adding his entire general framework or an independently sufficient equality holding. No objection or revision. |
| Confidential identification of referring physicians, agencies and services | No complete join. Refuses under Section II D.6–7: actual disclosure/referral burdens lack adequate benefit and fit. Minimum revision would remove the blanket demand while preserving lawful confidential collection, investigation and tailored accounting. |
| Confidential basis for a medical-necessity judgment | Direct join / author. Section II D.8 sustains limited confidential documentation of the relevant medical judgment, with sound judgment and culpability protections. The part preserves the actual 24-or-more-weeks necessity trigger and creates no prior permission system. No objection or revision. |
| Confidential basis for an emergency judgment | Direct join / author. Section II D.8 permits later confidential documentation without postponing care, shrinking the prospective emergency construction or criminalizing professionally defensible judgment merely because of later disagreement. No objection or revision. |
| Confidential account of gestational-age determination | Direct join / author. Section II D.8 permits the limited confidential account of actual assessment and its basis, without a newly prescribed test, speculative certainty or prior authorization. No objection or revision. |
| Notice-compliance reporting and the severable marital-status field | Partial join. Partial formal join: notice-given/nonnotice-reason invalidation under D.5 and E. Retention of independently severable marital-status data uses the approved silent-component fallback and adds no ground. No narrowing of his notice-invalidity position occurs. |
| Written certification of lawful receipt | Direct join / author. Section II D.2–3 permits documentation of lawful receipt, with medical and ancillary content separated from the timing/delivery obligations he rejects. No objection or revision to the ordinary receipt part; refusal of the latter links remains explicit in the judgment and authority entries. |
| The independent speech objection to medical-consent information | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 treats accurate material clinical information as legitimate informed consent. He joins only the narrow independent speech permission, without a general license to prescribe a clinician’s viewpoint. No objection or revision. |
| The independent speech objection to concise ancillary notices | Direct join / author. Section II D.3 expressly permits the concise accurate ancillary notices; the independent speech part preserves their factual, qualified and noncompulsory-receipt scope. No objection or revision; this is not a silence-based substitute for his materials objections. |
| Physician delivery of the specified medical information | No complete join. Refuses the sustained requirement under Section II D.2–3 and E: demonstrated added burden lacks adequate justification. Minimum compatible material revision would invalidate the unjustified delivery/waiting obligation and its dependent enforcement; it cannot be supplied by changing Stone’s vote. |
| The fixed twenty-four-hour interval | No complete join. Refuses the sustained requirement under Section II D.2–3 and E: demonstrated added burden lacks adequate justification. Minimum compatible material revision would invalidate the unjustified delivery/waiting obligation and its dependent enforcement; it cannot be supplied by changing Stone’s vote. |
| Public access to facility and affiliation reports | No complete join. Refuses under Section II D.6–7: actual disclosure/referral burdens lack adequate benefit and fit. Minimum revision would remove the blanket demand while preserving lawful confidential collection, investigation and tailored accounting. |
| Public access to quarterly and trimester totals | No complete join. Refuses under Section II D.6–7: actual disclosure/referral burdens lack adequate benefit and fit. Minimum revision would remove the blanket demand while preserving lawful confidential collection, investigation and tailored accounting. |
| The trimester timetable and the timing of regulation | Direct join / author. Joins the isolated timing change under Section II A and C, while preserving the constitutional foundation and proposing his own replacement. No revision needed; this join does not adopt another general test. |
| Stone: proposed substantive framework, Steps 1–5 | Direct join / author. Authors Section II C Steps 1–5, including the independent protected-care obstacle prohibition, meaningful lesser-burden justification and fit, allocation of proof and stated evidentiary, clinical and causation limits. No revision may replace these approved propositions. Stevens’s scoped join does not expand them. |
| Stone: Step 6 generalized facial-relief qualification | Direct join / author. Authors Section II C Step 6: prohibited statutory terms or reliably demonstrated unlawful operation in a large fraction of the independently defined restricted group, with actual prevalence and insufficiency of narrower relief explained and no numerical cutoff. No revision; Stone alone adopts the full generalized proposition. |
| Stone: clinical-evidence and enforcement protections | Direct join / author. Authors Section II B–C’s clinical-evidence protections: substantial weight, not a veto, for professional consensus; competent contrary evidence; professionally defensible judgment protected from mere later disagreement, with fraud, malpractice and misconduct outside that protection. The four-Justice part does not attribute his universal State-justification shift to Souter. No change to Stone’s broader separately stated proposal. |
| Stone: delay and physician-exclusivity applications | Direct join / author. Authors the invalidating applications in Section II D.2–3 and E: meaningful added burdens are inadequately justified; remove the unlawful exclusivity and fixed delay with dependent enforcement. No revision. Souter’s same judgment on Akron-adherence grounds is not a full join. |
| Stone: blanket referral-identity objection | Direct join / author. Authors Section II D.7’s objection to blanket referrer identification on the actual deterrence record and inadequate incremental justification, preserving lawful confidential reports and targeted investigation. No revision; the premise is not disclosure of patient names to the public. |
| Stone: automatic public-release objection | Direct join / author. Authors Section II D.6’s objection to automatic public release under the two distinct funding lookbacks, with confidential collection and adequately tailored public accounting preserved. No revision; these are actual disclosure burdens and fit, not categorical immunity from accountability. |
| Stone: particular-materials and parental-attendance vacaturs, remands and interim injunctions | Direct join / author. Authors the focused materials and parental-attendance vacaturs, remands and interim protections in Section II D.3–4 and E, with waiting-period invalidation remaining effective. No revision; he does not find a statutory visit command or direct wholesale invalidation. |
| Blackmun: complete separate opinion | No complete join. Refuses the complete stronger staged-scrutiny and categorical-ancillary position: Section II A/C replaces the machinery and D.3 preserves concise accurate ancillary notices. Minimum compatible revision would remove the continued timetable and categorical ancillary prohibitions, leaving the separately joined minimum protections and applications; a shared invalidating judgment does not itself achieve that revision. |
| Stevens: complete separate opinion | No complete join. Does not join the complete separate writing: he independently votes for the timetable change and opposes public-release provisions Stevens sustains. Minimum compatible revision would separate those opposed grounds and outcomes from the first-five-step and application propositions they actually join. |
| Souter: complete separate opinion | No complete join. Does not join Souter’s refusal to adopt the proposed complete framework or his sustaining referral/public-disclosure applications. Minimum compatible revision would confine their common writing to the retained core and specifically compatible applications, reserving Souter’s distinct precedent route and removing his contrary disclosure permissions; no change to Stone’s approved framework follows. |
| O’Connor: separate severity-first framework and independent spousal grounds | No complete join. Refuses the complete severity-first/compelling-justification formula and the independently sufficient equality/privacy additions. Section II C expressly rejects a threshold-only permission for lesser burdens. A compatible complete formulation would need the independent protected-care obstacle bar and meaningful-burden justification inquiry, with independent equality/privacy left unresolved; those are substantive changes her frozen commitments do not accept. Only the separately joined common core and actual notice relief remain available. |
| White: abortion foundation, stare decisis, spousal-notice and privacy analysis, report position and scope reservations | No complete join. Refuses the complete opposing foundation/notice rationale under Section II A–C and D.5. Shared permissions elsewhere do not make these rejected grounds compatible; substantive reversal of the proposed ground would be required. |
| White: intermediate scrutiny is the governing sex-classification tier | No complete join. Does not reach an independently sufficient equality ruling, as Section II A expressly states. No tier or merits rejection is attributed. Minimum treatment is to reserve this independent ground rather than replace his stated scope. |
| White: spousal equality application under intermediate scrutiny | No complete join. Does not reach an independently sufficient equality ruling, as Section II A expressly states. No tier or merits rejection is attributed. Minimum treatment is to reserve this independent ground rather than replace his stated scope. |
