### Preserved audit material — neutral packet at global freeze

# Neutral Modeling Packet — Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey

**Nos. 91-744 and 91-902; merits and cross-review; effective June 29, 1992.**

**Packet status:** Neutral source facts and framing frozen September 15, 2026 after the checks specified below. No Justice commitments are frozen by this document. Individual date-eligible Supreme Court writings remain to be read in full during the modeling stage before those commitments are frozen. A material new source fact or corrected legal premise must return through neutral refresh and, if needed, affected remodeling/refreeze. No unperformed writing check is reported as complete.

**Mode disclosure:** Single-conversation fallback with scoped files and staged handoffs. A new neutral-agent context could not be created because the tool returned 'agent thread limit reached'; this reused context previously reviewed state continuity and earlier public positions. True blindness was unavailable. It has not opened Stone's present supplement, the historical Casey comparator/full Supreme Court Casey opinions, the earlier simulated Casey record, or earlier Casey commitments. Primary-source retrieval agents disclosed incidental historical search/metadata exposures, but the content of those exposures was excluded from this handoff. This packet does not contain the historical Casey Supreme Court resolution or lineup. The user's conversation itself prevents any claim of true blindness.

## Event, channel, participation and chronology

Five clinics and one physician challenge specified 1988/1989 amendments to Pennsylvania's Abortion Control Act in a facial pre-enforcement action for declaration and injunction. The district court, 744 F.Supp.1323 (E.D.Pa.1990), after trial during the week of July30,1990, invalidated the emergency definition and numerous connected provisions, informed-consent provisions, parental informed consent, spousal notification, selected confidential-report items and public release of facility reports. Its independent rulings sustained other confidential reporting and ordinary prudent-physician gestational-age ascertainment. The providers did not cross-appeal the district-upheld provisions in the Third Circuit.

The Third Circuit, 947 F.2d682, No.90-1662, October21,1991, affirmed invalidation of spousal notice and reversed the other appealed invalidations. Providers seek broader relief in No.91-744; officials seek restoration of spousal notice in No.91-902. Review is by certiorari under 28 U.S.C.§1254(1). Party briefs verify January21,1992 grants; this source chronology is not a newly simulated independent grant event. Argument occurred April22,1992. Inventory fixes June29,1992 merits action. No judgment is determined here.

The questions cover governing abortion protection and reconsideration of Roe; medical emergency; medical disclosures, mandated ancillary notices, optional printed materials, delivery and delay/certification; informed parental consent with bypass; spousal notice; and the appealed reporting/public-release components. Independent First Amendment information claims and notice-related marital/informational-privacy and equal-protection theories are briefed. General alternative constitutional foundations for abortion protection are the subject of a contested conditional remand request if Roe protection is abandoned. A general funding entitlement, constitutional fetal personhood, independent validity of the sex-selection prohibition, every postviability limit, and the district-upheld unappealed reporting rules are not newly presented for full merits adjudication.

All nine Justices are seated at argument and decision: Chief Justice Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. No recusal or nonparticipation is established. Thomas took his oath October23,1991. Six is quorum; five controls judgment. Chief assigns on a component where he belongs to its judgment majority; otherwise the most senior participating Associate in that coalition assigns, in the listed Associate order. Authorship and opinion joinders remain undetermined.

The entering-law cutoff is immediately before June29 Casey. June26 events are date-eligible; June29 presentation order is Casey, Lucas, Espinosa. Uncoordinated same-day events share the common entering baseline. Lucas, Espinosa and July17 Benten are excluded from entering-law/modeling sources. A post-assembly chronological dependency review is separately required; no later-event substantive law is placed here.

## Source classification and completed checks

**Statutory text** is quoted below from the user-supplied statutory-only appendix and separately identified lower-court quotations. **District findings** state what the trial court found on its evidence, with material factual disputes retained. **Lower-court constructions/judgments** are appellate/district interpretations and dispositions under review, not new Supreme Court law. **Party/SG positions and argument questions** are advocacy or questions, never concessions, facts, votes or commitments unless the actual source warrants that characterization. Earlier simulated law enters solely through sanitized public sources.

Completed full-source reads: district opinion including findings, orders and appended notes by the source agent (`district_source_report.md`; any clipped initial paragraphs were recovered); Third Circuit majority/dissent and all footnotes by this validator (`third-circuit-source-report.md`; all176,735characters accounted for); providers' opening pages1–62 and reply pages1–25 plus AppendixA by root (`providers-source-report.md`); officials pages1–118, SG pages1–29, providers' supplemental plus full Guam appendix, and official April22 argument by source agent (`briefs/neutral-source-facts.md`). IA PDF identity checked against newly retrieved metadata for all five briefs. These reports contain fact provenance and exact page references. They are source maps, not a historical Supreme Court comparator.

## Material facts, constructions and qualifications by issue

### Emergency and the separate information defense

**Text:** §3203 protects immediate abortion to avert death or a condition where delay creates serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function, assessed by the physician's good-faith clinical judgment. It applies to the relevant exemptions in §§3205,3206,3209,3210,3211. **District findings/construction:** immediate clinical treatment can be necessary in preeclampsia, inevitable abortion and premature membrane rupture before the condition reaches what the district read as the statute's threshold. **Third Circuit construction:** serious risk is prospective; all three diagnoses qualify at onset without waiting for shock/infection/permanent damage; the legislature sought to prevent compliance from posing significant threat to life or health. It distinguishes negligible risk and significant risk of only transient problems. The Commonwealth agrees those three conditions must permit prompt care. The construction is federal, not a Pennsylvania state-court holding; providers dispute its breadth and enforceability. Officials and SG defend it; SG acknowledges the attorney general's view is not binding on state courts.

**Distinct text:** §3205(b) emergency explanation is required before abortion only if possible. The penalty paragraph §3205(c) protects omitted information if physician proves by a preponderance a reasonable belief that furnishing it would severely adversely affect physical or mental health. This is permission/protection concerning disclosures, not an instruction to defer emergency care, not a general waiting exception, and not a substitute for §3203. The supplied appendix's '(e)' penalty label is OCR; both lower courts cite/quote (c). Providers acknowledge the defense but argue it omits less severe harm. District did not reach vagueness; Third Circuit rejected it based on good-faith clinical judgment. Officials' Supreme Court waiver contention is disputed/limited by providers' actual brief; do not infer a required separate vagueness holding.

### Medical information, ancillary notices, materials, delivery, waiting and certification

**Text:** physician orally gives procedure, material risks/alternatives, probable gestational age and childbirth medical risks at least24hours before procedure; performing/referring physician or expressly delegated qualified personnel may give (a)(2)'s notices about optional free state materials, possible assistance, and paternal support at least24hours before procedure. Paternal-support information may be omitted in rape cases, without a police-report condition. This does not alter substantive support obligations. Printed materials must be given only if chosen; acceptance/reading are optional. Certification (a)(4) establishes receipt of required information, not mandatory agreement with it. Statutory §3208 demands objective, nonjudgmental scientific information; its text does not itself establish that every publication or listed agency complies.

**District findings181–189:** directory omits some agencies, especially in Monroe/Allegheny Counties, and lists some agencies providing misinformation/inflammatory literature increasing anxiety. Providers can recommend avoiding a listed agency, recommend another, and the department can supplement omissions. These facts do not establish all state materials false. Findings185–189 distinguish medically informed consent, patient-specific dialogue and face-to-face observation from simple consent; printed/taped information alone is inadequate in the record's practice. Findings204–213 establish qualified counselor capability and actual physician contact before procedures, plus physician-scheduling costs. Findings190–203 identify second trips, delays often48hours–two weeks due to schedules, added travel/lodging/lost wages/child care, demonstrator exposure and later-procedure risk, weighing especially on poor, young, rural, battered and working patients. The district's finding of no medical need for mandatory delay is not a finding that every separately asserted deliberation/potential-life interest is illegitimate.

**Third Circuit:** acknowledges cost/delay/second visits, but finds no sufficiently severe access restriction under its threshold test; regards medical information as already routinely given, physician delivery rational, ancillary notices accurate/relevant and materials optional, and waiting a rational deliberation measure. It rejects a speech challenge using commercial disclosure. **Disputed premise:** providers' reply p.21n.39 and AppendixA expressly dispute a commercial-speech concession. They maintain a distinct professional-speech/compelled-message claim under Riley/Wooley/Barnette; State and SG invoke Zauderer and accurate relevant disclosure. Providers attack content, physician-only delivery, fixed delay and mandatory certifications separately. Do not collapse opposition to imposed persuasion into opposition to ordinary informed consent.

### Parental informed consent and bypass

**Text:** applies to unemancipated minors under18; guardian rule for adjudged incompetents also appears, but no independent developed challenge for that adult class is supplied. Parent/guardian considers only minor's/ward's best interests. One parent generally suffices, mother alone where father participates in incest; alternatives address death/unavailability, custodial parent after divorce, and adult standing in place of parent. Medical emergency excuses. Minor may elect court directly without seeking parent first. Maturity/capacity plus actual informed consent is one authorization route; best interests is an alternative. Counsel, confidentiality and expedited rulings accompany the route; exact supplementary quotations below.

**District finding232 and findings238–259:** no explicit statutory clinic-visit command; clinics intend in-person attendance under medical-consent practice/liability concerns and will not obtain parental informed consent by phone. A parent may agree but be unable to attend for days/weeks or refuse attendance; extra expense/logistics and a redundant judicial encounter can result. The court treats attendance as an operation of informed consent and invalidates the section on those and incorporated content/emergency grounds. **Third Circuit fn23:** expressly does not decide whether the attendance interpretation is correct, holding sufficient bypass would cure even that premise. Its legal treatment of bypass is disputed. The trial has no evidence of current unprepared bypass courts (finding266); do not import the earlier1988 temporary-restraint implementation concern as a1990finding. Providers do not attack the internal bypass timetable/criteria independently; their objection is the added informed-parent requirement despite willing consent. Government invokes parental guidance and bypass. A statutory construction avoiding unnecessary attendance and the possibility of additional lower-court consideration are legal paths, not already adopted commands.

### Spousal notice and each exception

**Text:** married patient provides a signed, nonnotarized statement that she notified spouse; warning about false statements required. Signed nonnotarized exception statement substitutes if spouse is not father; could not be located despite diligent effort; pregnancy results from §3128 spousal assault reported to law enforcement with jurisdiction; or she has reason to believe notice likely will cause bodily injury to her by spouse or another. Medical emergency independently excuses notice. The assault route incorporates the90-day reporting restriction described in district finding297, distinct from rape-counseling permission. Threats of psychological harm, financial withdrawal, divorce/custody retaliation, publicity, injury to children/others and other family harm are not generalized statutory exceptions. Likely bodily injury is not rewritten as any form of coercion.

**Findings273–308 and Third Circuit:** most wives voluntarily consult spouses. For some who do not, coerced disclosure risks prevention of abortion, physical/economic/psychological retaliation and exposure of intimate information. Some battered women cannot practically certify danger despite objective grounds; underreported assault and reporting-related risks undermine the assault route. These are trial findings/legal applications, not findings all married women are battered. Third Circuit judges disagree on denominator and required proof: majority measures burdens on women compelled to notify, not the entire unaffected population; dissent demands a broader quantified practical effect, treats exceptions as reducing the affected group, and distinguishes notice from formal consent. Both recognize a legitimate interest in spousal participation; majority rejects sufficient justification for its found severe burdens. Providers additionally argue marital/informational privacy and equal protection; officials/SG contest with distinct familial/pregnancy reasoning. A complete ruling must distinguish preserving abortion decision authority from adjudicating every broader equality/privacy theory.

### Confidential reports and public facility reports

**Text/source:** §3214(a) procedure reports exclude patient names; different items require performing/referring identities; gestational-age result; specific medical-necessity rationale for §3211(b)(1); emergency rationale; §3210(a) diagnostic inquiries/exams/tests/basis; and marital/notice/reason information. The scope of each is separately quoted. §3207(b)'s facility/affiliation public-access lookback is12calendar months before inspection/copying request; §3214(f)'s quarterly procedure/trimester totals lookback is12calendar months before report filing. Nofunding representation receives confidentiality unless contrary evidence establishes funding. Public-release challenge does not contest filing with the agency.

**District findings324–387 and judgment:** confidentiality security adequate; reports not public records; law-enforcement disclosure requires good-cause common-pleas order; statistical reporting must not identify physician/facility/patient. Some referring doctors would cease referrals even absent leak risk (two specifically described); performing-physician reporting sustained and no demonstrated general inability to meet demand. District finds referral identification medically unnecessary/chilling; State counters referring doctors can perform consent/gestational assessment and answer follow-up. District rejects medical-judgment basis reports (a)(8),(10),(11) as interfering with judgment; sustained gestational-age result and most other information must remain distinct. Report administration cost about$12,000year for a clinic performing6,500–7,000abortions; cost alone found insufficient. It sustained complication and other requested demographic reporting notwithstanding data imperfections; these unappealed matters are not silently reopened. Marital/notice/reason fields require dependency analysis, not automatic invalidation of every marital-status inquiry.

**Public release:** district found reasonable increased-harassment fears and likely funding refusal harming access by indigent qualifying patients; State funding might support other services/building costs, not only abortions. Third Circuit accepts existing harassment but finds insufficient incremental disclosure nexus for its severe-burden threshold and relies on transparency concerning public expenditures. It rejects unconstitutional-conditions theory on its own framework. Neither view warrants a new finding that individual patients' identities become public. A remedy invalidating public release alone must retain unchallenged agency collection.

## Law entering, source hierarchy and lawful alternatives

Term-opening Holdings and Standards/Tests are empty of simulated rules; all are editionSeptember14,2026, baselineOctober7,1991, last completed termNone. Earlier simulated public law remains applicable at its exact scope (separate appended map). No prior simulated abortion replacement enters. Pre-divergence Roe/Doe and subsequent abortion precedents remain available unless a governing proposition was actually displaced. A lower court's attempted interpretation of Webster/Hodgson through Marks is a question here, not a binding new Supreme Court standard. Full individual writings must be checked before commitments.

| Issue | Lawful paths supported by neutral sources | Strongest counterargument and boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Constitutional protection and implementation | Retain Roe's protected choice and strict-scrutiny/trimester rules; preserve protected previability choice while revising implementation; accept ordinary rational-basis review and reconsider Roe as officials alternatively/SG principally urge. | Providers invoke liberty, repeated reaffirmance and reliance; State/SG invoke text/history, administrability and potential-life interests. Any revision must identify actual majority propositions and cannot be inferred from shared results or a current advocate's label. |
| Stare decisis | Retain distinct constitutional foundation and assess whether subsidiary machinery can change; retain all governing precedent; or overrule on demonstrated decisional grounds. | Constitutional reliance differs from Hilton's statutory or Quill's congressionally adjustable Commerce context. General doubts, turnover, or historical eventual outcome supply no position. |
| Emergency | Sustain text with prospective health-saving construction; invalidate insufficient protection or limit enforcement where compliance threatens life/health; remand if a legally essential construction cannot properly be established. | Broad health construction has federal/state bindingness limits; narrower reading may defeat timely treatment. A disclosure defense cannot cure delay. Vagueness scope must be respected. |
| Medical information and delivery | Sustain accurate material medical disclosures; separately sustain or invalidate physician-only delivery under the evidence and governing standard. | Material content does not automatically justify who must speak24hours earlier; qualified counselor evidence and costs must be confronted. Ordinary physician contact at procedure is not the same command. |
| Ancillary disclosures/materials/speech | Sustain optional-material notice and accurate assistance/support disclosures with express rape/disclosure-health limits; invalidate compulsory persuasion or defective fit on supported due-process/speech grounds; narrower severed construction if legally available. | Optional materials still have mandatory notices; some agency defects do not prove universal falsity; commercial-speech classification disputed. Permission to omit in rape is not obligation and lacks police-report condition. |
| Fixed waiting/certification | Sustain deliberation interval with emergency; invalidate fixed delay on burdens/fit or precedent; treat receipt certification separately, severing only invalid links. | General delay/medical risk findings differ from each woman's experience; benefits claimed are not solely medical; a State may not substitute stereotype for supported justification where governing law demands one. |
| Parental consent/bypass | Sustain one-parent informed consent with adequate bypass; reject excess attendance operation; preserve ordinary one-parent consent while resolving/remanding added visit/content operation if authorized by state law. | Text has no explicit visit, providers' intended practice is evidence, and fn23 reserves interpretation. An adequate bypass and willing-parent case are analytically distinct; do not invent a bypass defect. |
| Spousal notice | Sustain notice with full exceptions based on legitimate spousal participation and required facial proof; invalidate because actual compelled-disclosure coercion blocks protected choice; reach preserved equality/privacy/speech only if legally needed and supported. | Formal notice differs from veto, but legal condition plus predictable private coercion may matter. Denominator, exceptions' reach and evidentiary findings must be explicit. |
| Referrals and medical-judgment reporting | Sustain each challenged confidential item on data/enforcement fit; invalidate specific chill/irrelevance/overreach; retain ordinary confidential report collection with severed items as state law permits. | Confidentiality does not erase actual referral findings; deterrence of unlawful conduct differs from chilling lawful judgment; (a)(8),(10),(11) are different predicates, not one generic medical report. |
| Public release and notice-linked report | Sustain transparency condition or invalidate public release while retaining unchallenged collection; independently determine effect of notice disposition on (a)(12). | Two funding lookbacks differ; actual harassment/incremental causation disputed; notice-related reasons are not all marital-status research. |
| Facial scope and severability | Apply governing abortion/facial precedent and decide challenged categorical applications with relief narrowed by severability; deny overbroad facial relief while preserving proper later application challenges where law permits. | No unquantified 'some women' or 'most women' substitute for the controlling legal population rule; no automatic all-or-nothing Act disposition. |

Baseline authorities and individual source assignments: Roe,410U.S.113; Doe,410U.S.179; Danforth,428U.S.52; Bellotti,443U.S.622; Akron,462U.S.416; Ashcroft,462U.S.476; Thornburgh,476U.S.747; Webster,492U.S.490; Hodgson,497U.S.417; Ohio v.Akron Center,497U.S.502; Maher,432U.S.464; Harris,448U.S.297; Rust,500U.S.173; Marks,430U.S.188; Brockett,472U.S.491. Relevant speech sources include Zauderer,471U.S.626; Riley,487U.S.781; Wooley,430U.S.705; Barnette,319U.S.624. These are source pointers to be read at their exact levels; citations alone do not establish a holding or a Justice's join. Both appellate parties and SG identify the relevant doctrinal disputes. No later Casey language is an entering-law rule.

## Provenance for exact statutory terms below

The next appendix embeds `terms/OT1991/briefs/OT_1991CHUNK14_CASEY_STATUTE.md` byte-for-byte inside a fenced quotation. It is statutory-only, not the full Supreme Court case. Preserve OCR '(e) Penalty' as printed while identifying it in analysis as §3205(c), verified by Third Circuit's quoted '(c) PENALTY' and district citations. Likewise §3208 Free distribution '(e)' in supplied OCR corresponds to (c), expressly cited by district finding177. Other evident spellings such as 'offieership' and '12-ealendar-month' are source OCR, not new statutory alternatives. No source file is changed.

Supplementary quotations follow for incorporated §3210(a), material bypass safeguards and §3128 context. Quotations labeled 'as quoted by lower court' reproduce that court's statutory quotations; a source finding concerning90days is labeled finding and not passed off as full verbatim §3128(c). Neither §3128 as a whole nor unquoted portions of the bypass machinery are independently challenged; no absent operative qualification is filled by inference.


## Statutory appendix — supplied source quoted in full

```text
# 18 Pa. Cons. Stat. §§3203–3214 (1990), selected provisions — source text for Planned Parenthood v. Casey

Source: the Pennsylvania Abortion Control Act of 1982 as amended in 1988 and 1989, as reprinted in the statutory appendix at 505 U.S. 902–911, retrieved September 15, 2026 from the Caselaw Access Project (static.case.law, us/505/cases/0833-01.json). Neutral-preflight rule: this file is statutory text only. The appendix heading naming the historical authors has been removed, and nothing below states any Court’s resolution of the case. The text is verbatim from that source, including its quotation marks.

---

Selected Provisions of the 1988 and 1989 Amendments to the Pennsylvania Abortion Control Act of 1982
18 PA. CONS. STAT. (1990).
“§3203. Definitions.
“ ‘Medical emergency.’ That condition which, on the basis of the physician’s good faith clinical judgment, so complicates the medical condition of a pregnant woman as to necessitate the immediate abortion of her pregnancy to avert her death or for which a delay will create serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of major bodily function.”
“§3205. Informed consent.
“(a) General rule. — No abortion shall be performed or induced except with the voluntary and informed consent of the woman upon whom the abortion is to be performed or induced. Except in the case of a medical emergency, consent to an abortion is voluntary and informed if and only if:
“(1) At least 24 hours prior to the abortion, the physician who is to perform the abortion or the referring physician has orally informed the woman of:
“(i) The nature of the proposed procedure or treatment and of those risks and alternatives to the procedure or treatment that a reasonable patient would consider material to the decision of whether or not to undergo the abortion.
“(ii) The probable gestational age of the unborn child at the time the abortion is to be performed.
“(iii) The medical risks associated with carrying her child to term.
“(2) At least 24 hours prior to the abortion, the physician who is to perform the abortion or the referring physician, or a qualified physician assistant, health care practitioner, technician or social worker to whom the responsibility has been delegated by either physician, has informed the pregnant woman that:
“(i) The department publishes printed materials which describe the unborn child and list agencies which offer alternatives to abortion and that she has a right to review the printed materials and that a copy will be provided to her free of charge if she chooses to review it.
“(ii) Medical assistance benefits may be available for prenatal care, childbirth and neonatal care, and that more detailed information on the availability of such assistance is contained in the printed materials published by the department.
“(iii) The father of the unborn child is liable to assist in the support of her child, even in instances where he has offered to pay for the abortion. In the case of rape, this information may be omitted.
“(3) A copy of the printed materials has been provided to the woman if she chooses to view these materials.
“(4) The pregnant woman certifies in writing, prior to the abortion, that the information required to be provided under paragraphs (1), (2) and (3) has been provided.
“(b) Emergency. — Where a medical emergency compels the performance of an abortion, the physician shall inform the woman, prior to the abortion if possible, of the medical indications supporting his judgment that an abortion is necessary to avert her death or to avert substantial and irreversible impairment of major bodily function.
“(e) Penalty. — Any physician who violates the provisions of this section is guilty of ‘unprofessional conduct’ and his license for the practice of medicine and surgery shall be subject to suspension or revocation in accordance with procedures provided under the act of October 5,1978 (P. L. 1109, No. 261), known as the Osteopathic Medical Practice Act, the act of December 20, 1985 (P. L. 457, No. 112), known as the Medical Practice Act of 1985, or their successor acts. Any physician who performs or induces an abortion without first obtaining the certification required by subsection (a)(4) or with knowledge or reason to know that the informed consent of the woman has not been obtained shall for the first offense be guilty of a summary offense and for each subsequent offense be guilty of a misdemeanor of the third degree. No physician shall be guilty of violating this section for failure to furnish the information required by subsection (a) if he or she can demonstrate, by a preponderance of the evidence, that he or she reasonably believed that furnishing the information would have resulted in a severely adverse effect on the physical or mental health of the patient.
“(d) Limitation on civil liability. — Any physician who complies with the provisions of this section may not be held civilly liable to his patient for failure to obtain informed consent to the abortion within the meaning of that term as defined by the act of October 15, 1975 (P. L. 390, No. Ill), known as the Health Care Services Malpractice Act.”
“§ 3206. Parental consent.
“(a) General rule. — Except in the case of a medical emergency or except as provided in this section, if a pregnant woman is less than 18 years of age and not emancipated, or if she has been adjudged an incompetent under 20 Pa. C. S. §5511 (relating to petition and hearing; examination by court-appointed physician), a physician shall not perform an abortion upon her unless, in the case of a woman who is less than 18 years of age, he first obtains the informed consent both of the pregnant woman and of one of her parents; or, in the case of a woman who is incompetent, he first obtains the informed consent of her guardian. In deciding whether to grant such consent, a pregnant woman’s parent or guardian shall consider only their child’s or ward’s best interests. In the case of a pregnancy that is the result of incest, where the father is a party to the incestuous act, the pregnant woman need only obtain the consent of her mother.
“(b) Unavailability of parent or guardian. — If both parents have died or are otherwise unavailable to the physician within a reasonable time and in a reasonable manner, consent of the pregnant woman’s guardian or guardians shall be sufficient. If the pregnant woman’s parents are divorced, consent of the parent having custody shall be sufficient. If neither any parent nor a legal guardian is available to the physician within a reasonable time and in a reasonable manner, consent of any adult person standing in loco parentis shall be sufficient.
“(c) Petition to the court for consent. — If both of the parents or guardians of the pregnant woman refuse to consent to the performance of an abortion or if she elects not to seek the consent of either of her parents or of her guardian, the court of common pleas of the judicial district in which the applicant resides or in which the abortion is sought shall, upon petition or motion, after an appropriate hearing, authorize a physician to perform the abortion if the court determines that the pregnant woman is mature and capable of giving informed consent to the proposed abortion, and has, in fact, given such consent.
“(d) Court order. — If the court determines that the pregnant woman is not mature and capable of giving informed consent or if the pregnant woman does not claim to be mature and capable of giving informed consent, the court shall determine whether the performance of an abortion upon her would be in her best interests. If the court determines that the performance of an abortion would be in the best interests of the woman, it shall authorize a physician to perform the abortion.
“(e) Representation in proceedings. — The pregnant woman may participate in proceedings in the court on her own behalf and the court may appoint a guardian ad litem to assist her. The court shall, however, advise her that she has a right to court appointed counsel, and shall provide her with such counsel unless she wishes to appear with private counsel or has knowingly and intelligently waived representation by counsel.”
“§3207. Abortion facilities.
“(b) Reports. — Within 30 days after the effective date of this chapter, every facility at which abortions are performed shall file, and update immediately upon any change, a report with the department, containing the following information:
“(1) Name and address of the facility.
“(2) Name and address of any parent, subsidiary or affiliated organizations, corporations or associations.
“(3) Name and address of any parent, subsidiary or affiliated organizations, corporations or associations having contemporaneous commonality of ownership, beneficial interest, directorship or offieership with any other facility.
The information contained in those reports which are filed pursuant to this subsection by facilities which receive State-appropriated funds during the 12-ealendar-month period immediately preceding a request to inspect or copy such reports shall be deemed public information. Reports filed by facilities which do not receive State-appropriated funds shall only be available to law enforcement officials, the State Board of Medicine and the State Board of Osteopathic Medicine for use in the performance of their official duties. Any facility failing to comply with the provisions of this subsection shall be assessed by the department a fine of $500 for each day it is in violation hereof.”
“§ 3208. Printed information.
“(a) General rule. — The department shall cause to be published in English, Spanish and Vietnamese, within 60 days after this chapter becomes law, and shall update on an annual basis, the following easily comprehensible printed materials:
“(1) Geographically indexed materials designed to inform the woman of public and private agencies and services available to assist a woman through pregnancy, upon childbirth and while the child is dependent, including adoption agencies, which shall include a comprehensive list of the agencies available, a description of the services they offer and a description of the manner, including telephone numbers, in which they might be contacted, or, at the option of the department, printed materials including a toll-free 24-hour a day telephone number which may be called to obtain, orally, such a list and description of agencies in the locality of the caller' and of the services they offer. The materials shall provide information on the availability of medical assistance benefits for prenatal care, childbirth and neonatal care, and state that it is unlawful for any individual to coerce a woman to undergo abortion, that any physician who performs an abortion upon a woman without obtaining her informed consent or without according her a private medical consultation may be liable to her for damages in a civil action at law, that the father of a child is liable to assist in the support of that child, even in instances where the father has offered to pay for an abortion and that the law permits adoptive parents to pay costs of prenatal care, childbirth and neonatal care.
“(2) Materials designed to inform the woman of the probable anatomical and physiological characteristics of the unborn child at two-week gestational increments from fertilization to full term, including pictures representing the development of unborn children at two-week gestational increments, and any relevant information on the possibility of the unborn child’s survival; provided that any such pictures or drawings must contain the dimensions of the fetus and must be realistic and appropriate for the woman’s stage of pregnancy. The materials shall be objective, non-judgmental and designed to convey only accurate scientific information about the unborn child at the various gestational ages.' The material shall also contain objective information describing the methods of abortion procedures commonly employed, the medical risks commonly associated with each such procedure, the possible detrimental psychological effects of abortion and the medical risks commonly associated with each such procedure and the medical risks commonly associated with carrying a child to term.
“(b) Format. — The materials shall be printed in a typeface large enough to be clearly legible.
“(e) Free distribution. — The materials required under this section shall be available at no cost from the department upon request and in appropriate number to any person, facility or hospital.”
“§3209. Spousal notice.
“(a) Spousal notice required. — In order to further the Commonwealth’s interest in promoting the integrity of the marital relationship and to protect a spouse’s interests in having children within marriage and in protecting the prenatal life of that spouse’s child, no physician shall perform an abortion on a married woman, except as provided in subsections (b) and (c), unless he or she has received a signed statement, which need not be notarized, from the woman upon whom the abortion is to be performed, that she has notified her spouse that she is about to undergo an abortion. The statement shall bear a notice that any false statement made therein is punishable by law.
“(b) Exceptions. — The statement certifying that the notice required by subsection (a) has been given need not be furnished where the woman provides the physician a signed statement certifying at least one of the following:
“(1) Her spouse is not the father of the child.
“(2) Her spouse, after diligent effort, could not be located.
“(3) The pregnancy is a result of spousal sexual assault as described in section 3128 (relating to spousal sexual assault), which has been reported to a law enforcement agency having the requisite jurisdiction.
“(4) The woman has reason to believe that the furnishing of notice to her spouse is likely to result in the infliction of bodily injury upon her by her spouse or by another individual.
Such statement need not be notarized, but shall bear a notice that any false statements made therein are punishable by law.
“(c) Medical emergency. — The requirements of subsection (a) shall not apply in case of a medical emergency.
“(d) Forms. — The department shall cause to be published, forms which may be utilized for purposes of providing the signed statements required by subsections (a) and (b). The department shall distribute an adequate supply of such forms to all abortion facilities in this Commonwealth.
“(e) Penalty; civil action. — Any'physician who violates the provisions of this section is guilty of 'unprofessional conduct,’ and his -or her license for the practice of medicine and surgery shall be subject to suspension or revocation in accordance with procedures provided under the act of October 5, 1978 (P. L. 1109, No. 261), known as the Osteopathic Medical Practice Act, the act of December 20, 1985 (P. L. 457, No. 112), known as the Medical Practice Act of 1985, or their successor acts. In addition, any physician who knowingly violates the provisions of this section shall be civilly liable to the spouse who is the father of the aborted child for any damages caused thereby and for punitive damages in the amount of $5,000, and the court shall award a prevailing plaintiff a reasonable attorney fee as part of costs.”
“§3214. Reporting.
“(a) General rule. — For the purpose of promotion of maternal health and life by adding to the sum of medical and public health knowledge through the compilation of relevant data, and to promote the Commonwealth’s interest in protection of the unborn child, a report of each abortion performed shall be made to the department on forms prescribed by it. The report forms shall not identify the individual patient by name and shall include the following information:
“(1) Identification of the physician who performed the abortion, the concurring physician as required by section 3211(c)(2) (relating to abortion on unborn child of 24 or more weeks gestational age), the second physician as required by section 3211(c)(5) and the facility where the abortion was performed and of the referring physician, agency or service, if any.
“(2) The county and state in which the woman resides.
“(3) The woman’s age.
“(4) The number of prior pregnancies and prior abortions of the woman.
“(5) The gestational age of the unborn child at the time of the abortion.
“(6) The type of procedure performed or prescribed and the date of the abortion.
“(7) Pre-existing medical conditions of the woman which would complicate pregnancy, if any, and if known, any medical complication which resulted from the abortion itself.
“(8) The basis for the medical judgment of the physician who performed the abortion that the abortion was necessary to prevent either the death of the pregnant woman or the substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function of the woman, where an abortion has been performed pursuant to section 3211(b)(1).
“(9) The weight of the aborted child for any abortion performed pursuant to section 3211(b)(1).
“(10) Basis for any medical judgment that a medical emergency existed which excused the physician from compliance with any provision of this chapter.
“(11) The information required to be reported under section 3210(a) (relating to determination of gestational age).
“(12) Whether the abortion was performed upon a married woman and, if so, whether notice to her spouse was given. If no notice to her spouse was given, the report shall also indicate the reason for failure to provide notice.
“(f) Report by facility. — Every facility in which an abortion is performed within this Commonwealth during any quarter year shall file with the department a report showing the total number of abortions performed within the hospital or other facility during that quarter year. This report shall also show the total abortions performed in each trimester of pregnancy. Any report shall be available for public inspection and copying only if the facility receives State-appropriated funds within the 12-calendar-month period immediately preceding the filing of the report. These reports shall be submitted on a form prescribed by the department which will enable, a facility to indicate whether or not it is receiving State-appropriated funds. If the facility indicates on the form that it is not receiving State-appropriated funds, the department shall regard its report as confidential unless it receives other evidence which causes it to conclude that the facility receives State-appropriated funds.”
```

## Supplementary statutory source quotations and finding

### Section 3210(a), as quoted in district finding309

309. Section 3210 of the Act, as amended, provides as follows:
Except in the case of a medical emergency which prevents compliance with this section, no abortion shall be performed or induced unless the referring physician or the physician performing or inducing it has first made a determination of the probable gestational age of the unborn child. In making such a determination, the physician shall make such inquiries of the patient and perform or cause to be performed such medical examinations and tests as a prudent physician would consider necessary to make or perform in' making an accurate diagnosis with respect to gestational age. The physician who performs or induces the abortion shall report the type of inquiries made and the type of examinations and tests utilized to determine gestational age of the unborn child and the basis for the diagnosis with respect to gestational age on forms provided by the department.
18 Pa.Con.Stat.Ann. § 3210(a).

### Bypass confidentiality, expedition and penalties, as quoted by Third Circuit statutory footnote

The following are the source court's expressly selected excerpts, not an assertion that omitted subsections do not exist. The ellipsis under (h) belongs to that source. No further substantive bypass condition is inferred.

(f) PROCEEDINGS.—
(1) Court proceedings under this section shall be confidential and shall be given such precedence over other pending matters as will ensure that the court may reach a decision promptly and without delay in order to serve the best interests of the pregnant woman. In no case shall the court of common pleas fail to rule within three business days of the date of application. A court of common pleas which conducts proceedings under this section shall make in writing specific factual findings and legal conclusions supporting its decision and shall, upon the initial filing of the minor’s petition for judicial authorization of an abortion, order a sealed record of the petition, pleadings, submissions, transcripts, exhibits, orders, evidence and any other written material to be maintained which shall include its own findings and conclusions.
(3) The name of the pregnant woman shall not be entered on any docket which is subject to public inspection. All persons shall be excluded from hearings under this section except the applicant and such other persons whose presence is specifically requested by the applicant or her guardian.
(h) REGULATION OF PROCEEDINGS.—
... Any court to which an appeal is taken under this section shall give prompt and confidential attention thereto and shall rule thereon within five business days of the filing of the appeal.
(i) PENALTY. — Any person who performs an abortion upon a woman who is an unemanci-pated minor or incompetent to whom this section applies either with knowledge that she is a minor or incompetent to whom this section applies, or with reckless disregard or negligence as to whether she is a minor or incompetent to whom this section applies, and who intentionally, knowingly or recklessly fails to conform to any requirement of this section is guilty of “unprofessional conduct" and his license for the practice of medicine and surgery shall be suspended ... for a period of at least three months.

### Third Circuit footnote23: parental attendance is an unadopted interpretation

. The district court held that the requirement of “informed" parental consent would necessitate in-person parental consent. We need not decide whether that is correct, for any burden caused by one-parent, in-person consent is surely no more than that caused by two-parent consent, cf. Hodgson, 110 S.Ct. at 2970; and the Supreme Court has held that a judicial bypass option alleviates the undue burden caused by two-parent consent requirements.

### Section 3128(a)–(b), as quoted in district finding268

268. “Spousal sexual assault” is defined as follows:
(a) Sexual assault. — A person commits a felony of the second degree when that person engages in sexual intercourse with that person’s spouse:
(1) by forcible compulsion;
(2) by threat of forcible compulsion that would prevent resistance by a person of reasonable resolution; or
(3) who is unconscious.
(b) Involuntary spousal deviate sexual intercourse. — A person commits a felony of the second degree when that person engages in deviate sexual intercourse with that person’s spouse:
(1) by forcible compulsion;
(2) by threat of forcible compulsion that would prevent resistance by a person of reasonable resolution; or
(3) who is unconscious.
18 Pa.Con.Stat.Ann. § 3128 (1990 Supp.).

### District finding297 concerning section3128(c)

297. The marital rape exception to section 3209 cannot be claimed by women who are victims of coercive sexual behavior other than penetration. The 90-day reporting requirement of the spousal sexual assault statute, 18 Pa.Con.Stat.Ann. § 3128(c), further narrows the class of sexually abused wives who can claim the exception, since many of these women may be psychologically unable to discuss or report the rape for several years after the incident. [Trial Testimony of Dr. Walker, Vol. II at 13].

This is the district's source-supported finding that the incorporated spousal-assault law requires reporting within90days; it is not represented as a full verbatim quotation of subsection(c).

### Act64 severability, as quoted and applied by Third Circuit

. Our conclusion that § 3209 is unconstitutional does not prevent the Commonwealth from putting into effect the rest of the Act's provisions. The Act contains a broad severability provision. "The provisions of this act are sever-able. If any word, phrase or provision of this act or its application to any person or circumstance is held invalid, the invalidity shall not affect any other word, phrase or provision or application of this act which can be given effect without the invalid word, phrase, provision or application.” Act of November 17, 1989, 1989 Pa.Laws 592, No. 64, § 6. In addition, Pennsylvania has a general severability statute. 1 Pa. Cons.Stat.Ann. § 1925 (Supp.1991). Severability is a question of state law. Davis v. Michigan Dept. of Treasury, 489 U.S. 803, 818, 109 S.Ct. 1500, 1509, 103 L.Ed.2d 891 (1989). Because the remainder of the Act may be given effect without § 3209, we conclude that § 3209 may be severed from the Act in accordance with Pennsylvania law.

## Supplementary primary-writing pointer

Souter's full concurrence in Payne v.Tennessee,501U.S.808, June27,1991, joined by Kennedy, is available in `prior/payne.json` and `prior/payne-souter.txt`; root confirms full read including notes. It distinguishes constitutional error alone from special justification (Arizona v.Rumsey) and addresses particular unworkability and tension. It is a source for the later model, not a commitment on this case. O'Connor's Akron/Thornburgh threshold severe-obstacle approach and the distinctions in Ohio v.Akron Center must be read on their own terms; do not replace them at preflight with an as-yet unadopted formulation or blend threshold burden with subsequent justification.

## Sanitized earlier-current-term law and published positions

# Casey correction — sanitized entering-law and public-position map

Prepared for neutral preflight/modeling. Effective cutoff: immediately before the June 29, 1992 Casey merits event. This file contains no Casey disposition, prior Casey private position, comparator, or proposed current Stone position. It uses opening trackers and public renders only for the legal/public-position material. The control reviewer has inspected workspace chronology but does not pass its Casey or later law through this projection. No inference below is itself a vote commitment.

## Opening law and institution

- `state/HOLDINGS.md` and `state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md`: Last completed simulated October Term **None**; processed through **Term-opening baseline only, effective October 7, 1991**; edition **September 14, 2026**. Both registers are empty of simulated doctrine. Accordingly there is no operative Standards and Tests title, Present operation effective field, or Current wording authority to quote for abortion. Surviving pre-divergence law must be researched from primary sources; the empty register does not erase that law.
- `state/STANDING_STATE.md`, `foundation/COURT_COMPOSITION.md`, and the October 23, 1991 oath/reallotment source establish the nine-member Court at April 22 argument and June 29 decision: Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. Senior Associate order is White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. Thomas's oath predates argument. No roster reason for nonparticipation is identified.
- Full-Court judgment requires five of nine; six constitute a quorum. Chief assigns on a separable component when he is in that judgment coalition; otherwise the senior participating Associate in that coalition assigns. Assignment authority does not establish authorship.
- The earlier current-term events identified below supply only their stated law. No pre-Casey public render located in this review changes Roe or adopts an abortion framework. In particular, psychiatric-custody, trial-medication, competency-procedure, and public-employment cases do not silently settle the abortion framework.
- The five June 26 events are date-eligible public continuity. June 29 Casey, Lucas, and Espinosa share the ordinary uncoordinated entering-law baseline, with case-list presentation sequence Casey → Lucas → Espinosa. Neither Lucas nor Espinosa can supply entering law for this event. Later dependency work is segregated in `later_dependency.md` and is not a modeling source.

## Exact relevant public propositions, joins, and boundaries

### Foucha v. Louisiana — May 18, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK9.md`, entry beginning line 508; topology line 532 onward; public separate positions line 588 onward.

- White writes. Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter join Parts I, II-A, II-C, IV (six). White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter join II-B (five). White, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter join Part III equal protection (four; noncontrolling). The reversal/remand is 6–3; Kennedy and Thomas/Scalia dissent.
- Exact public rule, ended predicate: “When the State concedes that an insanity acquittee is no longer mentally ill, it may not continue his psychiatric confinement under the existing insanity-acquittee authority solely because he cannot prove that he will be harmless.” The original acquittal is not a suspended criminal sentence. Jones, O'Connor v. Donaldson, Jackson supply the continuing-predicate limit.
- Exact additional rule: “If the State invokes ordinary civil commitment, it must establish current mental illness and dangerousness by clear and convincing evidence; additional procedure cannot supply an absent substantive ground.” White's II-B has five direct joins; Vitek, Jackson, Addington, Baxstrom perform the stated work.
- Exact alternative-ground boundary: “A legitimate preventive purpose and periodic review do not cure the absence of a carefully limited substantive authority; the Court leaves materially narrower preventive regimes undecided.” Six join. Salerno's confined pretrial authority is distinguished from indefinite post-acquittal psychiatric restraint. No new general scrutiny tier or universal dangerousness proof standard.
- O'Connor's concurrence preserves materially narrower preventive schemes, limited in nature and duration to substantial safety concerns. It does not require States to provide an insanity defense.
- Kennedy's solo dissent: “He views state definitions of criminal insanity and incapacitation as constitutionally relevant and rejects automatic transfer of ordinary civil-commitment rules to this setting.” The distinct criminal adjudication channel matters.
- Thomas, joined by Scalia: acquittees differ from ordinary civil committees; State may attach continuing safety-based custody to acquittee status; periodic counseled review is adequate. Exact scope: “They reject an expansive new substantive right against this confinement and distinguish its lack of a fixed endpoint from a refusal ever to provide release review.”
- Souter's direct joinder is evidence of a protected substantive liberty and a real justification requirement in this defined setting, not proof that he has selected any abortion-specific scrutiny standard. Thomas's dissent is evidence against extension beyond a historically and doctrinally recognized channel, not an abortion holding.

### Riggins v. Nevada — May 18, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK9.md`, entry beginning line 608; topology about line 632; selected separate positions about line 672.

- O'Connor writes Parts I–IV, joined by Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter (six). Kennedy concurs only in the judgment. Thomas dissents, Scalia joining I, II-B, II-C, but not II-A. Reversal/new-trial judgment 7–2.
- Exact rule: “Once a pretrial detainee objects to continued antipsychotic medication, the State must establish medical appropriateness and a need sufficient to override the protected refusal interest, considering reasonable less intrusive means.” The opinion relies on Harper's bodily-liberty protection and Allen's trial interests; six direct joins.
- Exact controlling limit: “The Court does not impose a new documentary ritual, universal proof quantum or strict-scrutiny formula.” Complete conditions for competency-restoration medication and deliberate loss of competence remain undecided.
- A supported substantial risk of trial impairment that cannot reliably be reconstructed requires a new trial without proof exactly how an unmedicated trial would differ. This is record-specific, not a general structural-error rule.
- Kennedy would require an extraordinary showing that involuntary medication for competence presents no significant risk of materially changing reactions, participation, or counsel assistance. His proposed standard is **not the Court's rule**.
- Thomas/Scalia distinguish a protected refusal interest, potentially remediable civilly, from proven unfairness warranting conviction reversal; possible impairment alone does not suffice. Thomas alone additionally contests involuntariness and preservation/grant scope of an independent Harper theory. Do not attribute those extra objections to Scalia.
- This gives Souter direct public support for a state justification burden and consideration of less intrusive means once an established bodily-liberty interest is implicated; it does not establish that he would transplant either verbatim into abortion regulation. The same is true of O'Connor and the other majority joiners.

### Lee v. Weisman — June 24, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK13.md`, entry beginning line 535; topology about line 553; separate positions about line 613.

- Judgment 6–3 affirmance (Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter; White, Scalia, Thomas dissent).
- Blackmun's Part I has six; Part II sponsorship ground has Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter (five). Kennedy refuses Part II but writes a pressure-based concurrence joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter (independent five-Justice ground). Souter's further concurrence is joined by Stevens and O'Connor; Scalia dissent joined by White and Thomas.
- Sponsorship holding applies Engel, Schempp, Lemon to officials' selection, direction and incorporation of worship into graduation; neither nonsectarian wording nor formally optional attendance cures this official sponsorship.
- Kennedy's exact pressure holding applies when school control and ceremony importance impose “substantial pressure to participate, appear to participate, or forgo the occasion.” Formal permission to remain silent or abstain does not cure this record; “mere offense alone is insufficient.” Adult legislative audiences and general accommodation remain distinct.
- Souter's additional noncontrolling position: Establishment Clause reaches nonpreferential religious endorsement; coercion not universally necessary; accommodation must lift a discernible religious-exercise burden. The public description is a historical and neutrality account. These are subject-specific grounds, not free-standing substantive due-process methodology.
- Scalia/White/Thomas: historical ceremonial accommodation; religious coercion requires law-backed compulsion; respectful silence is civility; sectarian endorsement remains distinct.
- Relevance bounded: Souter, O'Connor, Stevens, Blackmun and Kennedy do not equate formal choice with meaningful freedom in the stated adolescent/state-directed setting. That supplies a method comparison for practical burdens, not an abortion vote. Scalia/White/Thomas demand a tighter coercion trigger here; that does not prove all actual effects irrelevant in other doctrines.

### Medina v. California — June 22, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK13.md`, entry beginning line 222; topology about line 243; law/positions about lines 287–301.

- Kennedy principal opinion joined White, Scalia, Thomas: four for Patterson methodology and broad rejection of Mathews. O'Connor concurrence in judgment joined Souter. Blackmun dissent joined Stevens. Stone separate dissent.
- Six expressly allow California's defendant-borne preponderance burden and equivalent presumption at an adequate competence hearing, preserving counsel, psychiatric access, inquiry when sufficient doubt arises, and prohibition on trial if actually incompetent. No complete opinion has five joins.
- Exact method boundary: “O'Connor and Souter retain Mathews as useful guidance. Their method and the principal opinion's historical approach are not logical subsets from which a broader Marks rule can be extracted.”
- O'Connor/Souter practical-fairness rationale: counsel and psychiatric inquiry provide substantial protection; cooperation/information incentives can improve the record; government persuasion does not necessarily improve overall reliability.
- Kennedy/White/Scalia/Thomas support historical constitutional limits and allowance for state procedural choice. Blackmun/Stevens would put risk of an inconclusive hearing on State because incapacity may impair the defendant's ability to prove it.
- Exact dependency caution: “Foucha v. Louisiana concerns the grounds and safeguards of psychiatric custody and does not allocate the competency-hearing burden. Riggins v. Nevada protects against unjustified compelled medication and its trial risks; it does not find every mental-health issue governed by the same burden.” This is unusually pertinent against deriving an abortion burden mechanically from Riggins or Foucha.

### Collins v. City of Harker Heights — February 26, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK5.md`, entry beginning line 394; topology about line 420.

- Stevens writes A and B for all nine; C for eight (all except Stone). Affirmance 9–0.
- Federal-right violation and municipal attribution remain separate. Voluntary employment creates no general constitutional safe-workplace guarantee or custody. Knowing safety omissions in the pleaded setting are not substantive constitutional arbitrariness; assumed state-law breach alone does not establish such a wrong.
- DeShaney and Youngberg distinguish State deprivations/involuntary confinement from general duties of protection. Daniels prevents transformation of ordinary tort law into substantive due process. The Court leaves actual compulsion, deliberate bodily injury, and distinct affirmative-deprivation questions open.
- Relevance: all eight non-Stone Justices require a source and boundary for the asserted right. The holding concerns voluntary workplace omissions; it neither erases established reproductive liberty nor decides State-imposed restrictions on its exercise.

### Hilton v. South Carolina Public Railways — December 16, 1991

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK3.md`, entry beginning line 68.

- Kennedy Court opinion joined Stone, White, Stevens, Souter (five of eight participants). Blackmun judgment concurrence. O'Connor dissent joined Scalia. Thomas did not participate because argument preceded his oath.
- Distinguishes Parden's established statutory FELA coverage from displaced constructive-waiver doctrine. Specific coverage remains enforceable in state court; congressional retention and State/employee compensation reliance support adherence. Does not revive federal-court constructive waiver or generalize every Article I damages action.
- Souter's join supports treating precedent by proposition, retaining an independently surviving component, and considering demonstrated reliance. It is a **statutory** precedent, with Congress able to amend; do not assume constitutional stare decisis follows automatically.
- White and Stevens join that same bounded rationale. Kennedy authors it. Blackmun's broader congressional-power judgment ground is not a join to the Court. O'Connor/Scalia regard coverage and waiver as inseparable and demand clearer text. Thomas supplies no precedent commitment from this case.

### Quill Corp. v. North Dakota — May 26, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK9.md`, entry beginning line 1176; topology about line 1201; separate positions about line 1272.

- Stevens Parts I–III, joined all eight non-Stone Justices, overrule Bellas Hess's due-process physical-presence prerequisite for collection duties; Stone does not reach due process.
- Stevens Part IV joined Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter (four) on broader Commerce analysis. Scalia concurrence joined Kennedy, Thomas retains precise Commerce precedent for adherence, justified reliance, and Congress's ability to coordinate change without endorsing substantive merits. White would overrule Commerce restriction.
- Eight direct supporters retain the **specific** Commerce collection precedent; no complete Part IV majority. No general physical-presence theory for other taxes. Software application separately has five (Stevens, Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter, Stone).
- Exact controlling explanation of pertinent limit: “Adherence does not require every Justice to endorse the precedent's original substantive reasoning.” Exact current-law limit: “Hilton's FELA coverage holding also remains separate; its limited treatment of precedent supplies no automatic fiscal rule.”
- Relevance: Souter accepts doctrinal change in one constitutional component while retaining another on concrete reliance/precedent grounds. Thomas and Scalia can respect a square precedent they decline to defend on original merits. White's willingness to abandon this particular Commerce precedent does not substitute for his abortion writings. Congress's dormant-Commerce adjustment power is materially different from power to override a constitutional liberty rule.

### Georgia v. McCollum — June 18, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK12.md`, entry beginning line 149; topology about line 170.

- Blackmun Court opinion joined White, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter. Thomas and Stone separate judgment concurrences. O'Connor and Scalia separate dissents. Judgment 7–2.
- Thomas **accepts Edmonson's controlling consequences despite objecting to its state-action premise**, and does not join the Court's opinion. Scalia accepts logical applicability but refuses to extend the premise he regards as erroneous; he does not join O'Connor's criminal-defense-specific distinction.
- Relevance: Thomas's bounded adherence conclusion must be accounted for in any stare-decisis model; his record does not justify an inference that he automatically discards any disputed precedent. Conversely it does not establish his answer when squarely asked whether to overrule a substantive constitutional premise. Souter joins both limited state attribution and practical enforcement of jurors' rights; neither creates an abortion burden rule.

### Hudson v. McMillian — February 25, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK5.md`, entry beginning line 21.

- O'Connor A/C joined Stone, White, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter (six); B universal Whitley formulation joined White, Kennedy, Souter only (four including author). Stevens and Blackmun separately reject the universal heightened standard, Blackmun judgment-only. Thomas dissent joined Scalia. Reversal 7–2.
- Six hold significant injury unnecessary for this proven nontrivial malicious beating. Thomas/Scalia require serious objective deprivation alongside intent, not necessarily visible/permanent physical marks. Souter joins a narrower disposition and broader noncontrolling framework, demonstrating the need to distinguish actual proposition joins from judgment support.

### Wright v. West — June 19, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK12.md`, entry beginning line 821.

- Thomas principal opinion joined Scalia. White separately adopts the record-specific Jackson demonstration. O'Connor concurrence joined Blackmun/Stevens; Kennedy and Souter separately concur. Souter stops at Teague new-rule bar and does **not** join the sufficiency merits ground. Eight others expressly support narrow Jackson merits sufficiency through separate writings; no whole opinion majority.
- Souter's position: requested postfinality restriction on the established inference from falsely explained recent possession was not dictated when final. Thomas finds sufficiency under Jackson while reserving broad review-standard choice. No general deference reform is adopted.
- Relevance: Souter is prepared to use a threshold precedent rule even though an apparently available merits path exists; Thomas's historical discussion is not transformed into a Court-wide rule or attributed as a finalized general theory beyond his actual reservation.

## Justice-by-Justice indexing for neutral modeling

| Justice | Strongest earlier public sources | What the sources warrant; unresolved limit |
|---|---|---|
| White | Foucha author; Riggins join; Collins join; Hilton join; Quill partial dissent; Lee dissent | Protects defined bodily liberty with an operative predicate/justification; honors some separable precedents and rejects others; no inference that this supplants his date-eligible Roe/Thornburgh writings. |
| Blackmun | Foucha join; Riggins join; Lee author/pressure join; Medina dissent; Hudson judgment concurrence | Recognized liberty requires adequate substantive protection and practical safeguards; rejects liability floors/heightened rules that defeat the established protection. Direct abortion writings remain primary for abortion. |
| Stevens | Foucha/Riggins joins; Collins author; Lee sponsorship/pressure/Souter joins; Medina dissent; Quill author; Hudson separate | Carefully bounded constitutional source, real practical burden, separate doctrinal components, ability to modify one precedent component while retaining another. No general law that all regulation with any burden fails. |
| O'Connor | Foucha narrow concurrence; Riggins author; Lee both grounds/Souter join; Medina practical-fairness concurrence; Hudson author; Quill Part IV join | Justification and less intrusive alternatives in established bodily-liberty setting coexist with state burden allocation under adequate safeguards in a distinct channel. Do not import a universal balancing rule or universal State burden. |
| Scalia | Thomas Foucha/Hudson joins; Thomas Riggins partial join; Lee author; Quill author; McCollum dissent; Medina principal join | Strong source/channel limits and refusal to expand some precedents; nevertheless square-precedent reliance in Quill. Preserve Riggins nonjoin to Thomas's extra scope objections. |
| Kennedy | Foucha dissent; Riggins extraordinary-showing writing; Lee pressure writing; Hilton author; Quill Scalia join; Medina author | Distinct regulated setting and real coercive effects matter; no automatic transfer of civil-commitment rules; strong trial-liberty protection does not prove a general abortion balancing standard. |
| Souter | Foucha substantive/procedural/EP joins; Riggins full join; Lee Court/pressure/Souter writing; Medina O'Connor join; Hilton/Quill joins; Wright Teague concurrence | Public support for protected liberty with real justification, practical fairness and reliance-sensitive precedent; bounded uncertainty remains whether and how these general methods translate into abortion-specific rules, exceptions and burdens. No prior abortion writing is supplied by these sources. |
| Thomas | Foucha/Riggins/Hudson authored dissents; Lee Scalia join; Medina principal join; Quill Scalia join; McCollum judgment writing; Wright principal | Public caution about expanded rights and exact grounds/remedies; also specific adherence to precedent despite doubts. Bounded uncertainty remains whether stare decisis sustains abortion precedent when squarely asked to reconsider it. No prior abortion writing is supplied; do not back-fill one from the present event's outcome. |

## Research/status boundary

This source map verifies simulated public content against its repository renders. It does not purport to verify pre-divergence abortion writings, lower-court Casey facts, statute terms, briefs, or argument; those require the independent primary-source preflight. All public excerpts above are quotes from the simulation's existing public files, not invented verbatim judicial language from historical opinions. Their effect is limited to their exact source entries. The map is available for the fresh freeze; the later-event report must remain segregated until post-freeze assembly/dependency review.


## Frozen handoff and incomplete-check gate

Neutral record/posture/statute framing is frozen as written. Referenced source reports remain the audit trail: `third-circuit-source-report.md`, `district_source_report.md`, `providers-source-report.md`, `briefs/neutral-source-facts.md`. Full individual Supreme Court writing review is a gate before Justice commitment freeze; it is not certified here beyond reported completed source reads. Model each actual component and framework proposition independently, keep alternative grounds and remedies separate, and state uncertainty for Souter/Thomas without a prior abortion writing. A material source correction requires neutral refresh before final freeze. No historical Casey comparator or Stone supplement may be opened by modelers before those commitments are frozen.

## Neutral scope addendum — spousal compelled speech and emergency vagueness

**Pre-comparator refresh, September 15, 2026.** This addendum corrects an omission in the scope catalog, not statutory text or any finding. All modelers must address these two grounds expressly before the global refreeze. The validator personally checked the full relevant passages in the official April 22, 1992 transcript, printed pages36–38 and51–52, against `briefs/argument-official.txt`; the full argument had already been read by the source agent. No comparator or Stone text supplies this correction.

**Actual argument, spousal speech:** At Tr.36 line25 through37 line15, the questioner identified in the continuing exchange as Justice O'Connor asks whether requiring the woman to communicate with her husband invokes the First Amendment. Preate answers that, in his view, it does not, and invokes the State's legitimate notification interest. At Tr.37 lines16–25 and38 lines1–19, O'Connor turns to physician counseling, questions characterization of consultation as commercial speech and distinguishes advertising; she expressly allows that the requirement might satisfy a First Amendment test while disputing the classification. These are questions and advocacy, not commitments. At Tr.51 lines15–25 and52 lines1–8, Kolbert argues that the notice law subjects family communication to prosecutorial inquiry and expressly asserts First Amendment concerns both in spousal notice and in counseling. Her allegation that the compelled messages transmit the State's ideology is an advocate's legal characterization, not a trial finding that every message is ideological. Her broad claim about every married woman is also argument, not an adopted denominator.

**Separate issue and reach:** The spousal compelled-speech theory is distinct from physician counseling speech, the substantive abortion-choice objection, marital/informational privacy, and equal protection. It cannot be omitted on an assertion that no speech theory concerning spousal notice was presented. Actual oral presentation does not itself establish every theory's preservation in the pleadings, lower-court decisions or question presented. The opening/reply reports establish developed counseling speech claims; no unqualified waiver or universally preserved independent spousal-speech holding is inferred from them. Available paths include reaching a sufficiently preserved independent compelled-speech ground with an identified standard, or declining to decide that distinct ground/preservation question because a sufficient liberty disposition affords complete relief. A Justice who upholds notice cannot silently treat another Justice's liberty invalidation as that Justice's own sufficient ground; that Justice must state whether an independent speech claim is reached, rejected, or left outside the necessary preserved merits, and why. The statutory notice certification does not itself say that the woman must personally utter a prescribed ideological formula; arguments about compelled communication must use the actual text and every notification exception already quoted in this packet.

**Emergency vagueness reach:** Officials' brief p.56 n.23 says the district court did not reach standalone vagueness and pp.56–57 claims providers failed to renew it. Root's full providers-opening/reply read, recorded in `providers-source-report.md`, finds that the opening calls the definition vague in its factual account but develops health protection and the permissibility of the saving construction at pp.60–61; the reply has no separately developed vagueness section. This is a preservation uncertainty, not an agreed waiver. Model standalone notice/clarity vagueness separately from whether the prospective good-faith definition protects the constitutionally necessary health interests. A disposition confined to the presented health/construction question may expressly leave standalone vagueness and its preservation undecided; it must not invent a separate holding rejecting vagueness or claim the statutory words already received an authoritative state-court construction.

**Parental informed-consent clarification, before the global comparator gate:** Section3206(a) itself requires informed parental consent but does not separately define that consent or expressly incorporate every timing and delivery mechanism of §3205. The district court's analysis at 744 F.Supp.1382–1384 used §3205's content to evaluate the parent's informed-consent duty and treated that content, the emergency definition and the evidence of clinic attendance as grounds for invalidation. That use of §3205 is a **lower-court construction**, not express statutory incorporation. In particular, a parent-specific24hour waiting period, nondelegable physician-delivery requirement or mandatory clinic visit cannot be invented merely from the adjective informed. The Third Circuit's footnote23 left the attendance interpretation unresolved. Model ordinary informed-parent content, any actual incorporated ancillary-content ground, and a proved timing/delivery/attendance construction separately; identify the source and scope of each rather than attributing every §3205 mechanism to enacted §3206 text.
