### Medina v. California — June 22, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK13.md`, entry beginning line 222; topology about line 243; law/positions about lines 287–301.

- Kennedy principal opinion joined White, Scalia, Thomas: four for Patterson methodology and broad rejection of Mathews. O'Connor concurrence in judgment joined Souter. Blackmun dissent joined Stevens. Stone separate dissent.
- Six expressly allow California's defendant-borne preponderance burden and equivalent presumption at an adequate competence hearing, preserving counsel, psychiatric access, inquiry when sufficient doubt arises, and prohibition on trial if actually incompetent. No complete opinion has five joins.
- Exact method boundary: “O'Connor and Souter retain Mathews as useful guidance. Their method and the principal opinion's historical approach are not logical subsets from which a broader Marks rule can be extracted.”
- O'Connor/Souter practical-fairness rationale: counsel and psychiatric inquiry provide substantial protection; cooperation/information incentives can improve the record; government persuasion does not necessarily improve overall reliability.
- Kennedy/White/Scalia/Thomas support historical constitutional limits and allowance for state procedural choice. Blackmun/Stevens would put risk of an inconclusive hearing on State because incapacity may impair the defendant's ability to prove it.
- Exact dependency caution: “Foucha v. Louisiana concerns the grounds and safeguards of psychiatric custody and does not allocate the competency-hearing burden. Riggins v. Nevada protects against unjustified compelled medication and its trial risks; it does not find every mental-health issue governed by the same burden.” This is unusually pertinent against deriving an abortion burden mechanically from Riggins or Foucha.

### Collins v. City of Harker Heights — February 26, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK5.md`, entry beginning line 394; topology about line 420.

- Stevens writes A and B for all nine; C for eight (all except Stone). Affirmance 9–0.
- Federal-right violation and municipal attribution remain separate. Voluntary employment creates no general constitutional safe-workplace guarantee or custody. Knowing safety omissions in the pleaded setting are not substantive constitutional arbitrariness; assumed state-law breach alone does not establish such a wrong.
- DeShaney and Youngberg distinguish State deprivations/involuntary confinement from general duties of protection. Daniels prevents transformation of ordinary tort law into substantive due process. The Court leaves actual compulsion, deliberate bodily injury, and distinct affirmative-deprivation questions open.
- Relevance: all eight non-Stone Justices require a source and boundary for the asserted right. The holding concerns voluntary workplace omissions; it neither erases established reproductive liberty nor decides State-imposed restrictions on its exercise.

### Hilton v. South Carolina Public Railways — December 16, 1991

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK3.md`, entry beginning line 68.

- Kennedy Court opinion joined Stone, White, Stevens, Souter (five of eight participants). Blackmun judgment concurrence. O'Connor dissent joined Scalia. Thomas did not participate because argument preceded his oath.
- Distinguishes Parden's established statutory FELA coverage from displaced constructive-waiver doctrine. Specific coverage remains enforceable in state court; congressional retention and State/employee compensation reliance support adherence. Does not revive federal-court constructive waiver or generalize every Article I damages action.
- Souter's join supports treating precedent by proposition, retaining an independently surviving component, and considering demonstrated reliance. It is a **statutory** precedent, with Congress able to amend; do not assume constitutional stare decisis follows automatically.
- White and Stevens join that same bounded rationale. Kennedy authors it. Blackmun's broader congressional-power judgment ground is not a join to the Court. O'Connor/Scalia regard coverage and waiver as inseparable and demand clearer text. Thomas supplies no precedent commitment from this case.

### Quill Corp. v. North Dakota — May 26, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK9.md`, entry beginning line 1176; topology about line 1201; separate positions about line 1272.

- Stevens Parts I–III, joined all eight non-Stone Justices, overrule Bellas Hess's due-process physical-presence prerequisite for collection duties; Stone does not reach due process.
- Stevens Part IV joined Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter (four) on broader Commerce analysis. Scalia concurrence joined Kennedy, Thomas retains precise Commerce precedent for adherence, justified reliance, and Congress's ability to coordinate change without endorsing substantive merits. White would overrule Commerce restriction.
- Eight direct supporters retain the **specific** Commerce collection precedent; no complete Part IV majority. No general physical-presence theory for other taxes. Software application separately has five (Stevens, Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter, Stone).
- Exact controlling explanation of pertinent limit: “Adherence does not require every Justice to endorse the precedent's original substantive reasoning.” Exact current-law limit: “Hilton's FELA coverage holding also remains separate; its limited treatment of precedent supplies no automatic fiscal rule.”
- Relevance: Souter accepts doctrinal change in one constitutional component while retaining another on concrete reliance/precedent grounds. Thomas and Scalia can respect a square precedent they decline to defend on original merits. White's willingness to abandon this particular Commerce precedent does not substitute for his abortion writings. Congress's dormant-Commerce adjustment power is materially different from power to override a constitutional liberty rule.

### Georgia v. McCollum — June 18, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK12.md`, entry beginning line 149; topology about line 170.

- Blackmun Court opinion joined White, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter. Thomas and Stone separate judgment concurrences. O'Connor and Scalia separate dissents. Judgment 7–2.
- Thomas **accepts Edmonson's controlling consequences despite objecting to its state-action premise**, and does not join the Court's opinion. Scalia accepts logical applicability but refuses to extend the premise he regards as erroneous; he does not join O'Connor's criminal-defense-specific distinction.
- Relevance: Thomas's bounded adherence conclusion must be accounted for in any stare-decisis model; his record does not justify an inference that he automatically discards any disputed precedent. Conversely it does not establish his answer when squarely asked whether to overrule a substantive constitutional premise. Souter joins both limited state attribution and practical enforcement of jurors' rights; neither creates an abortion burden rule.

### Hudson v. McMillian — February 25, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK5.md`, entry beginning line 21.

- O'Connor A/C joined Stone, White, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter (six); B universal Whitley formulation joined White, Kennedy, Souter only (four including author). Stevens and Blackmun separately reject the universal heightened standard, Blackmun judgment-only. Thomas dissent joined Scalia. Reversal 7–2.
- Six hold significant injury unnecessary for this proven nontrivial malicious beating. Thomas/Scalia require serious objective deprivation alongside intent, not necessarily visible/permanent physical marks. Souter joins a narrower disposition and broader noncontrolling framework, demonstrating the need to distinguish actual proposition joins from judgment support.

### Wright v. West — June 19, 1992

Source: `terms/OT1991/output/OT_1991CHUNK12.md`, entry beginning line 821.

- Thomas principal opinion joined Scalia. White separately adopts the record-specific Jackson demonstration. O'Connor concurrence joined Blackmun/Stevens; Kennedy and Souter separately concur. Souter stops at Teague new-rule bar and does **not** join the sufficiency merits ground. Eight others expressly support narrow Jackson merits sufficiency through separate writings; no whole opinion majority.
- Souter's position: requested postfinality restriction on the established inference from falsely explained recent possession was not dictated when final. Thomas finds sufficiency under Jackson while reserving broad review-standard choice. No general deference reform is adopted.
- Relevance: Souter is prepared to use a threshold precedent rule even though an apparently available merits path exists; Thomas's historical discussion is not transformed into a Court-wide rule or attributed as a finalized general theory beyond his actual reservation.

| Blackmun | Foucha join; Riggins join; Lee author/pressure join; Medina dissent; Hudson judgment concurrence | Recognized liberty requires adequate substantive protection and practical safeguards; rejects liability floors/heightened rules that defeat the established protection. Direct abortion writings remain primary for abortion. |

No prior abortion writing is supplied by these sources. |

## Research/status boundary

This source map verifies simulated public content against its repository renders. It does not purport to verify pre-divergence abortion writings, lower-court Casey facts, statute terms, briefs, or argument; those require the independent primary-source preflight. All public excerpts above are quotes from the simulation's existing public files, not invented verbatim judicial language from historical opinions. Their effect is limited to their exact source entries. The map is available for the fresh freeze; the later-event report must remain segregated until post-freeze assembly/dependency review.

# Frozen provisional commitments — O’Connor and Kennedy

**Matter:** Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, Nos.91-744 and91-902; June29,1992. **Stage:** after full neutral preflight; before historical comparator and before Stone. **Research cutoff:** June29,1992, using the entering baseline immediately before Casey. **Frozen September15,2026.**

These are newly modeled commitments. No earlier Casey commitment was examined or carried forward. The complete initial74,509-character `neutral-modeling-packet.md` was read after its validator declared it frozen; the subsequent pre-comparator “Neutral scope addendum — spousal compelled speech and emergency vagueness” was also read in full, and the explicit reach rows below were refrozen after that refresh. The complete own writings identified below, including notes, and the sanitized earlier-term public-position map were read. True blindness was unavailable: the reused source-retrieval context had disclosed an incidental historical search-result exposure, and creation of a fresh context was unavailable. This is the Engine’s disclosed single-conversation fallback with an explicit staged freeze, not a blind experiment.

**How to use the tables.** A and B are adversarial lawful paths, not two votes. The selected commitment states the sufficient ground and why the strongest contrary path loses on the frozen record. The final column supplies both the join barrier and the minimum narrower formulation the Justice would accept. These prospective boundaries do not invent drafts, conferences, dialogue, assignments, authorship, or historical joinders. A later proposed formulation that changes a selected commitment requires the Engine’s reconciliation procedure. Judgment support is never a presumed joinder to another Justice’s reasoning.

## Source keys and same-term continuity

Every row cites a Justice’s own writing or actual joinder first. Sources are full CAP opinions in `tmp/casey_v1_2/prior/`; exact source files and read certification are also in `oconnor_kennedy_source_map.md`.

- **OA:** O’Connor, Akron dissent,462U.S.416,452–475(1983), `akron.json`, opinion1. It separates initial severe burden from subsequent compelling justification, rejects trimester machinery, and addresses information, physicians, waiting, parental involvement and separate compelled ideology.
- **OT:** O’Connor, Thornburgh dissent,476U.S.747,814–832(1986), `thornburgh.json`, opinion4. Full-record adjudication; same burden structure; medical information, optional materials, independent speech, reports and saving constructions.
- **OW:** O’Connor, Webster concurrence,492U.S.490,522–531(1989), `webster.json`, opinion1, plus her expressly joined Court parts. Necessary decision, medically prudent viability tests, construction limits and funding.
- **OH/OHS:** O’Connor, Hodgson concurrence,497U.S.417,458–461(1990), and her joins to Stevens PartsI,II,IV–VII, `hodgson.json`, opinions1/0. She refuses Stevens PartIII’s general framework and PartVIII’s rejection of bypass; the latter is not her position.
- **OG:** O’Connor, Mississippi University for Women v.Hogan,458U.S.718(1982), `hogan.json`, opinion0. Sex classifications, State justification, actual fit and rejection of stereotyped roles; limited to the claim presented.
- **OM/KM:** O’Connor concurrence joined Kennedy, Michael H.v.Gerald D.,491U.S.110,132(1989), `michael_h.json`, opinion1. Neither accepts a compulsory most-specific-level historical method for liberty.
- **KW:** Webster Court/plurality writing joined Kennedy,492U.S.490(1989), especially518–522 and note15, `webster.json`, opinion0. Protected liberty; unreasonable trimester machinery; useful regulation; central Roe left undisturbed.
- **KH:** Kennedy, Hodgson writing,497U.S.417,480–497(1990), `hodgson.json`, opinion4. Distinct minor/parent authority, notice versus consent, safeguards, bypass, ordinary reasonable regulation; express rejection of mothers as husbands’ agents.
- **KO:** Kennedy, Ohio v.Akron Center,497U.S.502(1990), `ohio_akron.json`, opinion0. Bypass safeguards and physician-to-parent notice. His PartV does not have O’Connor’s join.
- **KP:** Souter’s Payne concurrence joined Kennedy,501U.S.808,842–856(1991), `payne-souter.txt`. Constitutional error alone insufficient; special justification, demonstrated unworkability and confined overruling.
- **KJ:** Kennedy, Texas v.Johnson concurrence,491U.S.397,420–421(1989), `texas_johnson.json`, opinion1. Genuine expression remains protected despite a State’s powerful competing commitments.

The following public sources are the actual simulated writings, as verified in `entering_law.md` and the frozen packet. **R:** Riggins/Foucha, outputCHUNK9: O’Connor protects established bodily refusal with supported justification and reasonable less intrusive means, while reserving narrower preventive regimes; Kennedy’s separate trial-medication rule and Foucha dissent insist on the particular constitutional setting. **L:** Lee, outputCHUNK13: both recognize concrete coercive pressure; mere offense is insufficient and adolescent school ceremony does not decide adult abortion. **M:** Medina, outputCHUNK13: O’Connor’s practical fairness versus Kennedy’s historically bounded procedural latitude; no universal State proof burden. **C:** Collins, outputCHUNK5: both require an actual constitutional source and affirmative deprivation rather than turning every harmful policy into a constitutional wrong. **Q/H:** Quill, outputCHUNK9, and Hilton, outputCHUNK3: severable precedent components and particular reliance, with different statutory/constitutional channels; O’Connor dissented in Hilton and Kennedy authored it. **X:** Hudson, outputCHUNK5, and Wright, outputCHUNK12: both distinguish a disposition from a broader nonmajority framework. A row’s continuity code invokes these exact limits, not a substitute abortion rule.

## Statutory and record qualifications used throughout

The complete statutory quotation in the frozen packet controls. This capsule identifies the qualifications this annex actually uses; none comes from a historical Supreme Court disposition.

- **Emergency:** §3203 defines a condition which, on the physician’s good-faith clinical judgment, necessitates immediate abortion to avert death or for which delay creates serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. The Third Circuit construes the risk prospectively to encompass the three diagnoses at onset without waiting for catastrophe. That is a federal lower-court construction under review, not a Pennsylvania supreme-court holding. Section3205(b) requires an explanation before emergency abortion **if possible**. Section3205(c) separately protects failure to furnish information if the physician proves by a preponderance a reasonable belief that furnishing it would severely adversely affect the patient’s physical or mental health. It does not excuse waiting generally. Sources: supplied statute;744F.Supp.1323, findings152–174;947F.2d682 emergency discussion. The supplied appendix’s penalty label “(e)” is identified by both lower courts as an OCR label for(c).
- **Counseling:** §3205(a)(1) oral procedure/material-risk/alternative, probable-gestational-age and childbirth-risk information must come from the performing or referring physician at least24hours earlier. Under(a)(2), either physician or expressly delegated qualified physician assistant, practitioner, technician or social worker supplies the three notices. The free materials are available only if she chooses to review them; under(a)(3) she receives a copy if she chooses; no acceptance, reading or endorsement is required. Assistance **may** be available. Paternal support includes the father who offered to pay for abortion; **“In the case of rape, this information may be omitted.”** This is permission, with no reporting condition. Section3205(a)(4) certifies receipt, not assent. Section3208 requires objective/nonjudgmental scientific content and annual updates. Sources: supplied §§3205/3208; district findings181–189 identify omissions and some listed agencies’ misinformation, not universal falsity; findings190–213 establish delivery/delay costs and qualified counselors.
- **Parents:** §3206 applies to an unemancipated minor under18 and separately an adjudged incompetent; no developed independent adult-incompetence challenge is supplied. Ordinarily one parent consents; only the child’s best interests may inform that consent. If father participated in incest, mother alone suffices. If both parents have died or are otherwise unavailable within reasonable time/manner, guardian consent suffices; after divorce custodial-parent consent suffices; if no parent/guardian is available within reasonable time/manner, an adult in place of the parent may consent. Medical emergency excuses. Minor may elect court without first approaching either parent: maturity/capacity plus actual informed consent, **or** best interests, authorizes abortion. Counsel is provided unless knowingly/intelligently waived or private counsel chosen; confidentiality, three-business-day trial ruling and five-business-day appeal protect effective access. Sources: statutory appendix and947F.2d682 statutory quotations. The statute does not expressly say “clinic visit”; district findings232,238–259 describe intended practice/operation; Third Circuit note23 leaves that interpretation undecided. Finding266 supplies no current implementation-failure proof.
- **Spouse:** §3209 requires a signed, nonnotarized notice statement with a false-statement warning. A signed, nonnotarized substitute certifying at least one condition suffices: spouse is not father; diligent effort failed to locate spouse; pregnancy resulted from §3128 spousal sexual assault reported to a jurisdictionally competent law-enforcement agency; or reason to believe notice likely causes bodily injury to the woman by spouse or another. Medical emergency separately excuses. Section3128(a)–(b), quoted in district finding268, covers sexual/deviate intercourse by forcible compulsion, qualifying threat of compulsion, or with an unconscious spouse; finding297 identifies the incorporated90-day reporting restriction. Neither psychological/economic coercion, injury to children, publicity, nor divorce/custody retaliation is a general exception. Sources: supplied §3209 and district findings268,273–308. This reporting requirement never attaches to the rape permission in counseling.
- **Reports:** §3214(a) excludes patient names. Referrer identity under(a)(1) is separate from performing/concurring/second-physician and facility identities; (a)(8) documents necessity under §3211(b)(1), concerning abortions at24or more weeks; (a)(10) documents the emergency invoked to excuse Act compliance; (a)(11) incorporates §3210(a)’s actual inquiries, examinations, tests and diagnostic basis, distinct from the gestational-age result in(a)(5). Section3210(a) excuses compliance when medical emergency prevents it and otherwise requires only what a prudent physician considers necessary. Section3214(a)(12) contains marital status, notice and nonnotice reason. District findings324–387 establish protected confidential collection, two actual prospective referral refusals, no general inability to meet demand, and distinct concerns over medical-judgment reports. No patient-name disclosure is inferred.
- **Public access:** §3207(b) makes facility/affiliation reports public if the facility received State-appropriated funds within12calendar months before the inspection/copying **request**; without such funds, access is limited to law-enforcement officials and the two medical boards for official duties. Section3214(f) makes quarterly total/trimester reports public only if funds were received within12calendar months before **filing**; a facility’s no-funding representation is confidential unless contrary evidence establishes funding. Only public release is challenged; collection remains. Sources: supplied statutory text and both lower courts’ separate release discussions. Act64 §6 and1Pa.Cons.Stat.§1925 supply broad provision/application severability.

## O’Connor — framework and grounds frozen before any proposed writing

| Component | Two lawful paths and strongest competing case | Selected commitment, own authority and continuity | Join barrier and minimum narrower position |
|---|---|---|---|
| Protected constitutional choice | A: eliminate Roe because its implementation is flawed and constitutional error is correctable. B: retain the protected decision while repairing rules actually at issue; an unsatisfactory implementation does not refute the liberty itself. | **B. Retain constitutional protection for the previability decision and the operative prohibition on its destruction.** OA/OT did not ask to erase the core; OW deliberately left it standing; OH accepts reproductive liberty; OM rejects a mechanically restrictive history test. No new premise demonstrates the core unworkable. Q/H allow component-specific treatment; C demands this established source. | Cannot join a declaration that abortion is wholly outside due-process liberty, or an endorsement of every Roe sentence. Minimum: retained protected choice, with regulatory implementation separately specified. |
| Trimester machinery | A: preserve strict calendar compartments because they administer a fundamental right. B: replace them because changing medical facts and detailed judicial supervision make them unsound. | **B. Reject trimester machinery as the exclusive regulator of permissible legislation.** OA/OT are direct; OW’s necessary-decision restraint is overcome here because delivery, delay and persuasion restrictions squarely require a choice. The strongest A objection—loss of the right—fails if its core remains separately protected. Q/X support proposition separation. | Cannot join unconditional preservation of the first-trimester immunity or a statement that potential-life interests arise only later. Minimum: regulate throughout pregnancy under the selected burden test while retaining choice. |
| Viability and health limits | A: discard every viability distinction because OA questioned its biological/conceptual basis. B: distinguish when interests exist from whether a State can prohibit the protected choice; retain Roe’s operative previability protection and postviability life/health boundary without approving calendar-based scrutiny. | **B, bounded.** OW applied rather than abolished the existing viability boundary; OA’s criticism does not itself decide a new comprehensive ban. Medical judgment must protect timely health care. R supplies a source-specific bodily-liberty analogy, not a fresh abortion formula. | Cannot join “no legitimate potential-life interest before viability,” or claim every postviability issue was decided here. Minimum: interests throughout; previability choice remains protected; this case does not validate all later restrictions. |
| Initial burden threshold | A: any meaningful cost, delay or deterrence triggers a balancing inquiry. B: first determine whether regulation severely limits access or imposes an absolute obstacle; modest burdens receive ordinary rational review. | **B.** OA and OT explicitly require the severe threshold; OH reaffirms it. Trial evidence is now final and must be taken seriously, but full evidence does not change the legal threshold. Her Riggins rule is confined to established involuntary medication; M expressly blocks a universal transfer. | **Cannot join meaningful-burden balancing as the general threshold, nor weighing the State’s interests while deciding whether the initial threshold is crossed.** Minimum: factual severity first, justification second. |
| Consequence of a severe obstacle | A: every substantial obstacle is automatically invalid, whatever justification. B: severe burden triggers exacting scrutiny and the State may still show compelling justification, except where an independently categorical constitutional rule forbids the condition. | **B.** OA/OT expressly preserve possible justification, including maternal-health interests; OH does not replace that structure. A is strongest where the State gives another person control of the protected choice; that is a distinct anti-veto principle, not authority to erase the general justification stage. R/M confirm context limits. | **Cannot join an unqualified automatic-invalidity formulation for all substantial obstacles.** Can join invalidation of an effective third-party veto on this record and unjustified severe burdens. Minimum: retain compelling justification and state any categorical exception separately. |
| State justification and fit below severity | A: once meaningful burden is proved, State must demonstrate adequate fit and actual justification. B: below severity a reasonable relation to a legitimate interest suffices, without judicial selection of the least costly policy. | **B.** OA/OT reject cost-minimization and ordinary strict scrutiny; OH still permits rational review to have teeth against a measure unrelated to legitimate purposes. R/M do not move the abortion proof burden. | Cannot join automatic State evidentiary burden for every meaningful burden or a universal least-restrictive-means test. Minimum: plaintiffs establish severe interference; State carries compelling justification then; genuine rationality remains required below. |
| Purpose, persuasion and medical accuracy | A: preferring childbirth proves unconstitutional purpose. B: State may encourage childbirth through truthful, relevant information while coercion, false ideology or denial of choice remains separately forbidden. | **B.** OA/OT distinguish legitimate informed deliberation from ideological compulsion; OW joined recognition of governmental preference in funding. Actual directory defects must be analyzed rather than dismissed. L distinguishes actual coercion from mere disagreement. | Cannot join a rule invalidating all birth-favoring regulation or immunizing inaccurate/ideological compulsion. Minimum: truthful relevant notices, independent speech review, and access protection. |
| Facial population and proof | A: lawful use for voluntarily notifying women defeats facial notice relief. B: evaluate the legal condition on those it actually compels, using established findings rather than dilution by unaffected patients. | **B for the notice condition; otherwise require demonstrated class-wide operation sufficient for the challenged rule, not a hypothetical bad application.** OHS accepts real family operation; OA/OW reject speculative access claims. B does not abandon facial discipline for every regulation. L supports examination of actual compulsion; C requires State attribution. | Cannot join “any hardship invalidates the statute,” a universal numerical denominator, or automatic Salerno immunity for a condition that compels unconstitutional third-party control. Minimum: source-specific operative population and narrow severance. |
| Stare decisis | A: uphold all subsidiary precedents because Roe is fundamental; or erase the core because machinery was mistaken. B: identify the particular rule, its workability and reliance, and change only what the demonstrated defect reaches. | **B. Retain the constitutional foundation; modify Akron/Thornburgh insofar as they forbid these otherwise permissible regulations by categorical trimester or anti-persuasion rules.** OA/OT supply specific criticisms; OW guards against unnecessary overruling. Q permits simultaneous correction and adherence, while her H dissent warns that separability must be shown. Settled reproductive planning supports adherence to the core; no quantified empirical reliance finding is invented. | Cannot join “error alone always suffices,” “constitutional precedent never changes,” or an independent legitimacy/controversy rule insulating a case because public opposition persists. Minimum: distinct doctrinal defect, actual scope, ordinary reliance and reasoned continuity. |

## O’Connor — each separable provision and alternative ground

| Component | Two lawful paths and strongest counterargument | Frozen disposition and concrete sufficient ground | Join barrier; minimum narrower formulation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Medical emergency, §3203 and dependent exemptions | A: invalidate because significant risks outside death/permanent injury may be left unprotected and federal construction does not bind Pennsylvania courts. B: sustain the prospective good-faith clinical construction, which permits immediate treatment of the three diagnoses at onset. | **Sustain as construed.** OW’s prudent saving construction and OT’s health-risk construction are primary. The construction addresses the trial’s concrete dangers without treating a disclosure defense as a delay exception. If State authorities later construe otherwise, OW permits renewed challenge. R/C support source-specific protection. | Refuse a narrow wait-for-catastrophe reading or a ruling that every health risk is exempt. Minimum: express prospective construction, prompt care for the three conditions, future contrary state construction remains reviewable. |
| Emergency vagueness — threshold and merits reach | A: reach a freestanding vagueness holding and affirm the Third Circuit because good-faith clinical judgment gives sufficiently definite guidance. B: decide the developed health-protection/construction challenge without making a separate constitutional vagueness rule or resolving the officials’ disputed waiver characterization. | **B. No independent vagueness merits holding and no independent vagueness relief.** OW’s necessary-decision restraint and OT’s procedural precision are the first anchors. The district did not decide vagueness; the Third Circuit rejected it; providers principally develop health protection and the legal adequacy of the saving construction. Sustaining that construction resolves the presented request for emergency-definition relief without inventing another developed claim. C/X require exact claim/ground boundaries. | Refuse either a finding that providers unequivocally conceded/waived every vagueness contention or a new universal rule that any good-faith standard defeats vagueness. Minimum: identify the lower-court treatment, decide the developed construction/health question, and expressly leave a standalone vagueness holding unreached. |
| Emergency explanation, §3205(b), and disclosure defense, §3205(c) | A: invalidate the explanation as delaying urgent care or find the defense substitutes for a health exception. B: give independent effect to “prior to the abortion if possible,” and the defense’s reasonable-belief/preponderance/severely-adverse-health terms. | **Sustain; no separate license to delay emergency treatment and no broadening of the defense.** OA/OT permit responsible information; OW construction avoids invented obstacles. Providers concede the defense exists, while their lesser-harm argument remains part of the general burden analysis. R/M prohibit transplanting a universal State proof allocation. | Refuse treating mental-health protection as a general waiting waiver, or declaring every less-severe adverse response irrelevant to all constitutional claims. Minimum: preserve the defense precisely and evaluate actual access/speech independently. |
| Medical-information core, §3205(a)(1)(i)–(iii) | A: invalidate imposed dialogue under Akron/Thornburgh; B: sustain material procedure/alternatives, probable gestational age and childbirth risks as ordinary informed consent. | **Sustain content.** OA/OT directly accept relevant medical information and contest categorical abortion exceptionalism. Full-record findings185–189 support individualized consent; they do not invalidate medical content. R protects patient choice, not a right to uninformed treatment. | Refuse approval of irrelevant, inaccurate or ideological speech, or conflation with who speaks and when. Minimum: accurate material medical content, preserving clinical judgment and independent delivery/timing review. |
| Physician-only oral delivery, §3205(a)(1) | A: invalidate because trained counselors are competent and doctor scheduling creates extra costs. B: sustain the legislature’s assignment of medical responsibility despite a less costly alternative. | **Sustain separately.** OA expressly defended physician delivery; OW distinguishes increased cost from severe access obstruction. Findings204–213 establish the real additional demand but not a severe loss sufficient to displace ordinary reasonable regulation. OH’s parent context is not the sole justification. M/C forbid converting policy disagreement into a new right. | Cannot join a finding counselors are incompetent or a universal physician-only rule. Minimum: reasonable assignment on this record, with later demonstrated severe access restriction reviewable. |
| Notice of state materials, §3205(a)(2)(i) | A: invalidate mandatory mention as compelled promotion of defective agencies. B: sustain a factual notice of the right to receive free optional materials without endorsing their contents or agencies. | **Sustain.** OT expressly leaves room for actual inaccurate/inflammatory material, now examined through findings181–189. Omission of agencies and some agencies’ conduct do not make the notice false; physicians may recommend avoiding an agency. OA supports accurate information. L distinguishes persuasion from coercion. | Refuse a blanket finding that every material is accurate or an immunity for later compelled misinformation. Minimum: notice of availability and choice only; no physician endorsement. |
| Assistance notice, §3205(a)(2)(ii) | A: invalidate because some patients cannot receive benefits and the information seeks childbirth. B: sustain the accurate statement that benefits **may** be available and detailed information exists. | **Sustain.** OA/OT permit relevant nonmedical consequences of choice; OW permits governmental childbirth preference within constitutional limits. The statutory conditional wording defeats an asserted guarantee of payment. R/C do not constitutionalize every incomplete counseling resource. | Refuse to describe benefits as guaranteed or to require acceptance of State policy. Minimum: conditional factual notice, allowing physician explanation of actual eligibility. |
| Paternal-support notice, §3205(a)(2)(iii) | A: invalidate as insensitive or misleading for women whose partners cannot/will not pay; B: sustain a statement of legal liability, including offers to fund abortion, with rape omission permitted. | **Sustain.** OA/OT distinguish useful legal information from an endorsement of private conduct. “In the case of rape, this information may be omitted” is permission, no reporting condition; practical inability to collect does not falsify liability. R/L leave actual coercive delivery reviewable. | Refuse obligatory omission, a police-report condition, or assurance of actual collection. Minimum: accurately state liability and preserve the statutory permission and independent health defense. |
| Optional copy, §3205(a)(3), and §3208 content safeguards | A: invalidate because optionality cannot cure inflammatory misinformation. B: sustain the woman’s chosen receipt under objective/nonjudgmental scientific requirements while permitting correction and advice about directory defects. | **Sustain this facial challenge.** OT requires actual-content inquiry, now satisfied without assuming universal accuracy. Findings183–184 permit professional correction; no compelled reading or agreement occurs. OA supports informed choice; L protects against actual compulsion. | Refuse “optional means constitutionally immune,” mandatory reading, or endorsement of agency claims. Minimum: optional receipt only, without foreclosure of a proved inaccurate/ideological application. |
| Receipt certification, §3205(a)(4) | A: invalidate as compelled affirmation of a State viewpoint or as inseparable from invalid counseling. B: sustain documentation that required information was provided. | **Sustain as a receipt record, with only valid underlying duties incorporated.** OA/OT’s informed-consent ground supports verification; it cannot certify agreement or mandatory review of unchosen material. X requires disposition-by-component precision. | Refuse a pledge of assent or certification of nonexistent/invalid obligations. Minimum: truthful receipt certification for duties this decision sustains; sever an invalid link if another coalition invalidates it. |
| Fixed24-hour interval | A: invalidate under Akron and the trial’s two trips,48-hour-to-two-week practical delays, expense and increased procedure risk. B: sustain reasonable deliberation without proven severe access obstruction. | **Sustain.** OA directly supplies both rational deliberation and distinction between increased cost and undue restriction; OT preserves that test. Findings are accepted, not reduced to a literal24-hour inconvenience. OHS PartVI/note35 recognizes the existing adult Akron rule but does not retract OA; this case squarely permits its limited correction. L/R require real effects, not automatic invalidity from them. | Refuse to call all delay medically beneficial or trivial. Minimum: rational deliberation on this record under the prospective emergency construction, with severe applications and medically necessary immediate care protected. |
| One-parent informed consent, §3206(a)–(b) | A: invalidate because adding parental information/permission restricts a minor’s protected choice. B: sustain the distinct parental-guidance interest with the statutory alternatives and adequate bypass. | **Sustain.** OA and OH/OHS accept one-parent involvement; their family-privacy concern is not an unconditional right of every minor to exclude all guidance. KO’s joined majority reinforces constitutional bypass safeguards. R/C require attention to this minor-specific authority. | Refuse parental absolute veto, mandatory approach to a parent before court, or a newly adjudicated adult-incompetence rule. Minimum: one-parent informed consent subject to each statutory substitute and effective alternative authorization. |
| Added in-person parental attendance operation | A: invalidate/remand because a willing parent unable to travel should not force redundant judicial proceedings. B: sustain even the district’s operation because informed parental participation is reasonable and bypass supplies independent access. | **B: reject this facial ground, without construing the statute to command attendance.** OH upheld bypass despite evidence that some minors already had a consenting parent and suffered court burdens; she refused Stevens VIII’s contrary conclusion. That is stronger than the analogy that bypass is useful only for an abusive second parent. OT demands full facts, now considered. M recognizes adequate alternative procedure can matter. | Refuse the fiction that attendance is express statutory text or that bypass eliminates every cost. Minimum: assume the found operation for decision, sustain with genuine bypass, leave Pennsylvania construction open. |
| Judicial bypass, §3206(c)–(f),(h) | A: presume implementation delay or treat court as an unlawful replacement parent. B: sustain mature/informed-consent **or** best-interests authorization, counsel, confidentiality and prompt decisions. | **Sustain its sufficiency; no independent internal challenge warrants broader review.** OH and her KO joins are direct. Finding266 supplies no current malfunction; mere possible bad administration cannot defeat the facial rule. M permits adequate safeguarded procedure. | Refuse an exhaustion requirement, cumulative maturity-and-best-interests test, or invented unreadiness finding. Minimum: statutory alternatives remain independent and practically effective; actual denial remains challengeable. |
| Spousal notice, §3209 — abortion-liberty ground | A: sustain formal notice as legitimate spousal participation, stressing voluntary notice and the full statutory substitutes. B: invalidate the legal condition because the proven coercive relationships make it an effective third-party control over protected choice. | **Invalidate and sever.** OA’s severe-obstacle protection and OHS’s refusal to impose an ideal family communication structure apply; husband has no parental authority over an adult wife. The operative group is women compelled to disclose, not women who already do so voluntarily. Financial/psychological/child-directed coercion and inability to use the danger certification remain after the exact exceptions. The State’s interest in voluntary consultation does not justify this control. L supports actual-pressure analysis; C is satisfied by the State-imposed disclosure condition. | Refuse analogy to parent-minor consent or a general declaration every unwanted disclosure is severe. Minimum: this record’s effective third-party control of abortion, with the precise exception limits and no compelled spousal authorization. |
| Spousal marital/informational-privacy ground | A: treat marriage as sufficient justification for governmental disclosure duties. B: protect adult decisional and intimate-communication autonomy against imposed disclosure used to enforce another’s control. | **Join a narrow independently sufficient adult-family-privacy ground tied to this condition and record.** OHS PartsV/VII protect actual family decision authority against a legislated communication ideal; OM rejects automatic confinement to the narrowest historical tradition. This is not a generalized secrecy entitlement. R/L reinforce real State interference. | Refuse a universal rule that spouses may never be required to disclose legally relevant information. Minimum: private reproductive communication as a prerequisite to protected medical choice, with this coercive record. |
| Spousal equal-protection ground | A: pregnancy’s biological difference and a father’s interest justify asymmetric notice. B: the law additionally allocates a marital entitlement to husbands and an affirmative medical-choice burden to wives without adequate fit, beyond biological classification. | **Join narrow independently sufficient sex-equality invalidation.** OG requires persuasive important-objective/substantial-fit justification and rejects stereotyped roles; OHS separates actual parental responsibility from imposed control. The State has not connected compulsory disclosure in these relationships to a legitimate equality-consistent marital goal strongly enough; biological difference does not authorize a husband’s supervisory entitlement. R/C require this distinct State allocation, not a claim all pregnancy rules are sex discrimination. | Refuse a holding that every pregnancy classification triggers the same result, or that fatherhood has no legitimate interests. Minimum: the asymmetric adult marital-control condition, applying Hogan’s fit analysis and this record. |
| Spousal compelled-speech theory — independent reach | A: reach a sufficiently preserved First Amendment objection because the State conditions treatment on a wife’s communication of an intimate fact, regardless of the separate abortion-liberty doctrine. B: leave the independent speech theory and its disputed preservation undecided because the selected liberty/privacy/equality grounds already afford complete notice relief. | **B. No independent spousal First Amendment holding; no merits rejection or waiver ruling.** OA/OT identify genuine compelled-ideology concerns, so the argument is not dismissed merely because the communication concerns medical care. OW’s necessary-decision restraint nevertheless controls: the actual statute does not prescribe a personally uttered ideological formula, and this case needs no additional general compelled-communication rule after notice is invalidated on the selected grounds. Kolbert’s Tr.51–52 argument establishes oral presentation; O’Connor’s Tr.36–38 questions do not establish a vote or settle preservation. C/X require a distinct source and exact reach. | Refuse a statement that spousal speech was never argued, that the theory was conclusively waived, or that the Court decided all spousal notification duties constitutional under the First Amendment. Minimum: acknowledge the distinct argued theory, preserve its independent merits/preservation questions, and provide full relief on the adopted grounds. |
| Independent speech: medical core | A: treat licensed professional speech as wholly commercial and freely compellable. B: recognize First Amendment protection but sustain accurate material medical information necessary to informed treatment. | **Reject the independent facial speech challenge on B’s narrow ground.** OA/OT reserve ideological compulsion but distinguish truthful informed consent. The providers did not concede all speech commercial. R protects informed choice; L does not transform ordinary information into coerced ideological assent. | Refuse blanket commercial-speech classification or immunity from First Amendment review. Minimum: factual, relevant medical disclosure without required endorsement. |
| Independent speech: materials notice, assistance, paternal support, chosen copy, certification | A: invalidate each as a compulsory pro-childbirth message. B: distinguish factual availability/liability notices, chosen receipt and receipt certification from adopting State ideology. | **Reject facial speech relief for each listed component separately.** OA/OT’s possible compelled-ideology concern receives an answer: no requirement to assert a moral belief, endorse an agency, guarantee assistance/collection, read unchosen material or assent. Some listed-agency defects do not change what the speaker must say. Omission in rape remains permitted, without reporting. L/C supply the boundary against viewpoint-based attribution alone. | Refuse approval of compelled ideological or demonstrably false content. Minimum: each precise factual notice and receipt act, physician disagreement/correction allowed, patient choice respected. |
| Referrer identity, §3214(a)(1) | A: invalidate because two doctors would stop referrals even with confidentiality and referral identity has little medical value. B: sustain identification of a participant who may furnish informed consent/gestational information and permit follow-up. | **Sustain the appealed referrer field.** OT demanded evidence rather than speculation; this record does establish limited referral refusal, but not severe access loss or irrationality of identifying participating medical sources. OW/OA distinguish cost/deterrence from undue burden. C prevents private reluctance alone from becoming an absolute constitutional immunity. | Refuse denial of the two-doctor finding or public disclosure of individual reports. Minimum: confidential identity for legitimate follow-up/enforcement, without declaring every conceivable source-identification demand valid. |
| Medical-necessity basis, §3214(a)(8) | A: invalidate because post24-week necessity judgments are chilled by retrospective scrutiny. B: sustain documenting why §3211(b)(1)’s necessity predicate was invoked. | **Sustain.** OT permits legitimate confidential records and professional accountability; OW allows useful regulation of medical judgment. Asking the physician’s basis does not itself change the substantive necessity rule or establish punishment for a reasonable decision. R/M distinguish a record requirement from moving every substantive proof burden. | Refuse characterization as a generic viability result or validation of all §3211 restrictions. Minimum: document this specific invoked predicate, retain clinical/legal safeguards and confidential handling. |
| Emergency basis, §3214(a)(10) | A: invalidate because documenting emergency may deter timely care. B: sustain retrospective documentation without requiring permission or delaying treatment. | **Sustain under the prospective emergency construction.** OA/OT/OW support medical regulation short of actual severe interference. No finding establishes that this confidential aftercare report itself prevents necessary care. R preserves substantive health protection. | Refuse preauthorization, immediate paperwork before treatment, or a narrower emergency definition. Minimum: after-the-fact basis for the actual exemption, without changing good-faith clinical discretion. |
| Diagnostic basis, §3214(a)(11) incorporating §3210(a) | A: invalidate intrusive judgment scrutiny despite unchallenged gestational-age result. B: sustain reporting actual inquiries/tests and basis under a prudent-physician standard. | **Sustain the appealed diagnostic-basis report.** OW’s useful, prudent testing is the closest anchor; OT opposes presumed invalidity of confidential medical documentation. This does not order additional unnecessary tests or reopen the sustained age-result field. R/C preserve the source and scope distinction. | Refuse conflation with(a)(5), fixed mandated tests, or loss of the emergency-prevents-compliance qualification. Minimum: actual prudent diagnostic process and basis only. |
| Notice-linked report, §3214(a)(12) | A: retain all information because reporting is independently useful. B: remove notice/nonnotice-reason fields which perpetuate the invalid disclosure condition; preserve separable marital-status collection. | **Sever notice and nonnotice-reason requirements; retain the marital-status field within otherwise unchallenged confidential collection.** OA/OT permit health data, while OH/OHS do not permit evasion of the substantive decision by a report demanding the same protected explanation. X supports separating the fields. | Refuse automatic invalidation of all marital statistics or compulsory reasons for exercising the right to withhold spousal disclosure. Minimum: marital status alone, no notice-compliance enforcement. |
| Public facility/affiliation access, §3207(b) | A: invalidate because real harassment fears induce funding refusal and likely harm indigent access. B: sustain public accountability for facilities receiving State funds; no patient identity is exposed and incremental severe access effect is not demonstrated. | **Sustain public release on this record.** OW’s funding holdings and OA/OT’s access threshold permit a rational transparency condition. The full findings of harassment and funding refusal are accepted; they do not establish the needed severe causal effect of these particular public reports. This is not a declaration that every funding condition is valid. C requires the condition’s actual operation. | Refuse conflation with §3214(f)’s filing lookback or denial of harassment evidence. Minimum: facility/affiliation data with the12months-before-request trigger; collection remains separately lawful. |
| Public quarterly totals, §3214(f) | A: invalidate because disclosure could amplify harassment and funding covers services beyond abortions. B: sustain aggregate accountability for a recipient of State funds without patient identification. | **Sustain separately.** OW and OA/OT support rational transparency absent severe demonstrated incremental obstruction. A nexus to public support need not be dollar-for-dollar abortion reimbursement to survive this review. The no-funding representation receives the statute’s confidentiality treatment. C/M retain distinct constitutional channels. | Refuse treating funding as waiver of every right or using §3207(b)’s request date. Minimum: aggregate totals/trimester report with12months-before-filing trigger and contrary-evidence rule. |
| Unappealed confidential collection/gestational ascertainment | A: reopen the entire scheme because framework changes. B: respect providers’ failure to cross-appeal the district-sustained items and the limited public-release challenge. | **Leave unappealed collection and prudent gestational ascertainment in place; do not newly adjudicate every item.** OW’s necessary-decision rule is direct; OT’s procedural caution and X prohibit expanding the case through generic labels. | Refuse a new omnibus constitutional holding or manufactured claim. Minimum: identify untouched scope and decide only appealed fields/dependencies. |
| Severance, mandate and conditional alternative-theory remand | A: strike the whole Act or remand every alternative constitutional foundation merely because the trimester rules change. B: sever invalid notice-related duties, affirm the other appealed dispositions on the grounds above and remand for conforming relief. | **B.** OW/OT favor limited necessary adjudication; retained constitutional choice removes the providers’ stated contingency for a wholesale remand of alternative foundations. Independently developed speech/equality/privacy grounds receive the treatment recorded above. Act64 severability permits unaffected provisions/applications to operate. Q/H demand actual separability, not a label. | Refuse a broad remand suggesting Roe protection was abandoned, or an injunction reaching unappealed requirements. Minimum: precise notice/dependent-report relief and ordinary conforming proceedings. |

## Kennedy — framework and grounds frozen before any proposed writing

| Component | Two lawful paths and strongest competing case | Selected commitment, own authority and continuity | Join barrier and minimum narrower position |
|---|---|---|---|
| Protected constitutional choice | A: because Roe is controversial and constitutionally debatable, replace it with ordinary legislative choice. B: retain the established due-process liberty while resolving the particular restrictions. | **B. Retain constitutional protection for the previability decision and reject comprehensive extinguishment of that choice.** KW expressly recognizes liberty and leaves Roe’s central holding undisturbed; KM rejects a mechanically narrow history rule; KP requires special justification beyond disagreement. No demonstrated defect in the central prohibition rule supplies it here. R/C respect defined constitutional channels. | Refuse a declaration of no constitutional protection or wholesale endorsement of Roe’s implementation. Minimum: protected choice survives; particular regulations receive separate examination. |
| Trimester implementation | A: retain the rigid calendar because it secures the choice. B: replace it because it makes medical details constitutional rules and wrongly confines potential-life interests to later pregnancy. | **B.** KW is explicit, including criticism of delivery distinctions and intricacy; KH/KO permit meaningful minor regulation. Q/H demonstrate that correcting implementation need not erase a distinct core. | Refuse a rule that all previability potential-life regulation is forbidden or that medicine’s calendar alone selects scrutiny. Minimum: legitimate interests throughout pregnancy, subject to the protected decision. |
| Viability and health | A: erase viability entirely because KW criticizes its role in the framework. B: retain the operative choice/prohibition distinction while permitting reasonable earlier regulation; preserve medical life/health protection. | **B, limited to the retained governing boundary.** KW distinguishes rejection of a rigid regulatory timetable from reopening Roe’s comprehensive-ban holding and sustains medically useful testing. This does not decide every postviability restriction. R shows that medical liberty requires its own precise channel. | Refuse to equate potential-life interest with a power to extinguish previability choice, or to approve all later restrictions. Minimum: regulatory interests can precede viability; the retained protected decision remains real. |
| General burden framework | A: announce a comprehensive new substantial-obstacle test for every regulation. B: retain a narrower rule: reasonable measures furthering legitimate interests are permissible when they preserve effective choice; a State may not create an effective third-party veto, and demonstrated serious interference requires analysis against the particular protected right. | **B.** KW never adopts a universal undue-burden formula; KH’s no-absolute-obstacle reasoning is tied to minor/parent authority and safeguards. R/Foucha/M insist that one setting’s test not migrate without justification. The present judgments can be resolved without freezing a new universal burden scale. | **Cannot join an unqualified general formula merely bearing “undue burden” or “substantial obstacle.”** Can join protected choice, reasonable nonpreclusive regulation and the specific anti-veto rule. Broader agreement would require a recorded reconciliation explaining the actual new proposition. |
| Smaller meaningful burdens, fit and proof | A: place State proof of adequate justification/fit on every meaningful burden. B: sustain reasonable nonpreclusive regulation without turning all cost or discouragement into heightened review. | **B.** KW expressly accepts useful costs and regulated medical judgment; KH rejects perfect-fit demands and KO rejects hypothetical administrative worst cases. Riggins’s extraordinary trial showing and M’s State procedural latitude are both context-specific. | Refuse a universal meaningful-burden evidentiary shift or least-restrictive-means requirement. Minimum: genuine reasonable relationship, actual record effects and independent constitutional limits. |
| Severe obstacles and State interests | A: declare any substantial obstacle automatically invalid under all circumstances. B: distinguish extinguishing the protected decision or giving another person control from a genuine health regulation needing its own analysis. | **B. The effective third-party veto here is forbidden; no universal automatic-invalidity rule is selected for every severe medical regulation.** KW’s medically prudent construction and KH’s safeguards make the distinction necessary. Mere potential-life preference cannot explain an external veto over the retained choice. R/M supply a methodological check only. | Refuse a formulation which silently decides every serious health regulation. Minimum: this anti-veto rule and justified, nonpreclusive regulation; a broader general test is an unresolved doctrinal choice, not a frozen joinder. |
| Purpose and independent rights | A: treat childbirth encouragement as sufficient proof of impermissible purpose, or license any regulation with that purpose. B: accept truthful encouragement while separately enforcing protected choice and genuine speech/privacy limits. | **B.** KW allows a governmental preference; KJ shows strong public commitments cannot defeat an independently established constitutional command. KH protects family participation for a particular legitimate reason, not at any cost. L distinguishes actual pressure from mere offense. | Refuse either categorical anti-persuasion or categorical immunity. Minimum: identify the precise message, effect and constitutional source. |
| Facial proof/operative population | A: voluntary notice by most wives defeats relief under broad facial-challenge language. B: the challenged legal compulsion operates on those who would not notify; its proven control cannot be diluted by unaffected relationships. | **B for spousal compulsion, with KO’s ordinary facial discipline retained elsewhere.** KH’s distinction between minors and adults and its reliance on usable safeguards mean its ordinary notice defense does not decide this case. L’s public pressure ground rejects formal freedom when State conditions make it unreal. C supplies affirmative State attribution. | Refuse a general “one hard case is enough” rule or a denial that KO requires more than hypothetical maladministration. Minimum: actual defined condition, proved operative class, tailored relief. |
| Stare decisis | A: constitutional error alone permits repudiation; or all Roe applications are inseparable. B: require special justification and determine which component its evidence reaches. | **B.** KP expressly requires special justification; KW demonstrates one for trimester machinery while leaving the core. H’s separable statutory holding and Q’s precise reliance-based adherence confirm methodological consistency but are not direct constitutional analogues. Settled ability to order intimate life supports keeping the core; that is an inference from the protected legal arrangement and advocates’ argument, not a new trial finding. | Refuse error-only overruling, immutable precedent, or a separate rule making controversy itself decisive. Minimum: worked defect and scope, respect for settled liberty, no invented quantitative reliance or institutional history. |

## Kennedy — each separable provision and alternative ground

| Component | Two lawful paths and strongest counterargument | Frozen disposition and concrete sufficient ground | Join barrier; minimum narrower formulation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Medical emergency, §3203/dependent exemptions | A: invalidate because only state courts can finally settle the definition and some health risks remain outside it. B: sustain prospective good-faith clinical treatment of serious risk and the three diagnoses without waiting for damage. | **Sustain as construed.** KW construes testing to avoid dangerous/irrelevant procedures and preserves clinical judgment; KO accepts workable construction rather than worst-case assumptions. A later contrary authoritative construction remains a new problem. R/M reject shifting a different procedure’s rule here. | Refuse catastrophe-first enforcement or claiming federal interpretation binds state courts permanently. Minimum: operative prospective construction and preserved challenge to materially different enforcement. |
| Emergency vagueness — threshold and merits reach | A: affirm a standalone vagueness rejection based on the physician’s good-faith clinical judgment and prospective construction. B: resolve the developed construction/health issue without deciding an additional constitutional theory whose independent preservation/necessity is disputed. | **B. Do not reach an independent vagueness merits holding; grant no additional vagueness relief.** KW’s refusal to decide unnecessary constitutional questions and KO’s attention to the actual statutory challenge control the reach decision. The district did not decide vagueness and the Third Circuit rejected it, but the providers’ developed argument here principally concerns health protection and the saving construction. Neither lower-court history nor a governmental waiver assertion creates a new fully litigated Supreme Court holding. C/X preserve the distinction between affirming the practical disposition and adopting every lower-court rationale. | Refuse a manufactured concession/waiver ruling or categorical immunity for statutes employing medical judgment. Minimum: sustain the construction on the developed ground, acknowledge the appellate vagueness treatment, and reserve a separate vagueness merits rule. |
| Emergency explanation and separate information defense | A: strike possible interference with urgent care; B: enforce the explanation only before abortion **if possible** and the separate severe-adverse-health reasonable-belief/preponderance defense as written. | **Sustain these bounded provisions.** KW’s clinical-construction method and KO’s practical statutory reading apply. Neither requirement creates prior permission for emergency treatment, and the information defense does not supply a general waiting waiver. R protects actual medically necessary intervention. | Refuse a new defense broader than text or emergency care conditioned on paperwork. Minimum: distinct functions and precise terms; no compelled postponement. |
| Medical-information core, §3205(a)(1)(i)–(iii) | A: preserve Akron/Thornburgh’s categorical restriction on structured dialogue. B: sustain relevant procedure, risk/alternative, gestational-age and childbirth information. | **Sustain content.** KW directly criticizes exceptional invalidation of informed-consent regulation; KO recognizes informed medical participation. The patient’s retained liberty is consistent with information rather than physician insulation. R/C require this specific medical relationship. | Refuse false or irrelevant content, compulsory ideological assent, or a conflation with timing/delivery. Minimum: accurate relevant informed-consent facts. |
| Physician-only oral delivery | A: invalidate because qualified counselors do the work and physician timing increases cost. B: sustain accountable medical delivery despite an available cheaper policy. | **Sustain.** KW note15 directly rejects automatic constitutional significance of physician versus assistant delivery. KO distinguishes adult Akron from physician-to-parent notice, so its holding alone would not settle this; KW’s broader criticism supplies the independent basis. Findings204–213 show real cost but not effective denial. M/C permit bounded legislative choice. | Refuse to say KO alone overruled adult Akron or counselors lack competence. Minimum: rational assignment of this medical responsibility on the actual access record. |
| Materials-availability notice, §3205(a)(2)(i) | A: invalidate mandatory referral to a State persuasion program with defective directory entries. B: sustain factual notification of free optional information without agency endorsement. | **Sustain.** KW rejects categorical anti-dialogue reasoning; KJ distinguishes genuine ideological compulsion from factual notification. Findings181–184 preserve correction/avoidance advice and do not falsify availability. L does not treat offense as compulsion. | Refuse a conclusion all agency advice is accurate or mandatory endorsement. Minimum: availability/right-to-review notice alone with correction allowed. |
| Assistance notice, §3205(a)(2)(ii) | A: condemn one-sided encouragement or an illusory benefits promise. B: sustain accurate information that assistance **may** be available. | **Sustain.** KW permits childbirth preference through lawful nonpreclusive measures; the conditional fact is relevant to the decision. Actual eligibility can be explained. KJ prevents expansion to forced moral belief, while R/C keep the medical-choice source clear. | Refuse a guarantee of benefits, or a rule that any governmental preference defeats speech protection. Minimum: conditional factual information. |
| Paternal-support notice, §3205(a)(2)(iii) | A: invalidate because actual support may be unavailable and rape counseling may cause harm. B: sustain legal-liability information, with omission in rape permitted and actual collection unpromised. | **Sustain.** KW’s information rationale and KJ’s fact/ideology distinction apply. The statute says **“In the case of rape, this information may be omitted.”** It imposes no law-enforcement-report requirement; legal liability remains different from payment certainty. R/L leave actual coercion independently examinable. | Refuse mandatory omission, police reporting, guaranteed collection or compelled moral endorsement. Minimum: precise legal fact and statutory permission. |
| Chosen materials, §3205(a)(3)/§3208 | A: invalidate actual inaccurate/inflammatory material even though nominally optional. B: sustain chosen receipt under objective/nonjudgmental scientific requirements where this record does not show all State text false. | **Sustain facially, without immunity for actual compelled falsehood.** KW allows information; KJ preserves the independent limit. The woman need not choose, read or endorse it and the clinician may correct directory defects. L distinguishes the legal choice here from State-imposed coercion elsewhere. | Refuse “optional means never reviewable” or an assertion every publication complies. Minimum: voluntary receipt and truthful factual statutory administration. |
| Receipt certification, §3205(a)(4) | A: invalidate as compelled viewpoint affirmation. B: sustain truthful documentation of valid disclosures and chosen receipt. | **Sustain.** KW permits informed-consent administration; KJ would resist ideological endorsement, which this certification does not require. X demands that invalid underlying obligations, if any, not be restored through a form. | Refuse certification of agreement or that the patient read unchosen materials. Minimum: receipt alone, linked only to valid duties. |
| Fixed24-hour interval | A: invalidate actual travel, repeated visits, extended scheduling, expense and risk under Akron. B: allow a reasonable deliberation interval which does not on this record prevent the protected choice. | **Sustain.** KW permits useful regulation/cost and attacks rigid trimester implementation; KH accepts deliberation in the parental setting, whose distinct interest is acknowledged rather than mechanically transferred. Here the adult deliberation/potential-life rationale is independently reasonable; proven cost/delay does not establish effective denial. R/L require those effects be accepted, not dismissed. | Refuse the claim KH alone decides adult waiting or that all delay improves health. Minimum: this record’s reasonable nonpreclusive interval with prospective emergency protection. |
| One-parent informed consent | A: invalidate because the minor’s choice is constitutionally protected. B: recognize parents’ distinct child-welfare responsibility with an independent lawful bypass. | **Sustain.** KH and KO are direct and stronger than general KW deference: minors’ maturity and parental obligations are qualitatively different from marriage. The prescribed one-parent and substitution routes fit that purpose. R/C prevent extending this child-care authority to an adult spouse. | Refuse an absolute parental veto, requirement to approach parent first, or new adult-incompetence adjudication. Minimum: statutory parental routes plus independent maturity/best-interests authorization. |
| In-person parental attendance operation | A: invalidate/remand the redundant judicial burden where parent agrees but cannot attend. B: sustain the assumed operation as informed parental participation with bypass preserving access. | **B, without adopting a statutory attendance command.** KH upholds more demanding two-parent involvement and expressly treats bypass as curing even an assumed constitutional defect. KO tolerates practical administrative burdens with real safeguards. The consenting-parent distinction matters but does not make an adequate alternative unconstitutional. M permits distinct adequate procedural choices. | Refuse turning findings into statutory words or denying the added practical burden. Minimum: assume district operation, uphold with genuine bypass, leave state construction unresolved. |
| Bypass alternatives and administration | A: infer unacceptable delays or inadequate confidentiality from possible applications. B: sustain maturity/informed-consent **or** best interests with counsel, confidential proceedings and statutory expedition. | **Sustain; no independent internal defect is presented/proved.** KO’s full bypass analysis and KH are direct. Finding266 defeats invented present unreadiness. The statute supplies access without exhausting parental requests. M’s bounded procedural latitude is consistent. | Refuse cumulative criteria, invented exhaustion, or a promise hypothetical future failures are immune. Minimum: actual effective alternatives and prompt/confidential review. |
| Spousal notice — protected-choice ground | A: extend KH’s formal notice/consent distinction, rely on normal marriage and exact statutory substitutes, and deny facial relief for an unquantified minority. B: distinguish adult wife from minor and identify the State’s disclosure condition as enabling actual third-party prevention of a retained protected choice. | **Invalidate and sever.** KH rests on parents’ legal responsibility for minors, usable abuse/health protection and no absolute obstacle; it expressly rejects mothers as husbands’ agents. Those premises fail here. KW leaves the choice protected. The findings show a defined group for whom financial, psychological and child-directed coercion survive the bodily-injury/assault routes, and some cannot use the certification. L supports real-pressure analysis; C attributes the compulsory trigger to the State. This suffices without a new universal obstacle formula. | Refuse “notice always equals veto” or disregard of the complete exceptions. Minimum: this law’s actual adult reproductive-control condition, preserving voluntary consultation and the narrow protected-choice ground. |
| Spousal marital/informational privacy | A: recognize a general spousal entitlement to reproductive disclosure. B: protect adult decisional autonomy where compelled intimate disclosure is used to condition medical choice. | **Join the narrow decision-autonomy proposition as part of the sufficient protected-choice ground; reserve a separate general informational-privacy holding.** KM and KH reject automatic historical confinement and marital hierarchy; KW supplies the actual protected liberty. The record does not require defining a general secrecy right. R/C favor this bounded source. | Refuse a universal immunity from legally required marital disclosures. Minimum: no spouse’s State-enforced control over this protected medical decision; broader privacy doctrine left open. |
| Spousal equal protection | A: independently invalidate the husband’s asymmetric entitlement under sex-equality doctrine. B: decide the protected-choice claim and reserve how pregnancy, marital classification and intermediate scrutiny interact. | **B: do not supply an independently sufficient equal-protection vote.** KH’s repudiation of mothers as agents strongly supports A’s anti-hierarchy concern, but does not itself adopt a test for all pregnancy-linked marital classifications. KM preserves liberty analysis; no new equality holding is necessary to the judgment. This is a bounded reservation, not approval of unequal marital control. C/M/X require precise grounds. | Refuse a claim he affirmatively approved this classification or joined a generalized sex-equality rationale. Minimum: identify equality as preserved but unnecessary, while joining the decision-autonomy ground. |

B: reserve that independent theory and its unsettled preservation after the protected-choice ground supplies complete relief from the notice condition. | **B. The statute does not itself compel a personally uttered ideological formula, and identifying the governing compelled-private-communication standard is unnecessary here. Their combined boundary permits facts, not forced belief. No commercial-speech concession is attributed. No command requires endorsement of a listed agency or reading unchosen material; actual defects do not falsify the distinct required notices. Rape omission remains permitted without reporting. L supplies a pressure boundary, not automatic deference. | Refuse validation of false or ideological commands or an assertion every directory entry is accurate. Minimum: each exact factual command and free correction/disagreement. |

The trial proves particular deterrence, not general inability to obtain care or irrationality of the data link. C requires more than private reluctance for categorical immunity. | Refuse denial of those refusals, exposure of confidential individual reports, or a generic immunity for every reporting demand. Minimum: this confidential field and its legitimate follow-up purpose. |
| Necessity basis, §3214(a)(8) | A: invalidate because retrospective review chills difficult post24-week judgment. B: document the invoked §3211(b)(1) necessity predicate without altering it. | **Sustain.** KW specifically allows useful medical-accountability regulation consistent with reasonable judgment. The report records rather than prejudges lawful necessity. R/M distinguish documentation from a new substantive burden or prior authorization. | Refuse treating this as gestational-age data alone or endorsing all later-abortion restrictions. Minimum: precise basis field and protected professional/legal standards. |
| Emergency basis, §3214(a)(10) | A: invalidate possible chilling of emergency care. B: sustain a confidential after-the-fact account of why compliance was excused. | **Sustain.** KW’s medical-discretion construction and KO’s nonhypothetical method apply.
