## Freeze summary for later coalition work

### Explicit health-boundary and proposition-count clarification

The framework rows retain Roe’s governing limit on postviability proscription: abortion necessary, in appropriate medical judgment, to preserve the woman’s life or health remains constitutionally protected. They do **not** newly adjudicate every degree of medical risk, every method-selection dispute, all §3211 restrictions, or every postviability regulation. O’Connor’s OT expressly reserved when a health/fetal-survival tradeoff becomes undue after construing the particular risk as real and identifiable; nothing in this freeze silently closes that reservation. Kennedy’s KW used a construction avoiding medically irrelevant or dangerous testing, without adopting a universal risk-calibration rule. Both accept the prospective emergency construction described in their provision rows; neither treats the separate information-omission defense as a substitute for timely emergency care. The governing source is the retained Roe proposition as discussed/applied in their own OW/KW writings, with OT’s express reservation, rather than a new factual finding or a wholesale joinder to Roe’s reasons.

Each reusable proposition must receive its own express count at assembly. These two Justices’ support for protected previability choice and the retained life/health limit can be counted only at that exact level; their matching dispositions on many regulations cannot be counted as joinders to a shared comprehensive framework. O’Connor’s severity-then-compelling-justification approach and Kennedy’s selected narrower contextual rules are different rationales. Neither is automatically a logical subset of the other merely because both sustain the same provisions here. Any eventual Marks analysis must show the actual rationales and entailment across their covered cases; this freeze supplies no default Marks conversion. The extended holdings on protected choice, postdecision regulatory standard, stare decisis and spousal notice must separately state their proposition supporters, actual controlling status and reserved breadth. No opinion-majority count follows from the judgment table alone.

**O’Connor:** sustain the appealed emergency/information/delivery/waiting/parental/reporting/public-release provisions on the precise grounds above; invalidate spousal notice and notice/nonnotice-reason reporting, preserving marital status. Independently supports the narrow spousal protected-choice, adult-family-privacy and sex-equality grounds. Retains constitutional choice, rejects trimester machinery, and selects her actual severe-burden-then-possible-compelling-justification framework with rational review below severity. She does not select meaningful-burden balancing or automatic invalidation of every substantial obstacle.

**Kennedy:** same specified provision dispositions, but not identical rationales. Retains protected choice, rejects trimester machinery and permits reasonable nonpreclusive regulation. Invalidates the actual State-enabled third-party control; does not yet select a universal undue-burden formula, automatic obstacle rule or meaningful-burden burden shift. Spousal decision autonomy is sufficient; a broader standalone informational-privacy rule and independently sufficient equal-protection holding remain expressly undecided. These are bounded doctrinal reservations, not missing judgment votes.

**No authorship or coalition assumption:** neither a joint opinion nor any author, assigned role, full-opinion joinder or opinion majority follows from these commitments. A proposed circulation must be tested proposition by proposition against the actual limits above. If a broader general formulation moves either Justice, the record must explain the new sufficient ground, the source and the reconciliation before freezing that change; a shared disposition or familiar label does not suffice.

**Pre-comparator scope-refresh reconciliation:** The validated spousal-speech addendum required express reach rows, not a change to any selected provision disposition or framework proposition. Both Justices leave the independent spousal-speech merits and preservation questions undecided because their own adopted grounds supply complete relief. Emergency-vagueness rows likewise identify nonreach of an independent merits/waiver holding. These clarifications were completed and refrozen before comparator or Stone access; no historical result supplied the choices.

**Parental-content clarification, before global freeze:** Both Justices’ parental-consent sustention treats the district court’s use of §3205 information to explain informed-parent content at744F.Supp.1382–1384 as a lower-court construction; it does not invent an express §3206 incorporation of every §3205 mechanism, a separate parent-specific24-hour waiting period, or a nondelegable physician-only parental-delivery command, and it leaves the distinct attendance findings and Third Circuit note23’s reserved interpretation exactly as identified above.

# Casey v1.2 — provisional Blackmun, Stevens, and Souter commitments

**Stage: FROZEN BEFORE HISTORICAL COMPARATOR AND BEFORE STONE TEXT.** September 15, 2026. Effective-law cutoff June 29, 1992 immediately before Casey. This is the Engine's disclosed single-conversation fallback with scoped source handoffs, not a claim of blindness. The operator who prepared these rows read the neutral record and entering public law; no historical Casey opinion, runtime comparator, Stone Section II, prior Casey record, or prior modeling was opened. The user's task description itself disclosed drafting concepts. It supplies no Justice's vote, assignment, or joinder.

The controlling neutral source is `neutral-modeling-packet.md`, frozen before modeling. Its entire statutory text, exceptions, provenance distinctions, lower-court full-read reports, and briefs/argument record are incorporated. This matrix identifies conclusions and legal reasons, not invented conference discussion or draft history. Each row independently tests two legally available paths. A candidate formulation that crosses a recorded barrier requires the Engine's reconciliation audit; it cannot be called confirmation. A newly discovered material source fact requires neutral refresh and affected refreeze before comparison.

## Sources and full-read gate

This modeler personally read Blackmun's full Webster separate opinion, Blackmun's full Ashcroft separate opinion and Ohio v. Akron dissent; Stevens's full Thornburgh concurrence, Webster separate opinion, Hodgson writing (all parts, statutory appendix and notes), Ohio v. Akron concurrence, and short Riley separate writing; and Souter's full Payne concurrence including notes. Root independently read the full Blackmun Roe and Thornburgh majority opinions including all notes; the bounded report `blackmun-roe-thornburgh-source-report.md` was read and incorporated before this freeze. The source packet's full Third Circuit opinion was read by this modeler; the full district opinion and all merits briefs and argument were read by designated source agents/root with reports in the packet. Full-read status is not inferred from a citation or extract. All opinion texts are fresh or source-validated CAP primary texts in `prior/` and `authorities/`.

Source keys below are exact writings, not whole-case labels:

- **BR**: Blackmun, Roe majority, 410 U.S. 113, especially 152–165 and paternal-right footnote; January 22, 1973. The abortion choice is protected Fourteenth Amendment liberty/privacy; distinct maternal-health/potential-life interests become compelling at different stages; postviability regulation must preserve life and health. No decision when life begins or on paternal rights.
- **BT**: Blackmun, Thornburgh majority, 476 U.S. 747, especially 759–771 and its notes; June 11, 1986. Stevens joined. Ordinary voluntary informed consent is valid; mandatory indiscriminate discouragement and irrelevant information are different. Confidential health reporting is permissible; public individual reporting that risks identifying the woman is not. The then-unresolved facility-publication and parental-operation issues are not prior holdings. The former paternal-support provision lacked today's rape omission permission. The older scheme lacked today's severability clause.
- **BW**: Blackmun, Webster separate, 492 U.S. 490, 537–560; July 3, 1989. Retains Roe's framework and viability, rejects rational-basis substitution, recognizes appropriately indicated viability examinations as consistent with Roe, and condemns medically useless/risky tests even on ordinary rationality. Reliance includes women's settled life arrangements and equal participation. Recognizes distinction between refusal of subsidy and turning essentially private care into forbidden public care.
- **BA**: Blackmun, Ashcroft separate, 462 U.S. 476, 494–505; June 15, 1983; joined by Stevens. Important health objective and no significant interference govern first-trimester ancillary regulation; unsupported $40 pathology cost matters to poor women; doctors can record reasons without a second doctor being necessary. Rejects the then-present parental/judicial-veto scheme and an invented federal saving construction of unqualified state text.
- **BO**: Blackmun, Ohio v. Akron dissent, 497 U.S. 502, 525–542; June 25, 1990. Treats Bellotti maturity/best-interest alternatives, anonymity and expedition as minimum safeguards; criticizes the concrete pleading, delay, proof and physician-notification burdens, cumulatively and separately. Earlier BA's broader disagreement is not erased; BO supplies a later route for evaluating the actual bypass instead of mechanically rejecting every parental rule.
- **ST**: Stevens, Thornburgh concurrence, 476 U.S. 747, 772–782; June 11, 1986. Childbearing decisions receive heightened protection, not ordinary economic regulation; secular potential-life interests develop during pregnancy; majority disagreement with a woman's choice cannot itself deprive her of liberty. Stare decisis is not absolute, but stability and the enduring contraception/liberty foundations matter. Harlan's historically informed judgment replaces no mechanical formula.
- **SW**: Stevens, Webster separate, 492 U.S. 490, 560–572; July 3, 1989. Viability testing can satisfy Roe if medically indicated; refuses to erase mandatory statutory language through construction; distinguishes medically useful advice from purposeless costs; rejects a theory of life that overrides individual liberty. Government need not be silent, but state-imposed coercion is different from private advice.
- **SH**: Stevens, Hodgson writing, 497 U.S. 417, especially Parts III, V–VIII and notes; June 25, 1990. State carries justification where the law places obstacles in the path of protected choice; interests and actual fit matter before the obstacle becomes an absolute denial. One-parent participation and a genuinely helpful consultation period can be reasonable; a second-parent rule with no useful fit is not cured by forcing a consenting parent and minor through court. Distinguishes adult fixed delay in Akron expressly. General childbearing discussion in Part III was not a majority holding, but is Stevens's own writing.
- **SO**: Stevens, Ohio v. Akron concurrence, 497 U.S. 502, 521–525; June 25, 1990. Joins Parts I–IV, not Part V; sustains facially adequate one-parent notice/bypass without inventing its future maladministration, requires an effective mature/best-interest exception, and permits reasonable steps at physician notice while preserving Akron's different adult counseling rule. Recognizes adjudication may change on an actual administrative record.
- **SR**: Stevens, Riley separate, 487 U.S. 781 (1988), joining Parts I–III and disagreeing only about licensing Part IV. Compelled noncommercial charitable speech cannot be put outside protection by a commercial label; insignificant licensing burden absent evidence of delay is different. This is a joined speech proposition, not an abortion speech holding.
- **SP**: Souter, Payne concurrence joined by Kennedy, 501 U.S. 808; June 27, 1991; full own writing in `prior/payne-souter.txt`. Constitutional error alone does not justify overruling; a special justification is required. Concrete unworkability/internal doctrinal tension can justify partial overruling while other categories remain undecided. The information excluded there was morally relevant, and ordinary due-process protections could police inflammatory presentation; it establishes neither categorical freedom to compel speech nor an abortion test.

Earlier **simulated public** sources, all actually read in `output/`, are mapped with exact quotations and joins in `entering_law.md`: **F** Foucha, May 18 (chunk9; Blackmun/Stevens/Souter join the liberty holding and procedural discussion; all three also join White's noncontrolling equality section); **R** Riggins, May 18 (chunk9; all three join O'Connor's State-justification/less-intrusive-treatment holding); **L** Lee, June 24 (chunk13; Blackmun authors sponsorship holding, all three join both sponsorship and Kennedy pressure grounds; Stevens joins Souter's separate historical-neutrality writing); **M** Medina, June 22 (chunk13; Blackmun/Stevens dissent, Souter joins O'Connor's practical-fairness concurrence sustaining the particular defendant preponderance burden); **C** Collins, February 26 (chunk5; Stevens authors, all three join constitutional-source limits); **H** Hilton, December 16 (chunk3; Stevens/Souter join Kennedy's preserved statutory-coverage precedent; Blackmun writes broader judgment concurrence); **Q** Quill, May 26 (chunk9; Stevens authors, Blackmun/Souter join the due-process holding and Stevens's Commerce plurality); **Mc** McCollum, June 18 (chunk12; Blackmun authors, Stevens/Souter join); **Hu** Hudson, February 25 (chunk5; Stevens and Blackmun narrower writings, Souter joins O'Connor); **W** Wright, June 19 (chunk12; Souter's threshold Teague writing alone). None supplies a previous abortion-specific commitment.

Every row below lists its own-writing key first and its pertinent public-continuity key second. A repeated key incorporates only the specific propositions stated above. It does not treat unrelated criminal procedure or religion law as controlling abortion law.

## Statutory qualifications used by every row

The following terms accompany, and are incorporated into, every provision reference in the matrices; no row relies on an unnamed exception. All are **statutory text** in the supplied statutory source unless a different source is named.

**Emergency/disclosure protection:** §3203 applies to a condition that, on the physician's good-faith clinical judgment, so complicates the woman's medical condition as to necessitate immediate abortion to avert death, or for which delay creates a serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. The **Third Circuit construction**, 947 F.2d 682's emergency discussion, covers significant health threats prospectively including onset of preeclampsia, inevitable abortion and premature ruptured membranes; it does not demand waiting for shock/infection. That is a federal construction, not a Pennsylvania Supreme Court holding. §3205(b) requires explanation of emergency indications before abortion if possible. The distinct §3205(c) affirmative defense requires the physician to prove by a preponderance a reasonable belief that furnishing subsection (a)'s information would have a severely adverse effect on the woman's physical or mental health. It does not itself waive delay. The supplied text has OCR subsection-label irregularities identified in the neutral packet.

**Information/receipt:** §3205(a)(1) medical facts are orally conveyed by the performing or referring physician at least 24 hours before abortion; (a)(2) permits the listed qualified delegate to convey notice of optional free printed material, possible prenatal/birth/neonatal assistance and paternal support. For paternal-support disclosure, **“In the case of rape, this information may be omitted.”** This is permission, without police reporting, and differs from the spousal-assault exception below. Printed material is furnished only if the woman chooses it; neither reading nor accepting it is compulsory. Written certification under (a)(4) establishes receipt of required information, not agreement. Section 3208 requires objective, nonjudgmental scientific content and lists of actual assistance/adoption agencies. District findings 181–189 concern particular directory omissions and misinformation by some listed agencies, with providers able to criticize and supplement; they do not find every state publication false.

**Parent:** §3206(a) requires, except in a medical emergency or as otherwise provided in the section, the informed consent of both the pregnant woman and one parent when she is under18 and not emancipated; for a woman adjudged incompetent it requires her guardian's informed consent. The parent or guardian must consider only the child's or ward's best interests. If the pregnancy results from incest and the father is a party to the incestuous act, the pregnant woman need obtain only her mother's consent. Under §3206(b), **if both parents have died or are otherwise unavailable to the physician within a reasonable time and in a reasonable manner**, the consent of the woman's guardian or guardians is sufficient. If her parents are divorced, consent of the parent having custody is sufficient. **If neither any parent nor a legal guardian is available to the physician within a reasonable time and in a reasonable manner**, consent of any adult person standing in loco parentis is sufficient. The minor may elect bypass without first approaching a parent: maturity/capacity and informed consent, or abortion in her best interests despite immaturity. Statutory safeguards provide counsel, confidentiality/sealed records and prompt disposition (three business days initially, five business days on appeal in the quoted operative safeguards). No current independent bypass-adequacy attack is developed. **No statute expressly commands a clinic visit by the parent:** district finding232 describes clinics' intended compliance/standard-of-care practice; Third Circuit footnote23 reserves construction and reasons bypass would cure even an assumed visit requirement. The incompetent-adult/guardian language is supplied text, without a developed independent challenge.

**Spouse:** §3209 requires a signed nonnotarized certification of notice with a false-statement warning, or an exception certification that the husband is not the father, cannot be located after diligent effort, the pregnancy resulted from spousal sexual assault under §3128 reported to an agency with jurisdiction, or the woman has reason to believe notice likely to result in bodily injury to her by her spouse or another. Medical emergency independently excuses notice. The incorporated assault offense includes force, threatening forcible compulsion that prevents resistance by a person of reasonable resolution, or intercourse with an unconscious person; the **90-day report condition is district finding297's account of incorporated §3128 law**, not a freestanding phrase in §3209. The statute has no general exception for psychological injury, economic retaliation, divorce/custody threats, exposure, or harm to children/others. Likely bodily injury to the woman is not silently expanded to all coercion.

**Reports:** §3214(a)'s individual reports are confidential and omit patient names. **District findings and Third Circuit**, not the face of (a) alone, establish adequate actual safeguards; good-cause judicial authorization governs law-enforcement disclosure, and annual statistics cannot identify physician/facility/patient. The appealed identity issue is referring physician, not performing physician. (a)(8) records basis for necessity under §3211(b)(1) to prevent death/substantial irreversible impairment; (a)(10) explains an emergency excusing statutory compliance; (a)(11) records §3210(a) diagnostic inquiries, examinations/tests, and basis for gestational-age judgment, not merely the resulting age. (a)(12) includes marital status, notice and reasons notice was not given; these are separable inquiries. Unappealed ordinary records remain outside merits reopening.

**Public facility reports:** §3207(b) makes facility affiliation/ownership reports public if state funds were received within 12 calendar months before the request to inspect or copy; otherwise access is confined to law enforcement/licensing officials for official duties. Noncompliance incurs $500 per day. §3214(f) makes quarterly abortion totals by trimester public if state funds were received in the 12 calendar months before filing that report. A no-funds representation receives confidentiality absent contrary evidence. These lookbacks are distinct. Neither provision makes individual patient reports public. The challenged public release is separate from unchallenged agency collection. Act64 §6 permits severance of words, provisions and applications when the remainder can independently operate.

## Blackmun — frozen commitments

Blackmun begins with his actual abortion opinions rather than reconstructing a hypothetical moderate center. His selected general rule remains Roe's protected choice, viability line and trimester-related scrutiny; a more protective minimum may be joined as a minimum without assent to replacing his rule. The present source corrections materially change some applications: true confidentiality, optional printed material, rape permission, a prospective emergency construction and express severability cannot be ignored. BA's broad parental objection is acknowledged, while the actual appealed increment and BO's later case-specific bypass analysis bound this adjudication.

| Component; own/public sources | Two adversarial paths and strongest counterargument | Frozen conclusion, join barrier, narrower position |
|---|---|---|
| Protected choice, viability and life/health; BR/BW; F/R | A retain fundamental previability choice and medical health protection: longstanding procreation liberty and actual bodily consequences. B return decision to States: text does not enumerate abortion and potential life deserves protection throughout. Strongest B point is constitutional interpretation's legitimacy; strongest A answer is established liberty cannot turn on majority disapproval. | Select A. Cannot join rational basis for prohibition or equal fetal/maternal constitutional priority. Can join narrower reaffirmance of choice/viability without every BR rationale; postviability life/health cannot be omitted. |
| Trimester machinery; BR/BW; Q | A preserve Roe's staged framework with medically responsive applications. B replace trimesters with a flexible burden test while retaining choice. Strongest B argument is variable medical facts; A answers BW expressly accommodates indicated testing without discarding the framework. | Select A. Refuses general abandonment of trimester scrutiny. Can join a proposition that dated week assumptions are not immutable medical facts, or a minimum protection compatible with stricter Roe treatment. |
| Substantial obstacle as independently forbidden; BW/BO; F/R | A a state-created obstacle preventing protected choice is impermissible regardless of the State's wish to discourage abortion. B exacting balancing could permit an obstacle for compelling health need. Strongest B case is medically necessary safety regulation; A separates a genuinely unsafe procedure from protected available care. | Joins A only for an obstacle to constitutionally protected care, preserving life/health and safe-practice distinctions. Cannot make substantial obstacle the exclusive threshold or equate inability to obtain unsafe care with denial of protected care. |

Cannot join ordinary adequate justification as universally sufficient or shift all uncertainty against the woman. Can join inadequate-fit invalidation as a floor. |

B disagreement, doctrinal friction and medical change justify overruling. B they chill judgment and the defense is too narrow to cure every information burden. Refuses making a preponderance defense a universal substitute for prospective health protection. Narrower: retain the protections when other information commands are severed. |

B a categorical script overrides individualized judgment even if facts often relevant. B truthful notice of a free option empowers choice and acceptance is voluntary. | Assistance notice §3205(a)(2)(ii); BT; F/R | A mandatory financial counseling to every woman irrespective of relevance is state-directed discouragement. B possible prenatal/birth/neonatal assistance may remove financial misinformation and expand actual choice. Strongest B point is genuine usefulness; strongest A response is lack of a general relevance/consent exception. | Invalidate the categorical command, while permitting requested or professionally appropriate truthful advice. Cannot claim benefits themselves illegitimate or false. Minimum revision permits patient-sensitive provision without a compulsory prerequisite to care. |
| Paternal-support notice §3205(a)(2)(iii); BT; R | A indiscriminate legal counseling remains unwarranted despite the rape permission. B support information, including abortion-payment offers, corrects a material financial misunderstanding; rape information may be omitted without reporting. Strongest B point corrects the old statute; A notes omission remains permission and severe-health defense is not general relevance discretion. | Invalidate mandatory across-the-board delivery on BT's independent intrusion ground; **not** because rape disclosure is required or conditioned on police reporting. Permits voluntary relevant advice; no change to substantive support law. |
| Printed materials §3208 and elective receipt (a)(3); BT/BW; L | A State may publish objective, nonjudgmental scientifically accurate information; woman's elective receipt imposes no categorical forced-reading burden. B directory defects and biased agencies can make this an intimidation mechanism. Strongest B point is actual findings181–189; A confines their reach and permits provider correction/supplement. | Sustain publication and optional receipt as severed from the invalid mandatory notice. Refuses a finding of universal accuracy or permission for deceptive implementation. Narrower challenges to concrete misleading publications remain. |
| Receipt certification (a)(4); BR/BT; C | A written receipt confirms valid informed consent; B certification enforces invalid components and may appear ideological assent. Strongest A point is no agreement is demanded; B succeeds only for invalid linked commands. | Sustain receipt certification limited to lawful information; enjoin certification of mandatory ancillary commands and invalid timing. Cannot require acceptance or agreement. Minimum revision tracks the severed lawful content. |
| Fixed 24-hour delay; BT/BA; R | A invalidate compulsory delay after informed readiness, given second trips, longer scheduling delays and risks/costs. B deliberation may benefit a decision with lasting consequences although not medically necessary. Strongest B point is nonmedical deliberation; A answers adult capacity and no demonstrated need for a uniform extra interval. | Invalidate fixed delay under Akron, independently of physician delivery. Cannot join an exemption limited to impending irreversible injury as full cure. Narrower voluntary reflection and medically indicated time remain lawful. |
| One-parent architecture and bypass; BA/BO; F | A BA objects to parental/judicial control of mature choice. B binding one-parent/bypass precedents plus BO's later minimum-safeguard analysis permit case-specific review of the appealed informed-parent increment. Strongest A point is court burden; strongest B is the core architecture is not newly challenged independently here. | Do not reopen unchallenged one-parent architecture or decide a general new parental theory. No separate facial bypass defect established on this record; preserve mature/best-interest, counsel, confidentiality and expedition safeguards. Refuses to describe this as abandonment of BA or an unrestricted parental veto. |
| Parent informed-consent increment/clinic attendance; BO/BA; R | A invalidate compulsory adult-parent clinic attendance because supportive parents can consent without a trip and unnecessary bypass adds burden. B statute never expressly commands attendance; fn23 reserves construction, and a functioning bypass supplies an alternative. Strongest B point bars treating a finding as text; strongest A point is clinics' documented operational requirement. | Affirm rejection of facial invalidation based on a supposed enacted clinic-visit command; no vacatur or special attendance remand. Preserve an actual future enforced-visit challenge and ordinary parental information. Cannot join fn23's universal assertion that bypass cures every added burden. The separately specified parental ancillary-content severance is a distinct partial-reversal vote. |
| Spousal notice; BR/BT/BO; F/R/L | A legal notice condition foreseeably gives coercive husbands practical control; evaluate women the law compels, with all exceptions in terms. B no formal veto, legitimate spousal participation and injury/assault safeguards reduce harm. Strongest B point is notice distinct from consent; A relies on actual coercion beyond those exceptions and underreporting. | Invalidate §3209; injunctive relief extends the notice prerequisite, with corresponding notice-report severance. Roe itself did not decide paternal rights. Refuses all-wives denominator or a claim every husband abuses. Narrower voluntary consultation remains unregulated. |
| Referring-physician identity §3214(a)(1); BT/BA; R | A actual two-doctor referral deterrence with weak health need burdens access even though confidentiality works. B referral doctors can supply clinical information and health research/enforcement can justify identities. Strongest B point distinguishes old public reports; A notes data can be sought from performing physicians without identifying every referrer. | Invalidate the referring-identity item on this record; retain performing identity and other unappealed records. Refuses invented leaks and categorical physician immunity from records. Minimum revision removes referring name while preserving relevant nonidentifying referral data. |
| Medical necessity basis §3214(a)(8); BR/BT/BA; F | A confidential reasons permit health study and review of existing lawful postviability necessity rules. B mandatory reasons chill independent medical judgment. Strongest B point is district finding; A distinguishes documentation from imposing a second approval or changing the medical rule. | Sustain with good-faith clinical judgment intact; not approval of a new substantive postviability test. BA itself recognizes recorded reasons can ensure reasoned choice. Refuses automatic professional-punishment inference. Narrower application challenge for actual interference remains. |
| Emergency reason §3214(a)(10); BT/BA; R | A confidential documentation checks genuine use of emergency exceptions without delaying care. B retrospective investigation may chill urgent judgment. Strongest B point is sanctions; A emphasizes reasonableness/good faith and no preapproval. | Sustain reasons under the prospectively protective emergency construction. Cannot join hindsight liability for an ultimately unrealized risk or reporting as a prior authorization. Narrower unlawful enforcement can be challenged. |
| Diagnostic basis §3214(a)(11); BW/BA; C | A record existing prudent clinical inquiries/tests and reasoning, without creating a new medically useless test. B comprehensive diagnostic listing intrudes more than age result and burdens professional care. Strongest B point is actual extra item; A notes existing §3210(a) prudence safeguard. | Sustain confidential record of actual diagnostic basis; do not expand into mandatory tests, and do not reopen the upheld age-result/ascertainment provisions. Would refuse wording requiring every imaginable test or findings clinically unavailable. |
| Marital/notice report §3214(a)(12); BT; C | A strike notice-compliance fields dependent on invalid §3209 while retain ordinary confidential marital-status data. B legislature designed one integrated field and marital status also stigmatizes. Strongest B point is practical linkage; A relies on explicit severability and adequate confidentiality. | Invalidate notice-given and nonnotice-reason demands; sustain severable marital-status inquiry. Refuses blanket description that every reporting item or every marital question falls. |
| Facility/affiliation public release §3207(b); BT/BW; R | A state funding for even unrelated services cannot justify exposing providers to proven harassment/access disruption without adequate fit. B taxpayers may identify funded facilities and affiliates; no patient disclosure. Strongest B point distinguishes Thornburgh individual reports; A relies on this trial's independent harassment/funding-refusal findings. | Invalidate public-release condition on this record; retain agency collection and official-duty access. Refuses a funding entitlement or claim all facility identities are private constitutional secrets. Minimum revision confines disclosure to legitimately necessary nonharassing aggregate fiscal accountability. |
| Quarterly totals public release §3214(f); BT/BW; F | A public abortion totals tied to facilities can intensify targeted harassment and discourage receipt of general-service funds. B aggregate procedure/trimester figures are useful public accountability and do not identify women. Strongest B point is aggregation; A requires actual fit and honors record of funding refusal/access harm. | Invalidate public release with its filing-date lookback; retain confidential quarterly collection and lawful nonidentifying statewide statistics. Cannot collapse into §3207's request-date lookback. |
| Independent First Amendment ground; BT; L | A compelled ideological or irrelevant presentation also burdens physician/patient expressive autonomy. B accurate clinical disclosure regulates professional practice; commercial-disclosure precedents apply. Strongest B point is ordinary informed consent; A rejects the providers' supposed concession and the commercial label as complete answer. | No need for a broader independent speech holding after due-process invalidation of compulsory ancillary/delivery commands. Preserve objection; sustain ordinary medical content without classifying all physician speech as commercial. No new constitutional right to withhold medically required facts. |
| Equality/marital/informational privacy alternatives; BR/BT/BO; F/L/Mc | A notice subordinates women's protected choice and exposes intimate information; B pregnancy and spouses' interests are distinct without a new general sex-classification rule. Strongest A point is practical subordinate status; strongest B is doctrinal scope beyond necessary relief. | Abortion liberty supplies complete relief. No independently sufficient equal-protection, general marital-privacy or informational-privacy holding, narrow or broad. Decision autonomy and coercion remain parts of abortion-liberty reasoning. No alternate-foundation remand because Roe is retained. |
| Facial scope, severance and remedy; BT/SW; C | A injunction may reach challenged commands whose record establishes constitutional defect, preserving independent valid remainder. B valid applications defeat every facial challenge under an all-applications formula. Strongest B point is prophylactic breadth; A notes BT adjudicated real record and current express severability permits precision. | Declaration/injunction provision by provision; preserve unappealed provisions and §3206 construction inquiry. No damages or unrelated drug/funding entitlement. Cannot import another Justice's universal facial formula. |

## Stevens — frozen commitments

Stevens's governing proposal protects choice and tests what a regulation actually does and whether its justification fits. SH is unusually direct evidence for a framework capable of invalidating burdens below complete obstruction and placing justification on the State. But his own writings also accept useful one-parent participation, genuine medical advice, sensible facial limits and ordinary reporting. Truthful optional information and the corrected statute require fresh applications; BT's prior disposition is not itself a present vote. He will accept restatement of doctrine that preserves its constitutional protection and reasoned scrutiny without promising every piece of prior trimester machinery is immutable.

| Component; own/public sources | Two adversarial paths and strongest counterargument | Frozen conclusion, join barrier, narrower position |
|---|---|---|
| Protected choice, viability, life/health; ST/SW/SH; F/R | A retain individual childbearing liberty, protected previability choice and medical health priority. B democratic resolution follows from the absence of express abortion text and fetal interests. Strongest B point is judicial limits; A locates abortion within established contraception/procreation liberty and varying secular interests. | Retain protection. Refuses ordinary economic rationality for prohibition or treating a conception theory as overriding the woman. Can join core holding without his distinct Establishment Clause discussion. |
| Trimester mechanics; ST/SW/SH; Q | A keep Roe's actual protection but replace mechanical staging with reasoned review sensitive to burden, medical facts and fit. B retain precise stages to prevent quiet erosion. Strongest B point is BW's warning about undefined standards; A answers a concrete burden/justification rule rather than undefined deference. | Accepts departure from rigid trimester machinery **only** with protected choice, viability, health and meaningful scrutiny preserved. Cannot join replacement merely to lower scrutiny of every lesser obstacle. Narrower application under existing law remains available. |
| Obstacle ceiling; ST/SH; F/R | A State may not prevent protected previability care even for a policy preference; B compelling health interests could warrant burdensome restrictions. Strongest B point is unsafe care; A distinguishes protection of a safe choice from entitlement to every method. | Joins an independently impermissible substantial obstacle to protected care, with legitimate safety limits. Refuses an obstacle-only test insulating other arbitrary burdens. |
| Meaningful burden, justification and fit; SH/ST; R/Hu | A State must justify an actual obstacle and connect its means to the permissible interest; lesser burdens can fail. B require extreme severity before any enhanced review to protect legislation from routine litigation. Strongest B point is administrability; A's SH examples show two-parent rule fails fit even with bypass. | Select A; State bears justification once meaningful burden shown. Can join consideration of affected women, aggregate rules and less burdensome alternatives without demanding optimal legislation. Cannot join a rule that childbirth preference alone licenses direct obstacles or that every trivial cost triggers invalidity. |
| Stare decisis and cases; ST/SW/SH; Q/H | A preserve Roe's foundation and distinguish later cases precisely while refining a workable rule. B either preserve every implementation or abandon the foundation as unprincipled. Strongest B point is stable lines; A's Q and ST permit separating doctrine without surrendering principle. | Retain foundation/viability; permits justified refinement of trimester implementation and fact-specific qualification of Akron/Thornburgh only where their protective grounds remain. Cannot announce wholesale overruling on a shared label or import Ohio parental notice as adult delay authority. |
| Emergency construction; SW/ST; R | A prospective good-faith construction protects significant threats without awaiting injury; B statutory irreversible/major-function language cannot be rewritten to cover every health concern. Strongest B point is SW fidelity to mandatory text; A notes risk-focused language is expressly prospective. | Sustain as Third Circuit construed, preserving serious health threats and onset conditions. Refuses calling the construction authoritative state law or treating every minor/transient symptom as emergency. A harmful later narrowing receives new review. |
| Emergency explanation/disclosure defense; ST/BT; R | A prior explanation if possible and adverse physical/mental-health defense protect informed choice; B a severe-adversity defense alone cannot validate irrelevant counseling or delay. Strongest A point is statutory flexibility; B correctly distinguishes the separate duties. | Sustain as protections, without treating them as cure for every other burden. Refuses conversion of preponderance defense into a presumption that all compelled information is relevant. |
| Ordinary medical information; ST/SW/BT; C | A procedure, material risks/alternatives, age and childbirth medical risks genuinely inform the woman's choice. B uniformly dictated information may displace physician judgment. Strongest B point is BT; A distinguishes concrete relevant medical facts from a view about what choice should be made. | Sustain medically accurate/relevant content, with existing disclosure-health protection; no compelled false risk or irrelevant ideology. Sever delivery and timing. |
| Physician-only delivery; SH/SO/BA; R | A adult counseling can be competently delegated, and the actual burden lacks demonstrated benefit. B a responsible physician can better answer medical questions. Strongest B point is SO physician-notice acceptance; A expressly distinguishes reasonable parental notice from Akron adult counseling. | Invalidate exclusive personal delivery; permit appropriate delegation with physician available for professional responsibility. Cannot join a general physician-only rule through the word informed consent. |
| Mandatory optional-material notice; ST/SW/BT; L | A truthful notice of free optional material leaves the choice of receipt and provider criticism intact; B compelled routing to a flawed directory is state discouragement contrary to BT. Strongest B point is findings181–189; A notes the statute demands objective accuracy and record does not condemn every material. | Sustain the notice against this facial challenge, confined to truthful availability and genuinely optional receipt. Does not sustain deceptive agency implementation. Refuses saying mandatory notice is itself optional; narrower relief could address identified false material on a properly framed implementation challenge. |
| Assistance notice; ST/SH/BT; F | A accurate information that assistance may exist can make the woman's options real; B State selects childbirth facts to discourage abortion regardless of relevance. Strongest B point is categorical delivery; A notes no compelled agreement or subsidization choice and brief accurate advice lacks shown independent material obstacle. | Sustain accurate notice with severe adverse-health defense and qualified delegation. Cannot join a rule that any truthful fact may be compelled regardless of burden; this limited financial option bears a concrete choice-related fit. |
| Paternal-support notice; ST/SW/BT; R | A truthful support information can correct financial pressure, and rape disclosure **may** be omitted without reporting. B a categorical script remains insensitive and the permission does not compel omission. Strongest B point is residual relevance; A treats discretion and health defense as real safeguards without overstating them. | Sustain this limited accurate notice facially, preserving the exact rape permission. No claim that support advice is mandatory in rape or that omission requires police contact. Refuses broader compelled advocacy or a change in support obligations. |
| Section3208 publication/elective receipt; SW/ST; L | A government may offer objective scientific and assistance information which a woman is free to decline. B misleading material defeats informed autonomy. Strongest B point is listed agencies' actual conduct; A distinguishes publication command from every agency's later speech. | Sustain statutory publication and optional receipt; inaccurate/nonobjective implementation is not immunized. No forced reading or endorsement. |
| Receipt certification; ST/BT; C | A certifying receipt of lawful information is modest evidence of informed consent. B it reinforces invalid physician/delay conditions and could be mistaken for compelled agreement. Strongest A point is exact statutory receipt text; B calls for severance of invalid links. | Sustain limited to valid information actually received; remove certification of invalid timing/delivery. Refuses required ideological agreement or acceptance of optional packet. |
| Fixed24-hour delay; SH explicit adult/minor distinction; R | A adult who has been informed may choose immediately; separate-trip delay adds costs/risks without a demonstrated benefit to her. B general reflection rationally protects a consequential decision. Strongest B point is SH accepted48hours for minors; A notes protected parental consultation and no delay after consent there. | Invalidate fixed adult delay under Akron and fit analysis. Voluntary reflection and individualized clinical scheduling remain. Cannot generalize SH's minor consultation to competent adult incapacity. |
| Core one-parent/bypass rule; SH/SO; C | A sensible one-parent rule with mature/best-interest alternative supports the minor's welfare. B BA/Bellotti objections show court/parent process can override mature choice. Strongest B point is actual family/court burdens; A's later own writings expressly accept the properly safeguarded general rule. | Sustain the challenged informed-parent rule's core, with statutory alternatives and actual confidentiality/expedition. No generalized parental veto. Keep independently unchallenged architecture outside reopening and preserve application challenges. |
| Parental clinic attendance; SH/SO; R | A a consenting parent and minor cannot be forced into an unnecessary trip or bypass merely because the State prefers clinic presence. B no statutory visit language; providers' anticipated standard of care does not establish a facial command, and bypass may solve absence. Strongest B point is fn23's reserved construction; A rejects bypass as universal cure. | Affirm §3206 facial disposition without an invented personal-clinic-attendance command; no vacatur or special attendance remand. Preserve a properly presented actual enforced-visit challenge. Cannot join a proposition that every parental trip is unreasonable, or that unnecessary court proceedings automatically cure one. |
| Spousal notice; SH/ST; F/R/L | A compelled family communication that predictably causes coercion is not justified by an ideal-family model. B spouses have legitimate shared interests, no formal veto, and express injury/assault/other exceptions. Strongest B point is meaningful husband interest; A's SH distinguishes desirable communication from State decree and evaluates affected women. | Invalidate notice with all statutory limits accounted for. Refuses the denominator of women already notifying voluntarily and a categorical assertion notice equals formal consent. Voluntary consultation remains desirable and constitutionally unobstructed. |
| Referring identity (a)(1); BT/BA/SH; R | A proved withdrawal of referrers adds access burden without demonstrated need for their names. B confidential follow-up may need an identifiable referring doctor. Strongest B point is new confidentiality; A distinguishes collection of useful clinical facts from indiscriminate identities. | Invalidate referring-name demand, retain performing identity and ordinary records. Cannot rest on imagined patient leaks. A targeted demonstrated medical follow-up requirement would be narrower. |
| Necessity basis (a)(8); SW/BA/BT; C | A confidential documentation can verify compliance with unchanged medical law; B compelled explanations chill lawful judgment. Strongest B point is district concern; A notes BA recognized reason records and no new approval or test. | Sustain reasons with medical judgment and health priority intact. No approval of unrelated postviability restrictions or of retrospective punishment merely because an assessed risk did not materialize. |
| Emergency basis (a)(10); SW/BT; R | A recording a good-faith emergency rationale fits compliance and research; B health care may be chilled by expected prosecutorial second-guessing. Strongest B point is professional risk; A keeps explanation after treatment distinct from permission beforehand. | Sustain under prospective emergency construction, without preapproval or hindsight rule. Narrower abusive application remains open. |
| Diagnostic basis (a)(11); SW/BA; C | A document the prudent clinical basis already required, preserving health study; B listing examinations/inquiries can become a demand for medically unnecessary tests. Strongest B point is SW's rejection of mandatory useless testing; A confines the item to actual appropriate inquiry. | Sustain records of existing prudent assessment; refuse adding tests or substituting checklist completeness for clinical judgment. Age result and ascertainment remain unappealed, distinct matters. |
| Marital/notice fields (a)(12); ST/BT; C | A remove the invalid notice machinery while preserving ordinary confidential demographic data; B all fields are colored by the invalid spouse rule. Strongest B point is statutory linkage; A uses broad severability and separates functions. | Strike notice-given/nonnotice-reason fields, preserve marital status. No assumption all information about marriage is unconstitutional. |
| Facility public report §3207(b); SW/BT; C/Q | A taxpayer knowledge of publicly supported facilities/affiliations has a concrete accountability fit, no patient identity disclosure. B even unrelated state funds trigger publicity that findings link to harassment and rejected funding. Strongest B point is actual effects, not hypothetical privacy; A distinguishes publication from conditioning the woman's right or prohibiting private care. | Sustain facially on this record's limited incremental-disclosure proof and the public-funds condition, while accepting harassment findings. Does not declare every funding condition valid. Narrower particular disclosure/access proof remains available; retain official-duty-only access for no-funds facilities. |
| Quarterly public totals §3214(f); SW/BT; Q | A aggregate funded-facility abortion/trimester counts reasonably describe services supported by public resources; B targeted counts may intensify harassment and distort funding choices. Strongest B point is breadth beyond earmarked abortion funding; A notes aggregate nonpatient data and absence of a demonstrated categorical care barrier. | Sustain facial public release with filing-date12month limit, preserving confidentiality for no-funds reports absent contrary evidence. Cannot claim findings found no harassment. |
| Independent speech ground; SR/ST/BT; L | A compelled ideological scripts receive meaningful scrutiny even in professional settings; B accurate facts within informed medical choice may be required. Strongest A point is Riley's rejection of automatic commercial categorization; strongest B is patient health autonomy. | Reject categorical commercial-speech shortcut but do not independently invalidate upheld narrow truthful disclosures. Due process disposes of physician monopoly/delay; no invented abandonment of Riley or blanket right to withhold relevant medical facts. |
| Equality/private-family alternatives; SH/ST; F/L/Mc | A notice impermissibly standardizes marriage and subordinate decisionmaking; B pregnancy/marriage differences complicate a general sex-classification holding. Strongest A point is actual family coercion; strongest B is unnecessary doctrinal breadth. | Abortion liberty supplies complete relief; private-family autonomy informs that application. No independently sufficient equality, general marital-privacy or informational-privacy holding, narrow or broad. Retaining Roe makes conditional alternative-foundation remand unnecessary. |
| Facial relief/severability; SO/SW/SH; C | A invalidate proven improper commands while sustaining separable constitutionally useful parts. B occasional valid operation saves everything or any bad application invalidates everything. Strongest point of each extreme is predictable administration; A uses actual record and statute-specific fit. | Separate injunctions and preserved valid provisions; unresolved parental visit construction is not invented as text. No universal no-set formula or indiscriminate abortion overbreadth announced. |

## Souter — frozen commitments and bounded uncertainty

Souter has **no date-eligible abortion writing**. No row claims otherwise. His own SP writing and his actual prior-term public joins provide method, while the abortion precedents supply entering law. SP demands special justification, recognizes partial doctrinal correction, and tests the actual defect and retained safeguards; F/R/L show sensitivity to actual coercion and burdens, while M/W/C caution against turning that sensitivity into a universal burden allocation or substantive test. The unresolved question is whether he would personally replace Roe's doctrinal machinery with a comprehensive abortion formula. The bounded conclusion here is **retain the constitutional protection and existing precedents as necessary, decide these provisions on their proved operation, and decline to endorse either a universal substantial-obstacle-only threshold or a new universal two-tier balancing framework before the actual proposed rationale is tested**. This is a frozen refusal to supply a missing abortion philosophy, not an uncast vote on the components below. Any later comprehensive joinder requires an explicit reconciliation explaining why it is compatible with, or changes, this boundary.

| Component; own/public sources | Two adversarial paths and strongest counterargument | Frozen conclusion, join barrier, narrower position |
|---|---|---|
| Constitutional protection/viability/health; SP; F/R/H/Q | A retain settled abortion liberty and viability absent special justification; B conclude Roe erroneous and return broad power to States. Strongest B point is constitutional error cannot be fixed by legislation; A uses SP's express rule that error alone is insufficient and existing procreation foundations. | Retain previability choice and postviability life/health protection. Uncertainty concerns original justification and exact framework, not this selected result. Cannot join overruling based solely on disagreement or institutional turnover. |
| Trimester framework; SP; Q/W/C | A preserve existing staging until a concrete defect justifies a narrower alteration; B medical variability and later doctrine warrant replacing machinery while retaining the right. Strongest B point is potentially more coherent doctrine; A asks for SP-type demonstrated unworkability and specific preserved protection. | No independent commitment to categorical abandonment or immutable retention of every trimester implementation. Applies entering precedents to this case. Cannot join a replacement solely because other Justices use its label; a specific justified refinement is a potential reconciliation question, not preauthorization. |
| Obstacle independently impermissible; SP; F/R/L | A actual State-created obstruction of protected previability choice cannot be justified by wishing for childbirth. B even significant restriction may advance permissible health interests. Strongest B case is genuinely unsafe care; A keeps protected-care qualification and legitimate safety regulation separate. | Can join this minimum prohibition with health/safety boundaries. Cannot join a rule making the obstacle threshold the exclusive safeguard or assuming every increased cost is independently prohibited. |
| Smaller burdens/State justification; SP; F/R/M | A meaningful burdens require reasoned justification and fit under existing protected-liberty doctrine. B below severe obstruction ordinary rationality governs all abortion rules. Strongest A evidence is R's burden and alternatives; strongest B response is M's refusal of universal procedural burden allocation and absence of abortion writings. | Rejects an automatic safe harbor for every less-than-severe burden; requires justification where existing abortion law does. Does **not yet endorse** a universal new burden-shifting test covering every regulation. Can join particular inadequate-fit judgments without joining a new general formula. |
| Stare decisis/precedent qualifications; SP; H/Q/W | A uphold Roe's foundation; distinguish materially different statutory facts and retain separable precedent. B partial precedents are incoherent, so wholesale overruling or rigid full retention is required. Strongest B point is unstable doctrine; A uses SP's deliberate partial-overrule practice. | Retain Roe and actual Akron/Thornburgh rules absent shown special justification. Permits distinctions for genuinely optional/accurate information, confidentiality and one-parent safeguards. No automatic importing of historical postdivergence precedents; no prediction that he will join an unknown new formulation. |
| Emergency saving construction; SP; R/C | A give operative force to prospective good-faith medical risk language, as Third Circuit does. B irreversible/major-function terms may exclude constitutionally important health needs and nonbinding construction risks chilling physicians. Strongest B point is text; A notes risk language protects before harm and onset conditions are included. | Sustain on this construction against facial challenge; no universal rule that only completed injury qualifies. Future contrary state construction remains open. Refuses rewriting or claims Pennsylvania's highest court adopted the construction. |
| Emergency explanation/adverse-health defense; SP; R/M | A explanation if possible plus protection for reasonably believed severe physical/mental effects is administrable and patient-directed. B after-the-fact preponderance defense burdens health judgment. Strongest B point is uncertainty; A differentiates liability protection from a prerequisite to medical care and M's case-specific burden analysis. | Sustain these protections, without allowing them to validate every other restriction or excuse delay merely because information may be omitted. No universal endorsement of placing health-risk proof on patients or physicians. |
| Ordinary medical facts; SP; R/C | A true procedure/risk/alternative/gestational and childbirth-risk information is directly relevant to informed choice. B any mandatory script may intrude on professional judgment. Strongest B is Thornburgh's relevance concern; A notes current narrowed clinical content and protective discretion. | Sustain content separately, limited to truthful medically relevant facts and operative health protection. Does not classify all medicine as unprotected commercial speech. |
| Physician-only delivery; SP; R/Q | A follow Akron's adult-counseling rule where qualified counseling is established and doctor-only costs add no shown benefit. B responsibility of licensed physician is rational and Ohio parental notice survived. Strongest B point is professional accountability; A distinguishes the parental function and existing direct precedent. | Invalidate delivery monopoly, retain responsibility/appropriate delegation. Uncertainty about a comprehensive framework supplies no ground to discard directly applicable precedent. |
| Optional-material notice; SP; C/L | A truthful notice of an optional information source assists choice without requiring assent or reading. B mandatory routing to biased or deficient agencies reproduces Thornburgh discouragement. Strongest B point is findings181–189; A notes optional receipt, statutory objectivity and narrower proven defects. | Sustain facially with actual voluntary receipt and truthful availability; not a finding all agency advice is accurate. Narrower concrete deceptive implementation remains open. Cannot defend compelled ideology on SP's relevance analogy. |
| Assistance notice; SP; F/R | A actual knowledge of available prenatal/birth/neonatal help can improve informed options. B mandatory provision can be paternalistic and unrelated to a particular woman's needs. Strongest B point is categorical command; A sees a brief accurate option notice with little independently demonstrated burden and permitted adverse-health omission. | Sustain limited truthful notice, not unlimited State advocacy. Refuses a rule that any accurate speech is automatically compulsory or that all monetary circumstances are irrelevant to autonomy. |
| Paternal-support notice; SP; F/R | A accurate legal support information may counter financial coercion, with rape disclosure permitted to be omitted without police reporting. B insensitive delivery persists because omission is permissive and severe-health defense is narrow. No endorsement of mandatory rape disclosure or police condition. A proven misleading/coercive implementation would raise a separate question. |
| Publication/elective receipt §3208/(a)(3); SP; C/L | A State supplies objective scientific and service information the woman may reject. B the directory's defects threaten voluntary informed choice. Strongest B point is actual listed-agency misinformation; A distinguishes statutory requirements from unlawful execution. | Sustain publication and elective receipt, without declaring every material accurate or immunizing implementation. Refuses mandatory acceptance/reading/endorsement. |
| Receipt certification; SP; M/C | A proof of receipt is a modest procedural assurance; B invalid delivery/timing terms infect the certification. Strongest B identifies dependency; A separates receipt from agreement and severable commands. | Sustain certification of lawful information only; remove invalid timing/delivery implications. No independent ideological-assent requirement. |
| Fixed24-hour delay; SP; R/Q | A Akron directly rejects compelled adult delay after informed decision; actual two-trip and longer scheduling burden lacks new justification overcoming that precedent. B reflection serves a nonmedical legitimate interest. Strongest B point is real decisional gravity; A notes that was already present in Akron and no new special justification is demonstrated. | Invalidate fixed delay, preserving voluntary reflection/medical scheduling. This is a bounded precedent application, not inferred adoption of someone else's new balancing standard. |
| Core parental/bypass rule; SP; M/C | A settled one-parent architecture with elective maturity/best-interest bypass, counsel, confidentiality and speed serves legitimate minor welfare. B court procedures may burden mature minors and supportive families. Strongest B point is practical fairness; A notes no current independent bypass defect or systemic record. | Sustain core informed-parent scheme with safeguards; do not reopen unappealed independent architecture or decide every possible bypass defect. No general parental veto or automatic acceptance of any court procedure. |
| Clinic attendance/parental increment; SP; R/M | A clinic-only appearance despite existing parental consent is unnecessary intrusion, and bypass may duplicate burdens. B statute does not say visit; district describes providers' intended practice, while fn23 reserves construction. Strongest B point is avoiding invented statutory commands; A focuses on any actual enforced barrier. | Affirm the §3206 facial disposition without an assumed enacted attendance command; no vacatur or special attendance remand. Preserve a properly presented actual enforced-visit challenge. Cannot join an assertion that bypass automatically cures any needless visit. |
| Spousal notice; SP; F/R/L | A in the group legally required to disclose, actual coercion and harm not covered by exceptions can prevent protected choice. B husband's legitimate participation, no formal veto and explicit exceptions support rational notice. Strongest B point is difference from consent; A refuses to count unaffected voluntary disclosure as validating imposed coercion. | Invalidate §3209 based on protected choice and record, with all exceptions expressly evaluated. Argument questions reinforce issue awareness, not a prior vote. No general equality theory or presumption all marriages coercive. |
| Referring identity (a)(1); SP; M/C | A legitimate clinical follow-up and enforcement may identify an actual medical referrer under adequate confidentiality. B two physicians would stop referring and their names may be medically unnecessary. Strongest B point is actual deterrence; A distinguishes subjective refusal from demonstrated service obstruction or exposure and performing/referring clinical roles. | Sustain facially, acknowledging referral findings and uncertainty of actual access impact. No alleged leak or premise that all reporting burdens are immaterial. Narrower proven arbitrary collection or access obstruction remains open. |
| Medical-necessity basis (a)(8); SP; C/R | A confidential documentation fits review of existing medical-law predicates. B anticipated investigation chills judgment. Strongest B is serious clinical stakes; A preserves good faith, no preapproval and distinguishes reasons from new treatment restrictions. | Sustain existing-law basis report; no ratification of an unchallenged new viability rule. A hindsight/nonmedical enforcement demand is outside the rationale. |
| Emergency explanation report (a)(10); SP; R/M | A documentation of an emergency supports lawful oversight; B retrospective litigation risk may make urgent treatment less available. Strongest B point is time-sensitive harm; A keeps treatment priority and prospectively reasonable judgment. | Sustain under protected emergency construction, not prior authorization or proof of actual injury. No universal government entitlement to override clinical risk assessment. |
| Diagnostic basis (a)(11); SP; C | A record actual prudent diagnostic steps, adding useful data without requiring new tests. B the field could become a medically irrelevant checklist and burden access. Strongest B point is difference between basis and age result; A refuses that expansion as the text is applied to actual judgment. | Sustain confidential actual-basis reporting; leave age result and underlying ascertainment outside reopening. No compelled useless procedures. |
| Marital/notice report (a)(12); SP; Q/C | A sever invalid notice compliance from lawful confidential demographic inquiry. B integrated field should fall whole. Strongest B point is legislative design; A invokes explicit severability and independent marital-data function. | Strike notice/nonnotice-reason fields, preserve marital status. Cannot describe all marital-status records as notice enforcement. |

B record predicts harassment and funding withdrawal, including where funds support other care. Strongest B point is genuine collateral burden; A distinguishes public institutional data and limited proven incremental barrier from individual compelled disclosure. | Sustain facially with request-date12month condition and no-funds official-duty limits. Acknowledge uncertainty about particular releases' effects; no categorical public-funding waiver rule. Particular materially obstructive disclosure remains open. |
| Quarterly totals release §3214(f); SP; C/Q | A funded-facility aggregate totals/trimester data inform accountability without patient identity. B targeted statistics may aggravate harassment and indirectly reduce services. Strongest B point is funding not necessarily paying abortions; A notes no demonstrated facially categorical obstruction and public-resource nexus. | Sustain facial release with filing-date12month condition, preserving no-funds confidentiality absent contrary evidence. Does not reject trial findings or conflate agency collection with publication. |

B relevant true clinical/option disclosures can govern informed practice. Unresolved larger professional-speech standard is expressly left open. |

Conditional alternative-ground remand is unnecessary because Roe remains. |
| Facial scope, relief, severability; SP; W/Q/C | A decide proved legal defects with severed remedies, leaving unchallenged matters and genuinely unresolved state construction alone. B universal all-applications requirement bars relief or broad overbreadth invalidates entire Act.
