### Preserved audit material — dependency final

# Casey v1.2 — final chronological dependency review

## Result and scope

No later committed event is materially affected by the assembled Casey v1.2 public law. No separate-correction blocker is identified. Insert the correction at June 29, 1992 in the established case-list order **Casey, Lucas, Espinosa**. Benten follows on July 17, 1992. The chronology cursor remains **July 17, 1992, after Benten**. No later record, Render Input or output has been modified.

This conclusion closes the earlier full-record review in `later-event-dependency-preflight.md`. That review read the complete Lucas, Espinosa and Benten records, including their audit annexes, rather than inferring dependency from case names. The final comparison used the current public drafts `extended-drafts-root.json`, `standard-draft-root.json`, `holdings-health-parent-spouse.json`, `holdings-speech-cert-root.json`, `holdings-delivery-public.json`, `holdings-reports-root.json`, `separate-stone.json`, `separate-bss.json`, and the public parts, judgments and separate writings in `decision-data.json`. The last minimal common-ground wording corrections are recorded in `final-common-wording-fixes.json`. Those corrections do not change any judgment, constitutional proposition, remedy or dependency conclusion.

The auditor remains unexposed to the private Stone Casey supplement and the earlier simulated Casey record. The public Stone summary was supplied solely after assembly for this dependency and joinder review. This is the disclosed single-conversation procedure, not a representation of true blindness. Casey's in-world research cutoff remains June 29, 1992; the subsequent committed records are read only to review chronological consequences, not to supply June 29 positions or authorities.

## Effective Casey predicates tested

The public decision preserves protected previability choice, the viability boundary and necessary life-and-health care. It removes the trimester timetable as an independent timing barrier to health and potential-life regulation, but does not install either Stone's complete six-step approach or a general substantial-obstacle formulation as a comprehensive majority test. Its delivery and waiting permissions are record-specific; its content, speech, parental and reporting holdings retain their distinct statutory predicates and remedies. Spousal notice and notice-compliance/reason reports are invalidated; independent confidential marital-status data survive. The decision creates no constitutional entitlement to admission or approval of a particular drug, no general immunity from medical law, no universal proof or facial-challenge formula, and no automatic substitution of a judicial outcome for an agency's remaining discretion. Stone's proposed meaningful-burden and facial-remedy formulations, and the separate Justices' alternatives, do not become general governing law through a judgment count.

## Lucas — June 29, 1992, after Casey

Record reviewed in full: `terms/OT1991/records/Lucas_v_South_Carolina_Coastal_Council_merits_1992-06-29.md`.

Lucas reverses and remands 6–3. Its five-Justice opinion states a takings rule for a demonstrated deprivation of all economically beneficial use, subject to background property and nuisance limits already inhering in the owner's title. Kennedy concurs in the judgment on his distinct account. Blackmun and Stevens dissent; Souter would dismiss as improvidently granted. The original absolute construction prohibition remains reviewable for its actual period despite a later avenue for a discretionary permit. The record does not finally award the trial court's damages amount, decide a new parcel or percentage rule, or replace the treatment of partial losses.

The actual dependencies are the property and compensation precedents and this term's relevant Yee and Lujan positions, together with the preserved finality and remedial issues. The record expressly identifies the same-day independence; it does not rely on any Casey proposition. Casey's removal of a pregnancy timetable does not alter Lucas's background-title inquiry. Its competing burden formulations do not change Lucas's necessary showing of total deprivation, its ordinary adjudication of the State's antecedent property-law defense, or its remand. Shared references to governmental justification or burden are not a legal dependency. **Result: no material effect on Lucas's judgment, controlling law, separate positions, remedy or publication.**

## Espinosa — June 29, 1992, after Lucas

Record reviewed in full: `terms/OT1991/records/Espinosa_v_Florida_summary_merits_1992-06-29.md`.

Espinosa summarily reverses and remands the sentencing judgment 7–2. Its holding concerns a constitutionally vague heinous-atrocious-or-cruel aggravator submitted to a jury whose general 11–1 recommendation receives great weight in Florida's weighing system. The sentencing judge's separate four-aggravator and two-mitigator findings do not by themselves cure the invalid route through that recommendation. A lawful reweighing or the applicable harmless-error inquiry must address the recommendation's influence; otherwise lawful new sentencing is required. The judgment does not order automatic life imprisonment, retry guilt, or establish a new collateral-review rule. White would set the matter for argument rather than decide the present merits; Scalia would deny and disagrees with the treatment of the selection/weighing distinction.

The operative authorities are Stringer, Sochor, Chapman, Maynard and the capital-sentencing precedents identified in that record. The record expressly treats the same-day cases as independent and contains no Casey premise. Casey's qualified scope of previability review supplies no new Eighth Amendment aggravator, harmless-error standard, capital remedy or vote. Its lack of a universal facial formula also leaves Espinosa's particular adjudicated sentencing defect untouched. **Result: no material effect on Espinosa's judgment, controlling law, separate positions, remedy or publication.**

## Benten — July 17, 1992

Record reviewed in full: `terms/OT1991/records/Benten_v_Kessler_A-40_application_to_vacate_stay_1992-07-17.md`.

### Presented dispute and controlling remedy

Benten is application A-40 to vacate the Second Circuit's July 15 stay of the District Court's preliminary order returning imported RU-486 for domestic use. The record fixes July 18 as the treatment deadline. Under the operative October 23 circuit allotment Scalia is the Second Circuit Justice; the referred full Court denies the application 7–2. Stone, White, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas join the remedy-based per curiam disposition. Blackmun would grant without a published explanation; Stevens publishes his individual liberty-and-method dissent.

The majority assumes a strong procedural challenge for purposes of the demanding appellate-stay review under Western Airlines and Paccar. The personal-use policy permits admission; it does not command it. A defect in the automatic-detention procedure can restore an agency decision without proving entitlement to a mandatory judicial return order. Ford Motor's remedial flexibility and APA §706(1) do not automatically select that remaining discretionary outcome. Rutherford leaves the statutory approval framework operative. The majority accepts urgent serious injury and the individual medical findings; it does not invent a danger, an available foreign physician or trial placement, or a later actual treatment outcome.

Stone's separate assessment that the 1989 version of §10.40(d) supplied an enforceable additional procedural undertaking remains a concurrence. Accardi and Nixon support obeying binding agency rules; Vermont Yankee does not erase rules an agency actually adopted. The different 1991 text does not answer the 1989 challenge. The majority does not newly adopt that procedural merits assessment. McCarthy's claim-specific exhaustion analysis and Franklin's completed-agency-action rule remain in their existing scope. Neither supplies entitlement to the agency's ultimate discretionary result.

### Constitutional presentation and the Casey correction

The Benten record expressly states that the constitutional claim discussed by Stevens was neither addressed below nor presented in the applicants' filings in the Supreme Court. It reserves that claim and the right to a particular method. Its limited source receipt is preserved: separate applicant filings and the appellate stay's reasons were not independently obtained in that run; no new filing, concession or appellate rationale is invented here.

Casey v1.2 does not create a constitutional claim Benten's parties did not present. It does not convert the procedural challenge into an adjudicated reproductive-liberty claim, displace Benten's agency-law questions, or establish entitlement to admission of the drug. The core holding expressly withholds a drug-admission entitlement; the comprehensive standards remain divided. Even a different future claim under those protections would have to be presented and decided on its own record and remedial basis. The existing Benten denial leaves the appellate stay effective, the return order unreinstated, and proper agency-action and appellate channels intact. No new Supreme Court merits proceeding or drug approval follows.

### Stevens's separate position

The continuity check concerns Stevens's actual public reasoning, not merely the majority result. In the new Casey public writing he treats actual interference, medical usefulness, adequate justification and fit as important. His Thornburgh concurrence, Webster opinion and Hodgson position support the first five substantive steps of Stone's proposal, while he declines the general sixth-step facial formula. He preserves stronger staged protection when his proposed replacement has not become controlling, invalidates the fixed adult delay and exclusive physician delivery, and sustains other particular requirements according to their distinct information or reporting purposes and evidence.

In Benten his published dissent would grant because the asserted federal interest does not justify the burden on the individual's protected choice and noninvasive method on that record; the absence of a demonstrated significant health risk under the personal-use policy is central. That individual justification inquiry is consistent with his Casey position. His sustention of particular accurate notices or funded-institution disclosures in Casey does not approve every interference with a chosen treatment. Conversely, his Benten dissent does not claim that Casey already granted a majority constitutional entitlement to import RU-486. It remains his dissenting application to the individual circumstances, and cannot present the constitutional claim for the parties or change the majority's reservation. Blackmun's unexplained Benten grant vote supplies no additional published constitutional ground to reconcile or invent.

**Result: no material effect on Benten's action, majority rationale, Stone's distinct procedural concurrence, Stevens's individual dissent, preserved questions, remedy or publication.** Its reference to Casey's precise effective law continues to function as a reservation after the correction; it does not incorporate a superseded Casey proposition as a necessary merits premise.
